# The staff

This chapter holds what a side's commander says as state, the commands that change it, the staff that a side may have, the reasons the staff gives, and days of supply. It is written by step 1 of stage 1 of step 3: the engine's half (command state, commands, the staff passed in, days of supply) and the staff's half (the crate `sim-staff`, the doctrine for keeping a base supplied, the reasons of staff commands, the staff's projection of days of supply, and the tools a seat commands with), step 2 adds priority between intents, commitments of stock and displaced demand, and step 3 adds holds, the override of a hold by the commander's own order, ending a run, the supply a free ship has aboard, the fallback of a cancelled plan, previews and the tools `hold`, `release` and `preview`, and step 4 adds interrupts and their acknowledgement, the staff's notices, a run of days that stops for an interrupt, the command report and the tools `set_interrupt`, `acknowledge`, `run` and `report`. Step 2 of stage 2 adds the intent tree (an intent has a parent, a maker and an addressee, and the staff forms sub-intents), the end "hold a place", the task "cover a base from the air" with the air doctrine that serves them, air groups in a forces limit, a hold on a whole air group, limits read up the tree and the measure of a hold intent. Step 3 of stage 2 adds the level a doctrine belongs to, the end "neutralise a base" with its doctrine at the area's level, the task "raid a base" with its doctrine at the base's level, and the cover's give of a raid's bomber and its escorts. Step 4 adds the end "deny an area" with its doctrine at the area's level, the tasks "strike ships from a base" and "search waters from a base" with their doctrines at the base's level, the size of a strike decided at the group's level, and the cover's arc over the waters to deny. The second and third parts of step 4 add the task force as a level that an intent is addressed to, the task "stand at a hex" with its doctrine at the task force's level and the rule of the engine it needs (the night stand, chapter 2), and the task "operate a carrier force" with its doctrine. Every side of every game has the staff; a side with no intent in force has a staff that does nothing, so a game with no command is played exactly as it was before this chapter. Step 8 of stage 3 adds the island, a scenario's own data, and the end "control an island", from which the staff draws a tree of the ends the earlier steps built, with its measure (Controlling an island, below). Stage 3 of the submarine rules (decision 0218) adds the end "patrol the waters with submarines" with its doctrine at the area's level, a hold on a boat, boats in a forces limit and the staff's regard for the waters a side suspects enemy submarines work in (Patrolling waters with submarines, below). [0107, 0111, 0115, 0116, 0117, 0120, 0123, 0124, 0125, 0151, 0218]

No rule here draws a random number. Every quantity is a fixed-point number (chapter 1, Numbers and chance).

## What is built and what is not

Built: command state (intents with their parent, maker and addressee, six limits, two leaves, plans with their states, baselines, amendments and runs, commitments of ships, holds, and interrupts); seventeen commands and who may give each; the staff passed in to the function that resolves a day, and what it hands in; the check of a staff's orders against the holds and against a forces limit; reasons; days of supply; the saved game of version 3, `replay --check-staff` and the doctrine version; a scenario's commands; the doctrine for keeping a base supplied, with its projection of days of supply and its proposals; priority between intents, what is committed the same morning, the protected limit, commitments of stock as runs and displaced demand (step 2); holds on a ship, a task force and the search arc of an air group, the override of a hold by the commander's own order (step 2 of the day), ending a run, the runs the staff sets aside for a held ship, the supply aboard a free ship counted toward a run's load, the fallback of a cancelled plan, and where a run lands in the projection (step 3); the tools of steps 1 and 3, with previews; and the interrupts of the commander, with the standing ones and their acknowledgement, tested each morning, the staff's notices, and the tools of step 4 (`set_interrupt`, `acknowledge`, `run` and `report`); and, from step 2 of stage 2, the intent tree, limits read up it, air groups in a forces limit, a hold on a whole air group, the end "hold a place" with its measure, the doctrine for covering a base from the air, and the chain of a reason; and, from step 3 of stage 2, the level of a reason, the end "neutralise a base" with the raid tasks it forms, and the raid doctrine; and, from step 4, the end "deny an area" with its measure, the strike-ships and search-waters tasks and their doctrines, and the level of a group in a reason; and, from the second and third parts of step 4, a task force as an addressee, the task "stand at a hex" with its doctrine and the night stand, and the task "operate a carrier force" with its doctrine; and, from step 6 of stage 2, ammunition as policy (a force short of ammunition does not sortie and is not a free escort); and, from step 2 of stage 3, the tasks "ready a landing" and "build up a base", the first doctrines that order land units, with land units in a forces limit and a land unit as a hold's target; and, from steps 3 and 4 of stage 3, the end "take a place", with the judgement of each morning ashore and a second lift, the check that a place can be held, a hold stated of a place not yet the side's, the men of a hold and the supply that follows it; and, from step 8 of stage 3, the island as map data, the end "control an island" with the tree the staff draws from it, a plan of their own for a take and a supply intent formed under it, the staff's withdrawing of what it formed, and the island's measure; and, from stage 3 of the submarine rules, the patrol order with its stations, a hold on a boat, a boat in a forces limit and the regard for submarine waters (decision 0218). [0107, rulings 10 to 16, 17 to 30 and 31 to 37; 0115, 0116, 0117, 0120, 0123, 0124, 0125, 0127, 0141, 0151, 0218]

Not built, and so not rules: a hold on a base (it waits for a stage whose doctrine acts on a base); the withdrawal of a numbered interrupt and any condition but a base's days of supply; a notice the staff hands in on a day it weighs no new run (Notices, below); escort and the recall of a run; a second source; an intent for fuel; and a phase of a plan (below, Plans). Escort and the bombers of a strike intent, a land formation as the addressee of an intent, a commander stating a sub-intent under a parent of his choosing, the staff withdrawing a sub-intent, are not built either (Not built, below). [0107, rulings 17, 30 and 35; 0115, 0117]

## Command state

Each side has one command state, held by the world beside its belief and hashed after the beliefs, Allied side first (chapter 1, The state hash). It holds the side's intents with their limits, its two leaves, its plans, its commitments, its holds and its interrupts. It changes only by a command, by the test of its interrupts each morning (Interrupts, below), or by the override of a hold, which an order of the commander's makes (Holds and the override, below). The enemy has none of it, and a side is never told whether the other has a staff. [0107 items 1 and 6]

A side sees: its own command state, whole, through its view. It sees none of the other side's. [0107 item 6]

### Intents

An intent is what the commander wants. Twelve kinds are built. Three name a base of the side, the two of decision 0120 name an enemy base as their target (Neutralising a base and Raiding a base, below), and the three of decision 0123 are Denying an area, Striking ships from a base and Searching waters (below). **Keep a base supplied** names a base, a floor in days of supply (at least 1) and, optionally, an interval in days (at least 1) between shipments. The floor is the days of supply the base's hex is never to fall below. The interval is the days of use each run is to bring above the floor. Where the commander gives none, the interval is not set and the staff proposes one. **Hold a place** and **cover a base from the air** name a base and nothing else (Holding a place and Covering a base from the air, below). **Neutralise a base** names a target, a base on the side's map that is not the side's, and no base of the side's own. **Raid a base** names a base of the side and a target (Neutralising a base and Raiding a base, below). **Deny an area** (code 6) names a hex and a distance in whole hexes and no base; **strike ships from a base** (code 7) and **search waters from a base** (code 8) name a base of the side (Denying an area, Striking ships from a base and Searching waters, below). The two of decisions 0124 and 0125 name a task force of the side and a hex and no base: **stand at a hex** (code 9) is the task of keeping the force at that hex for the night (Standing at a hex by night, below), and **operate a carrier force** (code 10) the task of the groups and the place of a force with a carrier in it (Operating a carrier force, below). The two of decision 0141 order land units (Readying a landing, and building up a base, below): **ready a landing** (code 11) names a task force of the side, a coast hex on the map and, optionally, a date, the morning by which it is to be ready (a whole number from 1); **build up a base** (code 12) names a base of the side and what is wanted there, in order: one to five of an airfield, its damage mended, defences to a number of points from 1 to 100, the port worked and aircraft repair, each at most once. A "ready a landing" whose hex is not a coast hex on the map is refused as "Not a coast hex". Every other intent has no date: it stands until it is withdrawn, and so does a "ready a landing", whose date says when it is to be ready and not when it ends. **Control an island** (code 15, decision 0151) names an island of the scenario and, optionally, a date that the takes formed under it are given; it names no base. It is an end given to the area and a root, and a commander who names an island the scenario does not have is refused as "No such island" (Controlling an island, below). **Patrol the waters with submarines** (code 16, decision 0218) names a hex and a distance and no base; it is an end given to the area, a root, which only a commander states, and it is the only intent that may use a submarine (Patrolling waters with submarines, below). [0107 items 17 and 22, question 5; 0115; 0117; 0120, 5 and 9; 0124, 7; 0125, 1; 0141, 1 and 8; 0151, 3; 0218, 11]

The commander names the intent's number when he sets it. It is a whole number from 1. A withdrawn intent keeps its number in the state and the number is never used again. An intent records the day it was first set, the plan that serves it, the day it was withdrawn, if it was, and, since the tree (The intent tree, below), its parent, its maker and its addressee. [0107, 0117]

An intent in force carries its limits as they stand. Setting an intent that is in force again may change its figures and not its kind: it replaces its base (and a supply intent's floor and interval) together, and leaves its limits, its parent and its maker as they are. A `set_intent` that names another kind of intent than the one in force is refused as "Kind cannot change"; the commander withdraws the intent and states another. [0107, 0117, "As built", the architect's ruling after the build]

### The eleven limits

A limit says what the staff may use, where it may go, when it may go, what it must know first and what its aircraft are to strike first. Ten belong to an intent and one to a base. A limit is read up the tree: every limit set on the intent or above it binds it, the narrowest binding (The intent tree, below). A limit set nowhere above it does not bind, with one exception that follows from what it is: an intent with no forces limit anywhere above it may use what its addressee commands, which for an intent given to the area is nothing, so it gives the staff no ship and no order for it, with the one exception of a neutralise intent (Neutralising a base, below); for one given to a base it is the side's air groups based there. With no area limit the sea is open. [0107; the architect's ruling, Nov 15, 2026; 0117 item 8]

| Limit | What it holds | What reads it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Forces | The pool: a set of ships, a set of task forces, a set of air groups, a source base, and a set of land units (decision 0141; a land unit not the side's is refused as "Not your land unit"), and a set of submarines (decision 0218; a boat not the side's, one that does not exist or one that is sunk is refused as "Not your submarine"). A supply intent's forces limit must name exactly one source (a `set_limit` of forces without one is refused as "No source"); the forces limit of another kind of intent need not | The check of a staff's order and of a staff command that names ships (below); the air doctrine, for the groups it may order; the two tasks of decision 0141, for the land units they may order; the patrol order, for the boats it may use |
| Area | A set of hexes the intent's forces may enter. It is not empty, and every hex is on the map. An area not set on it or above it does not bind: the sea is open | The doctrine's route |
| Priority | A rank, 1 first, or none. An intent with none, on it and above it, comes after every ranked intent, then by number | The order in which the doctrine serves intents (the rank read up the tree, for the air doctrine) |
| Protected | A set of ships the staff may not take from the intent | The doctrine: no other intent's plan takes a protected ship, free or not, whatever the ranks (The doctrine, below) |
| Risk | Preserve, balanced or press. Balanced where none is set on the intent or above it | The cover doctrine: the share of the patrol and the fatigue at which a group rests (Covering a base from the air, below) |
| Reserve | For a base, a supply reserve and a fuel reserve, in tons, neither below 0 | Refuelling, and a load the staff orders (chapter 2, Refuelling in port and Cargo orders) |
| Not before | A day, or none. Set only on a "take a place" or a "control an island" of the commander's | The release of a take (Taking a place, Told to wait, below) |
| Wait for | A task force or a land unit of the side, or none. Set only as not before is | The release of a take, as not before |
| After | Another "take a place" or "control an island" of the commander's, or none. Set only as not before is | The release of a take, as not before |
| Targets | One to six kinds of ship, each once, in the order they are to be struck, and whether the waters of a deny come first (`waters_first`, false where left out); or not set. The waters may come first only on a "deny an area" or an intent beneath one | The staff's order of a bomber's target list and of its waters to strike first, under a task that strikes ships (Striking ships from a base, What to strike first, below); a carrier force's search arc and its closing (Operating a carrier force, below); the raid on a place of a carrier force |
| Knowledge | None, a count, or a recent count of a number of days, at least 1 (3 where the commander gives none); or not set. Set only as not before is | The need to know of a take (Taking a place, The need to know, below), in place of the risk where it is set |

[0107 items 18 to 20, 0111, 0115 items 2 and 4, 0117 items 7 and 8, 0159 items 9 and 12, 0190]

**The three limits that tell a plan to wait** (decision 0159, rule C). Each is set by `set_limit` with its value and cleared by `set_limit` with none, by a seat and in a scenario's commands, like the others. Each is read up the tree, and every one on the chain binds: of the "not before" days the latest, and every "wait for" and every "after". Only the release of a "take a place" reads them, the commander's own and those the staff forms under an island; nothing else the staff does waits for them. No ring of waits is taken in: an "after" that would have two intents wait for each other, directly or through a third, is refused (Every reason a command is refused, below). [0159 item 9; the architect's ruling on step 3]

**The knowledge limit** (decision 0159, rule D, item 12) says how much must be known of the enemy at the place before a landing goes: **none** (on any count or on none), **a count** (seen from the air or by men in contact, of any day), or **a recent count** (such a count no more than so many days old on the morning it goes; 3 where the commander gives no number, and a recent count of no days is refused). It is set and cleared by `set_limit` as the three above are, only on a "take a place" or a "control an island" of the commander's, and read up the tree: the nearest on the chain binds. Where it is not set the risk sets it as before (press none, balanced a count, preserve a recent count of 3 days); where it is set the risk no longer says how much must be known, and still says the men wanted for each of the enemy's and everything else it says. [0159 item 12]

**The targets limit** (decision 0190) says which kinds of ship the aircraft of a task are to strike, and in what order, and whether the waters of a deny come first. It is set by `set_limit` with `limit` `targets`, the intent, `kinds` (one to six kinds of ship, each once, most wanted first) and `waters_first` (true or false; false where left out). It is set on any intent of the commander's in force, as a limit is. `set_limit` with no kinds clears it. It is read up the tree: the nearest set on the chain binds. A child's targets limit replaces its parent's and is never refused as wider than it. Set on an intent whose plan is approved it is an amendment of the plan, as every limit is. Only the commander sets it: the staff sets limits only on intents it formed, and its doctrine sets none. `waters_first` may be true only on a "deny an area" or on an intent beneath one. The limit is not an order to a unit and holds no group: the staff goes on ordering the groups it binds. It gives the groups under a task that strikes ships their target list, and their waters to strike first where `waters_first` is true (Striking ships from a base, What to strike first, below); it adds to the search of a carrier force and moves what the force closes on (Operating a carrier force, below). With no targets limit anywhere, no order, ranking, closing or search arc differs from what it would have been without the rule. [0190 items 1 to 8, 19, 27]

A ship is in the pool of a forces limit if the limit lists it, or if it lies in a task force the limit lists, afloat in it or away from it at the yard. A task force is inside the limit if the limit lists it, or if it has at least one ship and every ship of it is a ship the limit lists. An air group is one an intent **may use** if it is one of the side's own and either the intent's forces limit, its own or the nearest above it, lists it, or no forces limit is set on it or above it and the group is at the base the intent is addressed to (an intent given to the area may use no group without a forces limit; a group aboard ship or in no base is not at a base). An intent given to a task force may use that force's ships and nothing else, and the air groups aboard them where every forces limit on its chain names the group or has the force inside it (A task force as an addressee, below). Under "control an island" a forces limit that names no air group does not bind the air groups: the groups based at a base are used as with no limit (Controlling an island, Forces limits under an island). A submarine is one an intent may use only if the intent is a patrol in force and the boat is one of the side's own and not sunk and is named in every forces limit on the patrol's chain that names at least one boat; with no limit on the chain naming a boat, every boat of the side's. A limit that names no boat does not bind the boats (Patrolling waters with submarines, below). [0107, 0115 item 2, 0117 item 8, 0124 item 6, 0125 item 1; 0151 item 4; 0218, rule 12]

The reserve limit names a base and no intent. It writes the base's own two reserves (chapter 2, Bases on hexes): one store, not a second. A scenario's base entry gives the same two figures (`supply_reserve_t` and `fuel_reserve_t`). It is taken in at the first step of the day, so it binds that morning's refuelling. [0111, 0107]

### The three leaves

A side's commander holds two leaves for his staff. Leave to prepare lets the staff itself move a draft plan to preparing. Leave to launch lets it move a plan that is preparing to released. The defaults are the spec's for a player: prepare given, launch withheld. Only the commander sets a leave, and he may set either on any day. [0107 item 21]

**The leave to pursue** is a third, and unlike the other two it is an order's and not the side's. It says whether a carrier force of that order may leave the order to go after what it has found (Operating a carrier force, items 28 to 41). [0214, rule 4]

- **Three values.** `ask`: the staff asks the commander's leave for each find. `go`: the staff takes the leave as given and tells him. `stay`: nothing changes from before; the staff looks for no find. [0214, rule 4]
- **Set on an intent, read up the tree** as a limit is: the nearest setting on the chain from the intent up is the one that binds, and the answer says which intent it is set on. The commander sets it with `set_pursuit` on any intent in force, his own or one the staff formed, and clears it by giving none, so that it is read up the tree again. It is not an amendment of a plan. [0214, rule 4]
- **The default,** where none is set on the chain: `go` where the side's leave to launch is given, and `ask` where it is withheld. A side with no commander is given both leaves, so it goes. A player's side has leave to launch withheld until he gives it, so it asks. [0214, rule 4]
- **Only the commander sets it.** The staff has no command that sets it, and it never changes it. A commander's rating does not move it (Not built). [0214, rule 4]
- **It is state:** the settings by intent are hashed after the rest of the side's command state (state layout `flagplot-state-v55`) and saved through the commands that set them. [0214, rule 15]

### Plans

A plan is what the staff does to serve one intent and the intents beneath it: one plan for a tree, with two exceptions that decision 0151 makes (below). It is drafted when a root intent's number is first set, and numbered in the side's own series, 1, 2, 3 and on, so a side's plan numbers depend on its own history only. A sub-intent has no plan of its own: it belongs to the plan of the nearest intent on its chain that owns one, which is its root's, and forming it drafts nothing and is no amendment (The intent tree, below). The two exceptions are a "take a place" formed under "control an island" and a "keep supplied" formed under a "hold a place" that stands under "control an island": each has a plan of its own, drafted when it is formed, so the plan of the island is not theirs and what is beneath each of them belongs to it (Controlling an island, Plans of their own). [0151 item 9] A plan has one of six states:

| State | Means |
| --- | --- |
| Draft | Drafted, not yet approved |
| Preparing | Approved for preparation. It has a baseline |
| Released | Approved to run: it may be carried out |
| Complete | Ended: its intent was withdrawn while it was released, or the staff marked it complete |
| Failed | Ended: the staff marked it failed |
| Cancelled | Ended: the commander cancelled it, or its intent was withdrawn before it was released |

Preparing and released are together **approved**. Complete, failed and cancelled are **ended**: nothing moves an ended plan again. The moves are these, and no other:

- Draft to preparing, and preparing to released, by an `approve` command (below).
- A plan that is not ended to cancelled, by a `cancel_plan` command.
- An approved plan to complete or to failed, by a `mark_plan` command, which only the staff gives.
- A plan that is not ended, when the intent that owns it is withdrawn, its root or an intent beneath the island that has a plan of its own (above): a released plan becomes complete and a draft or preparing one becomes cancelled. A take under an island whose plan its own doctrine has marked complete is ended already. [0107 items 22 to 24; 0151 item 9]

Setting again an intent that is in force whose plan has ended drafts a new plan for it, with the next number, and the intent serves from then on. A plan that has ended with its intent still in force does not withdraw the intent. [0107]

Limit: decision 0107, item 23, says a plan's phase begins when it is released and ends when the intent is withdrawn. The code has no phase state. A plan's runs are tasks held in the plan, and its state is the one of the six above (differences.md row 42). [0107 item 23]

### The baseline and amendments

A plan's **baseline** is taken the first time it is approved: the day, the intent's kind and the intent's limits as they stand that day. It never changes. A plan that is not yet approved has none.

An **amendment** is a numbered change to a plan that is approved. While a plan is approved, a `set_intent` for its intent, and a `set_limit` of any limit of its intent (or of any intent beneath it, which shares its plan), apply to the intent as they stand and are also kept in the plan as its next amendment, numbered 1 and up, with the day and the change. A reserve is no amendment, because it names a base and no intent. Withdrawing the intent is none either: it ends the plan. A change made while the plan is a draft, or after it has ended, changes the intent and is no amendment: a draft's change enters its baseline when it is approved. [0107 items 22 and 25; 0190 item 21]

What the staff may change alone in a plan is its runs and commitments, inside the limits. A change to the days, the base or a limit, and cancelling, are the commander's. [0107, question 3]

### Runs

A run is one convoy of a supply plan: a task inside the plan's one phase. It has a number in its plan (1 and up), its ships, its load in tons of supply (above 0), its source, which is the source base of its intent's forces limit on the day the run is added, and four days:

| Day | Means |
| --- | --- |
| Load | The day loading begins |
| Sail | The day the force sails |
| Arrive | The day it is due at the destination |
| Free | The day its ships are free: the morning after the day they have finished fuelling at the source on their return (by the hours of the fuelling, below, Its days) |

The days are in this order or equal. A run added has its load day no earlier than the day the command is taken in, and a run timed again has its free day no earlier than that day. A run does nothing by itself. The orders that carry it out, form the force, load it to a weight, send it and unload it, are the staff's to give on the days it names. [0107 items 26 and 27, rulings 7 and 8]

A run also records **the day it ended**, none while it has not: the day an `end_run` command was taken in (below). An ended run is ended for good: nothing takes it up again. It holds no ship, is pending no load and is counted by no reading, and so is a run of a plan that has ended: the staff reads both as finished. What is aboard its ships stays aboard, and ending a run frees no commitment: that is a command of its own. [0107, rulings 20 and 29]

### Commitments

A commitment gives one ship to one plan, and to one of its runs if named, from one day to another, both days included. A ship holds at most one commitment on any day: a commitment that overlaps another of the same ship, whatever plan it is for, is refused. The staff commits ships and frees them. Freeing a ship ends every commitment it has to the plan. No rule of the day reads a commitment: it is the staff's record, and the check of the next commitment reads it. A commitment to a plan that has ended holds no ship from the staff's reading (The doctrine, below).

A **commitment of stock** is no record of its own: it is the run. A ton at a run's source is committed to its plan from the day the run is added until it is aboard: the run's load less the supply aboard its ships while it loads there. The stock at a source that a new run may draw on is the stock, less the reserve, less those tons for the runs of every plan of the side. [0107 item 27, ruling 12]

## Commands

A command is a change to the side's command state, handed in beside orders. It is checked against the side's own units and its command state, and it is accepted or refused with a reason, as an order is. A refused command changes nothing but the log. [0107 items 1 and 2]

### Who may give each command

| Command | Given by | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `set_intent` | Commander | Sets an intent (a root), or changes the figures of one in force that the commander made, never its kind |
| `withdraw_intent` | Commander, or the staff on an intent it made (decision 0151, item 8b) | Withdraws an intent in force that the giver made, everything beneath it, and ends its plan. The commander's on an intent the staff made is refused as "Made by the staff"; the staff's on one the commander made is refused as "Not yours". The staff gives it only for the island's neutralising that has done its work (Controlling an island, The staff withdraws) |
| `set_limit` | Commander, or the staff for a forces limit | Sets one limit, on any intent; the staff sets a forces limit only, and only on an intent it formed (decision 0142, 12) |
| `set_leave` | Commander | Gives or withholds leave to prepare, or leave to launch |
| `set_pursuit` | Commander | Sets the leave to pursue (`ask`, `go` or `stay`) on an intent in force, or clears it so that it is read up the tree again (decision 0214, rule 4) |
| `answer_pursuit` | Commander | Answers, yes or no, a force's asking leave to pursue a contact (decision 0214, rule 8) |
| `approve` | Commander, or the staff with leave | Moves a draft to preparing, or a preparing plan to released |
| `cancel_plan` | Commander | Cancels a plan that has not ended |
| `hold` | Commander | Holds a ship, a task force, a whole air group, one setting of an air group (its search arc), a land unit or a submarine from the staff |
| `release` | Commander | Lifts a hold, whatever made it |
| `set_interrupt` | Commander | Sets a numbered interrupt, or sets it again, or switches a standing kind off or on (Interrupts, below) |
| `acknowledge` | Commander | Marks the last firing of an interrupt seen |
| `commit_ships` | Staff | Commits ships of the intent's pool to an approved plan from one day to another |
| `free_ships` | Staff | Ends the commitments of ships to a plan |
| `add_run` | Staff | Adds a run to an approved plan, with the plan's next run number |
| `time_run` | Staff | Gives a run new days |
| `mark_plan` | Staff | Marks an approved plan complete or failed |
| `end_run` | Staff | Ends a run of an approved plan for good (Holds and the override, below; The doctrine) |
| `form_intent` | Staff | Forms a sub-intent under an intent in force whose plan is released, or, for a "ready a landing" under "take a place", preparing or released (The intent tree, below; decision 0142, 12). Under "control an island" it forms a take, a hold, a denial or a neutralising (Controlling an island, below) |

A command from the other issuer is refused as not yours. A staff may give `approve` only where the commander has given it the leave for the move: leave to prepare for a draft, leave to launch for a plan that is preparing. The commander's `approve` needs no leave. [0107 items 4 and 21]

The commander's commands are handed in with the orders for a day and taken in at its first step, the Allied side's first, each side's in the order handed in, so a hold or a release given for a day binds from that morning, before the staff is asked. The staff's are taken in after the staff is asked, in the order handed in, before any order. [0107, The order of a day; rulings 17 and 23]

### Every reason a command is refused

The reasons for one command are tried in the order written. The first that applies is the one recorded. A unit or a base that is not the side's, and one that does not exist, are refused alike, so a refusal never shows that an enemy unit exists. A ship is the side's own only if it is not sunk.

| Command | Reason | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Any | Not yours | The issuer may not give this kind of command. Tried first |
| `set_intent` | Intent number not usable | The number is 0, or it is the number of an intent that was withdrawn |
| `set_intent` | Made by the staff | The number is that of an intent in force that the staff formed |
| `set_intent` | Kind cannot change | The number is that of an intent in force of another kind; it is withdrawn and another stated (the architect's ruling after the build of 0117) |
| `set_intent` | Not your base | The base the kind names as the side's own is not one of the side's. Every kind that names a base of the side's names one |
| `set_intent` | Not an enemy base | The target of a neutralise or a raid intent is not a base on the side's map that is not the side's (decisions 0120, 5 and 9). A base the side may not know of and one that does not exist are refused alike, in the same words. Tried after "Not your base" |
| `set_intent` | Not your force | The task force a stand-at, operate-carriers or ready-a-landing intent names is not one of the side's: "H is not one of the side's forces". A force of the other side and one that does not exist are refused alike (decisions 0124, 7, and 0125, 1). Tried after "Not an enemy base" |
| `set_intent` | Not on the map | The hex of a deny intent, a patrol intent (decision 0218, 11), a stand-at intent or an operate-carriers intent is not on the map (decisions 0123, 1, 0124, 7, and 0125, 1). Tried after "Not your base", "Not an enemy base" and "Not your force" |
| `set_intent` | Not a coast hex | The hex of a ready-a-landing intent is not a coast hex on the map: "H is not a coast hex on the map" (decision 0141, 1). Tried after "Not on the map" |
| `set_intent` | Not land | The hex of a take-a-place intent is not a hex of land on the map, coast or inland: "H is not a hex of land on the map" (decision 0142, 1). A hex off the map is refused so too. Tried after "Not a coast hex" |
| `set_intent` | No such island | The island a control-an-island intent names is not an island of the scenario: "island N is not an island of the scenario" (decision 0151, 3). Tried with the other checks of an intent's kind, after "Not land" |
| `set_intent` | Not a root | The kind is search waters, which a commander cannot state: it stands under a deny intent only and is the staff's (decision 0123, 11). Tried after "Made by the staff" and "Kind cannot change", before the kind's other checks |
| `set_intent` | No days | The floor is 0, or an interval is given and is 0 |
| `withdraw_intent` | No such intent | No intent of that number is in force |
| `withdraw_intent` | Made by the staff | The commander gave it and the intent was formed by the staff |
| `withdraw_intent` | Not yours | The staff gave it and the intent was made by the commander (decision 0151, 8b) |
| `form_intent` | No such intent | The parent is not in force. Tried after the issuer |
| `form_intent` | Wrong plan state | The parent's plan is not released; a ready-a-landing under a take-a-place is taken in while the plan is preparing too (decision 0142, 12), and so is an operate-a-carrier-force under a take-a-place, a force moved to look (decision 0159, 14) |
| `form_intent` | Cannot stand under | The kind cannot stand under the parent's kind: the pairs built are cover from the air under hold a place for the same base, and a raid under neutralise a base for the same target, strike ships and search waters under deny an area, and strike ships under hold a place for the same base (decision 0123, 5 and 11); and, since decisions 0124 and 0125, stand at a hex under deny an area where the hex is the centre of the waters, stand at a hex under hold a place where the hex is the base's, and operate a carrier force under either (the hex of a stand task under a hold that is not the base's is refused in the same words); and, since decisions 0144 and 0159, keep supplied under hold a place for the held base or for a base of the side in the held base's hex (a base in another hex, or the other side's, is refused in the same words); and, since decision 0142, ready a landing under take a place where the landing's hex is the place; and, since decision 0151, take a place, hold a place, deny an area and neutralise a base under control an island, and operate a carrier force under the deny an area that stands under an island. A patrol the staff cannot form: it is refused so under every parent, and no kind stands under a patrol (decision 0218, 11) |
| `form_intent` | Not your base | The base is not one of the side's |
| `form_intent` | Not your force | The task force of a stand-at or operate-carriers intent is not one of the side's |
| `form_intent` | Not on the map | The hex of a deny, stand-at or operate-carriers intent is not on the map |
| `form_intent` | Not an enemy base | The target is not a base on the side's map that is not the side's |
| `set_limit` | No such intent | The limit is of an intent (all but the reserve) and no intent of that number is in force. Tried first |
| `set_limit` | Not yours | The staff gave it and the intent is not one the staff formed (decision 0142, 12). Tried after "No such intent"; a staff's limit of another kind than forces is refused as not yours first |
| `set_limit` not before, wait for, after | No wait limit on | The intent is not a "take a place" or a "control an island", or the staff formed it: "intent:N is not a take_place or a control_island of the commander's: only those may be told to wait" (decision 0159, 9). Tried after "Not yours", for a limit set and one cleared alike |
| `set_limit` knowledge | No knowledge limit on | The intent is not a "take a place" or a "control an island", or the staff formed it: "intent:N is not a take_place or a control_island of the commander's: only those may be told how much must be known" (decision 0159, 12). Tried after "Not yours", for a limit set and one cleared alike |
| `set_limit` knowledge | Recent count of no days | The limit asks for a recent count of 0 days (decision 0159, 12). Tried after "No knowledge limit on" |
| `set_limit` targets | Bad target kinds | The limit names no kind, or a kind twice (the tool also refuses a `waters_first` of true with no kinds): "the targets limit on intent:N must name one to six kinds of ship, each once" (decision 0190). Tried after "Not yours" |
| `set_limit` targets | No waters first on | `waters_first` is true and the intent is not a "deny an area" and does not stand beneath one: "intent:N is not a deny and does not stand beneath one: only there may the waters come first" (decision 0190, 27). Tried after "Bad target kinds" |
| `set_limit` forces | No source | The intent is a supply intent or a take-a-place intent and the limit names no source (decision 0142, 2) |
| `set_limit` forces | Not your ship | A listed ship is not the side's, in ship order |
| `set_limit` forces | Not your force | A listed task force is not the side's, in force order |
| `set_limit` forces | Not your group | A listed air group is not the side's, in group order |
| `set_limit` forces | Not your base | The source is not one of the side's |
| `set_limit` forces | Not your land unit | A listed land unit is not the side's, in unit order (decision 0141) |
| `set_limit` forces | Not your submarine | A listed submarine is not the side's, does not exist or is sunk, in boat order (decision 0218, 34). Tried after "Not your land unit" |
| `set_limit` area | Bad area | The area has no hex, or a hex off the map |
| `set_limit` wait for | Not your force, not your land unit | The task force or land unit named is not the side's: the same words as a forces limit naming it, so a force or unit of the other side and one that does not exist are refused alike (decision 0159, 9) |
| `set_limit` after | No such intent | The intent named is not in force: one withdrawn, and one never set, are refused alike |
| `set_limit` after | Not an end to wait for | The intent named is the limit's own intent, or is not a "take a place" or a "control an island", or the staff formed it, or the limit would close a ring of waits: walking the "after" limits from the intent named, each intent's read up the tree, comes back to the limit's own intent, directly or through others: "intent:N is not another take_place or control_island of the commander's to wait for" (decision 0159, 9; the ring, the architect's ruling on step 3) |
| `set_limit` protected | Not your ship | A listed ship is not the side's |
| `set_limit` reserve | Not your base | The base is not one of the side's |
| `set_limit` reserve | Bad weight | The supply reserve, then the fuel reserve, is below 0 |
| `set_limit` forces, area, risk | Wider than its parent | The intent has a parent and the limit does not narrow (The intent tree, Limits read up the tree). Tried after every other reason of the limit |
| `approve` | No such plan | No plan of that number |
| `approve` | Wrong plan state | The plan is neither a draft nor preparing |
| `approve` | No leave | The staff gave it, and the leave the move needs is withheld |
| `cancel_plan` | No such plan | No plan of that number |
| `cancel_plan` | Wrong plan state | The plan has ended |
| `commit_ships` | No such plan | No plan of that number |
| `commit_ships` | Wrong plan state | The plan is not approved |
| `commit_ships` | No forces limit, no source | The plan's intent has no forces limit of its own; or it has one that names no source |
| `commit_ships` | Bad days | The first day is before the day of the command, or after the last |
| `commit_ships` | No such run | A run is named and the plan has no run of that number |
| `hold` | Not your ship | A ship is named and it is not the side's, or is sunk, or does not exist |
| `hold` | Not your force | A task force is named and it is not the side's |
| `hold` | Not your group | An air group is named, whole or by a setting, and it is not the side's |
| `hold` | Not your land unit | A land unit is named and it is not the side's (decision 0141, 14) |
| `hold` | Not your submarine | A submarine is named and it is not the side's, does not exist or is sunk: one reason for all (decision 0218, 34). Each of these five is tried for the one target the command names |
| `hold` | Already held | The target has a hold already, made by a command or by an order. A whole air group and its search arc are two targets, and a group may have both |
| `release` | Not held | The target has no hold. An enemy unit and one that does not exist are refused so too |
| `set_pursuit` | No such intent | No intent of that number is in force: "intent:N is not in force". An intent the commander made and one the staff formed are alike. Not an amendment: nothing of a plan is looked at (decision 0214, rule 4) |
| `answer_pursuit` | Not your force | The task force is not one of the side's: "F is not one of the side's forces". A force of the other side and one that does not exist are refused alike. Nothing else is checked: the answer need not match an asking that was made, and the contact's number is not looked at (decision 0214, rule 8) |
| `set_interrupt` | Interrupt number not usable | A numbered interrupt is set and its number is 0 |
| `set_interrupt` | Not your base | A numbered interrupt is set and its base is not one of the side's |
| `set_interrupt` | No days | A numbered interrupt is set and its number of days is 0. Setting a standing kind has no other reason: it is refused only as not yours |
| `acknowledge` | No such interrupt | A numbered interrupt is named and none of that number was set |
| `acknowledge` | No such plan | A standing interrupt for a plan is named and the side has no plan of that number |
| `acknowledge` | Not your ship | A standing interrupt for a ship is named and the ship is not the side's, whether it exists or not |
| `acknowledge` | Not your force | A standing interrupt for a task force is named and the force is not the side's, whether it exists or not (decision 0214) |
| `acknowledge` | Not fired | The interrupt has never fired: for a standing one, the side holds no record of it |
| `acknowledge` | Already acknowledged | The last firing is acknowledged already |
| `commit_ships` | Not your ship, not in the pool, held, already committed | For each ship in ship order, in that order: it is not the side's; it is not in the pool of the forces limit; it is held (itself, its force, or any ship of its force); it has a commitment that overlaps the days asked for |
| `free_ships` | No such plan | No plan of that number. A plan of any state is found |
| `free_ships` | Not committed | A ship has no commitment to the plan |
| `add_run` | No such plan, wrong plan state, no forces limit | As for `commit_ships` |
| `add_run` | No ships | The run names no ship |
| `add_run` | Not your ship, not in the pool, held | For each ship in ship order, in that order, as for `commit_ships` (a ship that is held is refused before the load and the days are looked at) |
| `add_run` | Bad weight | The load is not above 0 |
| `add_run` | Bad days | The days are not in order, or the load day is before the day of the command |
| `time_run` | No such plan, wrong plan state | As for `commit_ships` |
| `time_run` | No such run | The plan has no run of that number |
| `time_run` | Bad days | The days are not in order, or the free day is before the day of the command |
| `mark_plan` | No such plan, wrong plan state | As for `commit_ships` |
| `end_run` | No such plan, wrong plan state | As for `commit_ships`: the plan is not approved |
| `end_run` | No such run | The plan has no run of that number |
| `end_run` | Run ended | The run has ended already |

`set_leave` is refused only when the staff gives it, and so are `set_pursuit` and `answer_pursuit`, as not yours: the staff has neither. [0107 items 1, 4, 22 and 27, rulings 17, 20, 31 and 33; 0214]

The same intent number set twice. Two `set_intent` commands of one day that name the same new number are taken in in the order handed in: the first sets the intent and drafts its plan, and the second changes the intent that now exists. The plan is a draft, so the second is no amendment and drafts nothing. The intent is as the second left it. [0107]

### What a command does and records

A command that passes is applied, then `CommandTaken` is recorded with the whole command and its issuer. It has no cause: it is the root record of the change. A command that fails records `CommandRefused` with the command, its issuer and the reason, and has no cause. What an accepted command sets off is recorded after `CommandTaken` as an event that cites it:

| Event | Recorded when |
| --- | --- |
| `PlanDrafted` | An intent's number is first set, or an intent in force whose plan has ended is set again. Names the plan and the intent |
| `PlanStateChanged` | A plan changes state, by `approve`, `cancel_plan`, `mark_plan` or the withdrawal of its intent. Names the plan and both states |
| `PlanAmended` | A change is kept as an amendment. Names the plan and the amendment's number |
| `IntentFormed` | A `form_intent` is taken in. Names the side, the new intent's number and its parent |
| `IntentWithdrawn` | A `withdraw_intent` is taken in: one for each intent beneath the one withdrawn, in the order the tree gives (The intent tree, below). Names the side, the intent and the one it stood under |

A `set_leave`, a `set_pursuit`, a `hold`, a `release`, a `set_interrupt`, an `acknowledge`, a `commit_ships`, a `free_ships`, an `add_run`, a `time_run` and an `end_run` set off no other event: the command's own record, with the whole command, is the record of the change. An `answer_pursuit` sets off none either; a yes begins the pursuit (Operating a carrier force, item 34) and its `CommandTaken` is the event the pursuit and the staff's orders under it cite as their cause. There is no command number. Withdrawing an intent whose plan has already ended, and setting an intent that is a draft again, set off nothing. [0107]

One more event is recorded for a hold, and it is not a command's:

| Event | Recorded when |
| --- | --- |
| `HeldByOrder` | The override (below) holds a unit by an order of the commander's. Names the side, what is held and the order. It cites the order and the event that accepted it, and is recorded after them, at the step of the override, with no pulse; for an order given inside a day, at the start of the pulse it is taken in, with that pulse (decision 0156) |

A side sees: its own `CommandTaken`, `CommandRefused`, `PlanDrafted`, `PlanStateChanged`, `PlanAmended`, `HeldByOrder`, `IntentFormed` and `IntentWithdrawn`, whole, and the enemy sees none of them. Each of these kinds is decided in `side_events.rs`. The two events of step 5, `StaffNotice` and `InterruptFired`, are the same (Interrupts and Notices, below). [0107, ruling 23; 0117]

## The intent tree

Intents form a tree. An intent is an end, a state of the world the commander wants of one of the side's bases; the staff breaks an end into sub-intents, and orders are given at the leaves. The tree is state: it is hashed, changed only by commands, and rebuilt by a replay. The engine records and checks it and never reads a reason's words about it. [0116, 0117]

**What an intent gains.** Each intent has three more fields beside its kind and base:

| Field | Holds |
| --- | --- |
| Parent | The intent it stands under, or none for a **root** |
| Maker | The commander (by `set_intent`) or the staff (by `form_intent`) |
| Addressee | The level the intent is given to. It is set by the kind and chosen by nobody: keeping a base supplied, neutralising a base, denying an area, patrolling waters with submarines and controlling an island are given to the area; holding a place, covering a base, raiding a base, striking ships from a base and searching waters from a base are given to the base they name (for a raid, the base it raids from). A task force is an addressee for the two tasks that name one: stand at a hex and operate a carrier force are given to the force they name (A task force as an addressee, below). An air group is a level too, but no intent is given to it (A doctrine belongs to a level, below). A land formation is not built as an addressee |

The level that owns an intent is the kind of its addressee. [0117 items 1 and 2; 0124 items 6 and 7]

**Roots and sub-intents.** A commander's `set_intent` makes a root, whatever its kind: a commander states a task such as cover himself by stating it as a root (every kind but search waters, which is refused as "Not a root"; stand at a hex and operate a carrier force he may state, naming the force and the hex). The staff's `form_intent` makes a sub-intent: it names the parent and the kind, and the new intent takes the number one above the highest the side has ever used (a withdrawn number is never reused). Its maker is the staff, its addressee is its kind's, its plan is its parent's (so its root's: one plan for a tree, but for the two intents of decision 0151 that have a plan of their own, Plans, above), it has no limits of its own, and it is set on the day the command is taken in. Intake refuses a `form_intent` as in Every reason a command is refused: the parent must be in force, its plan released (or preparing, for "ready a landing" and "operate a carrier force" under "take a place"), and the kind able to stand under the parent's kind. Ten pairs are built, and the four that stand under control an island (below): cover from the air under hold a place for the same base, a raid under neutralise a base for the same target, strike ships and search waters under deny an area, strike ships under hold a place for the same base, stand at a hex under deny an area (the hex is the centre of the waters) and under hold a place (the hex is the base's, which intake checks), and operate a carrier force under deny an area and under hold a place, and, since decision 0202, under neutralise a base (Carriers serving an order for an end, below); and, since, "ready a landing" under "take a place" at the place's hex (decision 0142) and "keep supplied" under "hold a place" for the same base, or for a base of the side in the held base's hex, which intake checks (decisions 0144, 7, and 0159, A: its floor may be 0, the margin at press, and with no forces limit of its own the supply doctrine reads the nearest one up the tree, the hold's); a commander cannot name a parent. Under "control an island" the staff forms a take a place, a hold a place, a deny an area and a neutralise a base, and under the denial an operate a carrier force (decision 0151, 6). `form_intent` records `IntentFormed`, citing the command's `CommandTaken`; it drafts no plan, but for a take or a supply intent under an island (Plans, above), which drafts its own and records `PlanDrafted` after `IntentFormed`, and is no amendment. [0117 items 3, 4 and 9; 0151 items 6 and 9]

**A staff-made intent is the staff's.** `set_intent` and, from the commander, `withdraw_intent` on the number of an intent the staff formed are refused as "Made by the staff". The commander shapes it by its limits (below), by holds, and by withdrawing the intent it stands under. The staff may withdraw an intent it made, and only that (decision 0151, item 8b; Controlling an island, below): one it has not made is refused as "Not yours". [0117 item 5; 0151 item 8b]

**Withdrawing a tree.** A `withdraw_intent` withdraws the intent and every intent in force beneath it, depth first, each branch by number, all with the day of the command. Each intent beneath records an `IntentWithdrawn` event naming the intent it stood under, citing the command's `CommandTaken` (the intent named in the command has no event of its own: the command is its record). A withdrawn intent's plan ends as for any intent (Plans, above) when it is the root, or owns a plan of its own; withdrawing the island ends the plan of each take and supply intent beneath it that has one. [0117 item 6; 0151 item 9]

**Limits read up the tree.** The commander may set a limit on any intent in force, a staff-made one too (`set_limit` names the intent). Every limit set on the intent and above it binds it, so the narrowest binds: the risk is the least bold of those set on the chain, balanced where none is set; an air group may be used only where every forces limit on the chain names it, and a ship, a task force or a forming base is checked against every forces limit on the chain; the area is the hexes in every area set on the chain. The rank is the nearest set on the chain, and the protected limit is the intent's own. A limit set on a parent after one on its child binds the child from then on. (As first built, a child's own limit shadowed its parent's; the architect ruled after the build that the narrowest binds, decision 0117, "As built".) With no forces limit on it or above it, an intent may use what its addressee commands (The six limits, above). A limit set on an intent that is in force while its plan is approved is an amendment of the plan, whichever intent of the tree it is on. Under "control an island" the limits above an intent are read as Controlling an island, Forces limits under an island, says. For a "keep supplied" under a hold under an island whose plan is its own and that has no forces limit of its own, intake reads the forces limit and the source of a run (`add_run`, `commit_ships`) up the tree: the hold's, set by the staff. [0117 items 7 and 8; 0151 items 4 and 9]

**A limit must narrow.** On an intent that has a parent, `set_limit` of forces, risk or area is refused as "Wider than its parent" unless it narrows what the parent may use, read up the tree from the parent:

- **Forces:** each ship is in the parent's pool, each task force is inside the parent's limit, each air group is one the parent may use, and a source is named only where it is the parent's source. Where the parent has no forces limit on it or above it, the limit may name air groups the parent may use and no ship, task force or source.
- **Risk:** no bolder than the parent's, from preserve the force (the most careful) through balanced to press.
- **Area:** a subset of the parent's area. Where the parent has none, any area.

A priority, a protected set and a reserve are never refused for this. A limit on a root is not narrowed against anything. This reason is tried after every other reason of the limit. [0117 item 7. The rule for an area is in the code and decision 0117 does not state it]

**A reason names the chain above it.** A reason's serves part gains, for the intent it serves, the intents above it, nearest first: each with its number, its maker and its statement in words as it stood that morning. The top of every chain is an intent the commander stated. A reason for a root has none. The words follow the statement, for example "intent 2: cover base:1 from the air; plan 1; under intent 1 (the commander's): hold base:1". A game saved before the chain reads each reason with none. The engine records the chain and never reads it. [0117 item 10]

**Order among intents.** The staff serves intents of a kind in priority order: a rank, 1 first, read up the tree, then the intents with none, each by number. The supply doctrine serves keep-supplied intents (roots, and those the staff forms under a hold, decision 0144) as it always has; the air doctrine serves hold and cover intents (The doctrine, covering a base from the air, below). [0107 ruling 10; 0117 item 11]

**The tools** show the tree: `command_state` lists each intent under its parent with its maker, addressee, children and depth, and its limits as set and as read up the tree with the intent each is read from; `propose` shows the tree the staff would form; `why` and `staff_orders` give the chain (The tools, below). [0117]

A side sees: its own intents, whole, with `IntentFormed` and `IntentWithdrawn`, and the acceptance or refusal of each command. The enemy sees none of it. [0117]

## Holds and the override

A **hold** keeps one thing from the staff: the commander says "this is mine, not yours". It is a record in the side's command state, hashed with it, and it has no date of its own: it stands until a `release`. [0107, rulings 17 and 18]

**What can be held.** A ship (the side's own and afloat), a task force of the side, an air group of the side as a whole, and one setting of an air group, which today is its search arc (a group's search arc and the mission that sets it flying). A group held as a whole is given no order of any kind by the staff; a group whose search arc is held is given no order for its arc or its mission. A group and its search arc are two targets and each may have a hold. A land unit of the side may be held (decision 0141, 14): the staff gives it no order. So may a submarine of the side's own, not sunk (decision 0218, 34): the staff gives her no order, and a patrol passes her over (Patrolling waters with submarines, below). A hold on a base waits for a stage whose doctrine acts on a base. A target has one hold: a second `hold` of a target already held is refused, whoever made the first. A hold records the day it was made and what made it: a `hold` command, or an order of the commander's (the override, below). A `release` lifts the hold on a target whatever made it, and a later order of the commander's to the unit holds it again (below). A hold moves nothing and stops nothing: a held unit keeps its standing orders and goes on by them. [0107, rulings 17 and 19]

**What a hold takes from the staff.** One rule says which ships are held, and every rule below uses it: a ship is held if it is held itself, if the task force it is in is held, or if any ship of that force is held. A held ship is not free to the staff, no ship of a held force is free, and no ship of a force that has a held ship in it is free: an order to that force would move the held ship. A held setting keeps the staff from changing it, and a held air group is one the staff gives no order to: the air doctrine leaves a group out of its work altogether if the group or its search arc is held (Covering a base from the air, below). Nothing else of the side is touched. The staff gives no order to what is held. [0107, ruling 18]

**The guard behind it.** The engine refuses what the staff would do to a held thing, so the doctrine's own care is not the only check:

- A staff command that names ships, `commit_ships` and `add_run`, is refused as held for a ship that is held (Every reason a command is refused).
- A staff's order is refused **held** ("the unit is held by its commander", with the target) if it is for a task force that is held or has a held ship in it; if it is an order to form a task force that names a held ship, or a ship in a held force; if it is any order for an air group that is held as a whole; or if it is a search-arc order or an air mission order for an air group whose search arc is held (a patrol-share order for such a group is not refused as held); or if it is any order for a land unit that is held (decision 0141, 14), or any order for a submarine that is held (decision 0218, 34). It is tried before the check of the forces limit (The forces limit on a staff's orders, below), so a held unit is refused as held whether or not it lies in the limit. A base order other than forming is never refused as held.
- The guard binds orders handed in by a staff. It does not bind the commander's own orders, and it does not bind an order a scenario's script gives with the issuer `staff`, which is a scenario's statement (below).

A refused order is numbered, kept and recorded as refused, as any is. [0107, rulings 18 and 30]

**The override.** The commander's own order to a unit the staff works with holds that unit, from that morning. It is step 2 of the day (chapter 1, Before the pulses). In the code it runs after the returns from the yard and before the staff is asked. For each side with at least one intent in force:

1. The side's pending orders are walked in the order intake sorts them: orders to form a task force first, then the rest by the unit they are for. An order of the issuer staff is skipped, so a scenario's scripted staff order never overrides.
2. An order **overrides** if it is for a unit inside the forces limit of an intent of the side in force: a task force the limit lists, or one with at least one ship whose every ship the limit lists, or one that an intent of the side in force is addressed to (decision 0124, 6); or an order at any base to form a task force that names at least one ship of the pool of such a limit (a ship the limit lists or one in a task force it lists). Or it is an order of any kind for an air group that some intent of the side in force may use (The six limits, above: named in the forces limit read up the tree, or, with none, at the base the intent is addressed to; the intent's plan is not looked at). Or it is an order of any kind for a land unit that some intent of the side in force may use (Readying a landing, and building up a base, below), which holds the unit (decision 0141, 14). Or it is an order for a submarine that a patrol in force may use or that an intent in force names in its forces limit (decision 0218, 12 and 34), which holds the boat; an order for a boat no intent may use or names does not override. An order for a land unit no intent may use, a base order other than forming, a group no intent may use, or a unit in no forces limit and in no intent's addressing does not override: it stays pending, in the order handed in, for step 5, as it was.
3. An order that overrides is taken in at once: numbered then, ahead of every other order of the day, and checked as any order is. If it is accepted, it holds what it was for, a task force, an air group as a whole, a land unit, a submarine, or each ship of the pool that a forming order names, and a `HeldByOrder` event is recorded for each, citing the order and its acceptance. An order that is refused holds nothing. A target that is held already gets no second hold, and the order is taken in all the same.

A side with no intent in force is not touched, and the day is as it was. `World::at_staff_step` takes the override too, so the view a staff is asked on shows the holds of the morning. A hold made by an order is lifted by a `release` like any other, and a later order of the commander's to the unit holds it again at the next step 2. [0107, rulings 23 and 30]

**The override inside a day.** In a scenario that turns orders inside a day on (chapter 1, Orders inside a day), the override acts at 0600 and 1200 as well. An order the commander hands in there is taken in at the start of the pulse that follows, with the pulse on its events; if it is accepted and its unit lies inside the forces limit of an intent in force, by the same test as step 2 above (the code is the same), it holds the unit with a `HeldByOrder` event in that pulse, citing the order and its acceptance. A unit already held is not held again. An order that is refused, as given at dusk or for any other reason, holds nothing. The staff is not asked inside a day: the hold binds it from its next asking, at dusk, when it sets aside a run of the held unit that has not sailed (The doctrine: keep a base supplied, Each morning, before any intent) and gives the unit no order. A trial asked at 0600 or 1200 (chapter 9, A seat's trial, rule 14) does not try such an order, nor the hold it makes. [0156, rule 5; 0204, 5]

A side sees: its own holds, whole: each with its target, the day, whether a command or an order made it, and the order's number, in the agent's `command_state` (`holds`); its own `HeldByOrder`; the acceptance or refusal of each hold and release command and of each order, with the reason, including a staff order refused as held. The enemy sees none of it. A hold on an enemy unit, or on one that does not exist, is refused as not the side's, so a refusal never shows that an enemy unit exists. [0107, rulings 17 and 23]

## Interrupts

An interrupt is something the commander wants to be told of: when it fires, a run of days stops (The tools, `run`) and the command report lists it. It changes nothing else: a firing moves no unit, gives no order and changes no stock. It is command state: the interrupts set, whether each is armed, the day each last fired and whether that firing is acknowledged. It is hashed, saved through the commands that made it and rebuilt by a replay. [0107, rulings 31 and 32]

**The kinds.** One is set by the commander, and six stand without being set. [0107, ruling 33; 0159 item 7; 0191 item 21; 0214 item 7]

| Kind | Holds on a morning when | Armed apart for |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Set: a base's days of supply below a number | The days of supply of the hex of the base (Days of supply, below), read when the staff is asked that morning, has a figure and the figure is below the number of days. 35 is not below 35. It does not hold where the hex has no figure (no supply is planned to be used there), or where the base is no longer the side's | Each numbered interrupt |
| Standing `cannot_meet` | The staff handed in a cannot-meet notice for the plan that morning (Notices, below) | Each plan |
| Standing `waits_for_leave` | The staff handed in a notice for the plan that it waits for a leave, to prepare or to launch | Each plan |
| Standing `cannot_bring_enough` | The staff handed in a notice for the plan that it cannot bring the men its take wants (Notices, below), and where men ashore wait with nothing more coming (Taking a place, Ashore, item 10: it fires on the first such morning, not on later notices). Added at the end, code 4 (decision 0159, 7) | Each plan |
| Standing `lift_turned_back` | The staff handed in a notice for the plan that it turned its take's lift back (Notices, below; Taking a place, A lift turned back). Added at the end, code 5 (decision 0191, 21) | Each plan |
| Standing `ship_lost` | A ship of the side was lost the day before: a ship sunk in a fight (`ShipSunk`) or lost in port with its base (`ShipLostWithBase`) on the day before, by the side the ship belongs to. Tested only for a side with at least one intent in force, so a game with no command gets no event of it. On day 1 there is no day before | Each ship |
| Standing `asks_leave_to_pursue` | The staff handed in a notice that a force asks leave to pursue (Notices, below) that morning. Its subject is a task force, the force the notice names. Added at the end, code 6 (decision 0214, rule 7) | Each task force |

A set interrupt names a base of the side and a number of days, at least 1. It has its own number, a whole number from 1, that the commander gives; a number is not withdrawn and no command withdraws one. A standing kind is on until the commander switches it off, and on again when he switches it on. A kind that is off is not tested; the records of its subjects stay as they were. The kinds in the code are `SupplyBelow` for the set one and `cannot_meet`, `waits_for_leave`, `ship_lost`, `cannot_bring_enough`, `lift_turned_back` and `asks_leave_to_pursue` for the standing ones; they are tested in that order, the order of their codes. [0107, rulings 31 and 33; 0159 item 7; 0191 item 21; 0214 item 7]

**Armed, fired, acknowledged.** A set interrupt is armed when it is set, never fired and not acknowledged. A standing interrupt for a subject that has no record is armed and never fired, and gets a record the first morning it fires. On a morning when the condition holds and the interrupt is armed, it fires: it is no longer armed, it last fired today and it is not acknowledged. On a morning when the condition holds and it is not armed, nothing happens. On a morning when the condition does not hold, it is armed again, which for a standing kind is every record of the kind whose subject does not hold. So an interrupt already true when it is set fires that morning, once, and then fires again only after a morning on which it did not hold. Acknowledging marks the last firing seen and arms nothing; the next firing is not acknowledged. [0107, ruling 32]

**When they are tested.** Each morning, at step 5: after the staff's commands are taken in and its notices recorded, and before any order is taken in. The figure a set interrupt is tested on is the one read at step 4, on the view the staff was asked on (after the commands and the override, before the staff's commands), so a staff's order or command of that morning changes no reading it is tested on. The sides are tested in side order, Allied first; for a side the set interrupts by number, then the standing kinds in the order of their codes (`cannot_meet`, `waits_for_leave`, `ship_lost`, `cannot_bring_enough`, `lift_turned_back`, `asks_leave_to_pursue`), and for a kind its subjects in plan, then ship, then task force number order. A side with a set interrupt and no staff is tested all the same. [0107, ruling 31]

**What a firing records.** An `InterruptFired` event, with no pulse, naming the side and the interrupt. A set one carries its condition and the figure it was tested on; a standing one carries neither. It cites the cause of the firing:

| Interrupt | Cites |
| --- | --- |
| A set one | The latest `CommandTaken` of the command that set that number. A set interrupt of a scenario cites its scripted command |
| A standing one for a plan | The `StaffNotice` events of the morning for the plan that make it hold |
| `asks_leave_to_pursue` | The `StaffNotice` events of the morning that the force asks leave to pursue |
| `ship_lost` | The event that sank the ship, or lost it with its base |

When breakdowns are on, a set interrupt's firing records the stock in tons, the planned use in tons, the days of supply and the number it fell below. Recording it changes nothing. A firing draws no random number. [0107, rulings 31 and 33]

**The commands.**

- `set_interrupt` with a number, a base and a number of days sets that interrupt, or sets it again: the condition is replaced, the day set is today and its status is as a new one's, armed and never fired. With a standing kind and `on` it switches the kind off or on, from that morning.
- `acknowledge` names a set interrupt by its number, or a standing one by its kind and the plan or the ship. It marks the interrupt's last firing seen.

Both are the commander's and are taken in at the first step of the day, so a set interrupt is tested that same morning and an acknowledgement made that day stands for the firing of an earlier day. The refusals are in the table above (Every reason a command is refused). An acknowledgement of a ship that is not the side's is refused as not the side's, whether the ship exists or not, so a refusal never shows that an enemy unit exists. [0107, rulings 31 to 33]

**The interrupt of a force's asking** (`asks_leave_to_pursue`, decision 0214, rules 7 and 9). It is the one interrupt that stops the clock before a day and not after it:

- **It fires at the morning's test,** as the others do, on the notice that the force asks leave to pursue. It is armed apart for each task force, and armed again on a morning on which the staff hands in no such notice for that force. A force that asks on a run of mornings fires once, on the first.
- **The seat is stopped before that.** A seat that runs time (`run` or `advance`) is stopped in the evening, at the dusk before the day the staff would ask in, with the day not yet resolved; chapter 1, The stop in the evening, has it. The stop is worked on a copy of the coming day with the side's queued commands taken in, and it is made where the standing interrupt is on and the force's record is absent or armed. So a force that was asked about the dusk before and not answered does not stop the clock again while its asking goes on unbroken. `end_day` does not stop.
- **A firing on a played day does not stop `run` again** after that day. The stop for that asking was the evening before; a second stop for it would be a second one. `run` stops after a day for every other interrupt as before. The firing is still listed in the command report and in `command_state`.
- **Switching it off does not give leave.** The standing kind off leaves the staff asking in its notices, and the force where it is: no stop, no answer, no leave. It is the setting of the leave to pursue that gives or withholds leave (The three leaves, above), and not the switch.
- **Its subject is a task force,** so `acknowledge` of it names the kind and the task force, in the side's number. The refusals of `acknowledge` are as for the other standing kinds (a force that is not the side's is refused as not the side's, whether it exists or not).

A side sees: its own set interrupts, standing records and the kinds it has switched off, with whether each is armed, when it last fired and whether that is acknowledged, in the agent's `command_state` (`interrupts`) and `report`; its own `InterruptFired`, whole; and the acceptance or refusal of each command. The enemy sees none of it. [0107, rulings 31 and 37]

## The staff

### What a staff is, and when it is asked

A staff is code outside `sim-core`. `sim-core` defines what it is: given a side's view, it decides the day and hands in a `StaffDecisions`:

- **Orders**, each with the intent it serves and its reason.
- **Staff commands**: the commands of the staff's column above, each with its reason.
- **Decisions to give no order**, each a reason.
- **Notices**: structured statements beside these, each resting on one of the reasons above (Notices, below).

A staff is a function of the view: it is asked with a shared reference, so that a staff asked twice on the same view gives the same answer. That is what `replay --check-staff` tests. The function that resolves a day takes each side's staff or none. A side with none is not asked, and a day with no staff and no command is resolved as it was. [0107 items 4, 7 and 8]

A staff is asked once a day, at its place in the day's order (chapter 1, Before the pulses): after the commander's commands are taken in, after forces back from the yard are home, and after the override has taken in the commander's orders to units in a forces limit and held them, and before any other order is taken in. It gets the side's view as it then stands, so it sees the reserves, the leaves and the holds set that day, the orders that held a unit that morning, and the fuel ships and bases held at the end of the day before: nothing is refuelled before the staff is asked (chapter 2, Refuelling in port). It never gets the truth (chapter 1, Truth and belief). [0107, The order of a day]

What a replay hands in, in place of asking, is taken in at the same step as if a staff had given it. If a side has a live staff and something handed in for it, both are taken in, what was handed in first.

The staff's commands are taken in first, each side's in the order handed in, and after each side's commands its decisions to give no order are recorded, and then its notices (Notices, below). When every side's are done, the interrupts are tested (Interrupts, above). Then every order of the day that the override has not already taken in is taken in: the commander's and the staff's together (chapter 1, How orders are numbered and sorted). A staff's order comes after the commander's to the same unit, so the staff's replaces the commander's in a standing slot. A staff's order is marked with the issuer staff and the intent it serves (chapter 1, Who can issue), and is refused as held if it is for something the commander holds (Holds and the override, above). An air group's order serves the cover intent, and not the hold intent above it. A cargo order the staff gives heeds the reserve of a base (chapter 2, Cargo orders). [0107, 0111]

Every runner gives both sides the staff of `sim-staff` (`sim_staff::staffs()`): the doctrine for keeping a base supplied and, after it, the air doctrine for holding a place and covering a base from the air (below). A staff whose side has no intent in force and no commitment to free hands in nothing: nothing is taken in and nothing is saved for that day. [0107, 0115, 0117]

### The forces limit on a staff's orders

A staff's order that is not refused as held (Holds and the override, above) is refused as **outside the forces limit** ("the order is for a unit outside the forces limit of the intent it serves") unless the intent the order serves is in force and:

- for an order to an air group, the group is one the intent may use (The six limits, above): named in every forces limit on its chain, or, with no forces limit on it or above it, at the base it is addressed to, or, for an intent addressed to a task force, aboard that force's ships with every forces limit naming it or having the force inside it; or
- for an order to a land unit, the unit is one the intent may use (decision 0141; Readying a landing, and building up a base, below): named in every forces limit on its chain, or, with no forces limit on it or above it, for a "build up a base", a working unit of the side with men ashore in its base's hex; or, for a "hold a place" with no land unit named in any forces limit on its chain, a fighting unit of the side with men ashore in its base's hex (decision 0144, 4); or
