8. **Reasons.** An order's reason says the group, its kind, its fatigue against the level of the risk, what the group has and what is wanted, and, for the share, the share and what raised it; for an arc, the threats and the arc they give, and the contacts the group's arc was set against that the side has since dropped. A decision to give no order is handed in on a morning on which a group of the intent got no order or the intent cannot be met: it lists each such group with its fatigue, mission and setting, the share, the level and the arc, and, where it cannot, the sentence that it cannot. [0115 item 11]

**A raid's bomber.** Where a raid task is in force at the cover's base (Raiding a base, below), a group that the raid may use and that can bomb the raid's target on normal reach is the raid's, though it can also search. The cover gives it no order: it is left out of the groups the cover serves, and the cover's reason says that the group is the raid's. The cover therefore searches only with the groups no raid in force may use as bombers. A fighter is not a bomber and stays the cover's: where a cover of its base that may use it is in force, the raid does not order it (Raiding a base, rule 16). The cover's order of the morning is worked first, so that the raid reads, for each fighter the cover has, the patrol share the cover wants this morning, or that the cover rests it. [0120, 16, amending 0115, 7]

**A strike-ships task's bomber, and the waters to deny** (decision 0123). A group that a strike-ships task in force at the cover's base may use as a bomber is the task's, as a raid's bomber is: the cover gives it no order (Striking ships from a base, below). The search arc of rule 6 has more threats where the base's search groups reach the waters of a deny intent in force with a released plan: every hex of those waters is added, beside the hexes of the enemy's bases and the contacts the side holds, where a range of a search group the cover serves touches them (Searching waters, below). A deny intent whose waters none of them touches adds none. One arc serves the base's search groups. [0123 item 13]

A cover intent's limits narrow as the tree says (The intent tree, above): a forces limit on the staff's cover intent names only groups its hold intent may use, and a risk limit is no bolder than its hold intent's.

**A bomber that takes a turn at the search** (decision 0131, 8). Where a raid task or a strike-ships task is in force at the cover's base and the cover has no group that can only search (a group that can search and cannot attack a ship), the task that has the bombers lends the cover its search, where it has two or more bomber groups that can search, can fly and are not held: the least tired of them, then the lowest numbered, chosen each morning. That group is the cover's that day, whatever the cover's forces limit says: it flies the cover's arc by rules 3 and 4 (it rests above the level, as any search group does) and the raid or the strike task gives it no order and says it searches today. With one such group, it holds the strike or the raid and the cover has no searcher. Where both a raid and a strike task have such bombers, the first in priority lends. Who lends is worked out once each morning, before the cover is served, and the same choice is read by the raid and the strike task. A group that searched yesterday and strikes today is given the missions in turn by orders. The search task's groups are read the same way (Searching waters, below). [0131 items 8 and 9]

**The cost of search.** The engine sends every aircraft of a search group that can fly, in the morning alone when it is set to search once a day and in both daylight pulses otherwise. A search tires a group by its own figure for distance where the fatigue figures give one (chapter 5, Fatigue gained and lost): with the live figure a flying boat group searching to 600 nm once a day is rested by the afternoon. The doctrine does not send part of a group. [0115, What it leaves for later steps; 0131]

A side sees: its own orders, their acceptance or refusal, reasons and notices, whole. The enemy sees none of it. [0115]

## Neutralising a base

The end "neutralise a base" and the doctrine at the area's level that serves it. It is run after the hold doctrine and before the cover doctrine, on the same morning (The day's order, below). It forms sub-intents and gives no order. It draws no random number. [0120 items 5 to 8]

**The end.** A neutralise intent names a **target**: a base on the side's map that is not the side's. It names no base of the side's own. It has no date: it stands until withdrawn. It is given to the area. It is a root, stated by the commander with `set_intent` and the kind `neutralise`, and its plan is drafted, made ready and released by the leaves as a hold intent's plan is (Holding a place, above). `set_intent` refuses a target that is not an enemy base on the side's map as "Not an enemy base"; so does `form_intent`. Under it the staff may form a raid task for the same target (Raiding a base, below), a bombard task (Bombarding a base, below) and, since decision 0202, a carrier task for a free carrier force (Carriers serving an order for an end, below), and nothing else. [0120 items 5 and 9; 0202 item 18]

**What it may use.** An intent given to the area may use no air group without a forces limit (The six limits, above); a neutralise intent is the one exception. With no forces limit on it or above it, it may use every base of the side for the forming of raid tasks, and each raid task may then use the air groups at its base. A forces limit that names air groups narrows it, as any does: a base qualifies (below) only where a group the limit names is, and only the groups it names count there. [0120 item 7]

**Each morning, for each neutralise intent in force whose plan is released,** in priority order, the staff reads each base of the side, in base number order, that the intent may use, with the distance from the base's hex to the target's, in nautical miles by air, and the groups the intent may use at it. A group counts whether it is held or not, as the hold doctrine counts one. A **bomber** here is a group that is not a fighter and whose type carries a weapon; its **reach** is its type's strike radius, the distance it can fly on normal reach. A base **qualifies** if it has a bomber whose reach is at least the distance. Then:

1. **No base qualifies.** Where no carrier force serves it either (Carriers serving an order for an end, below), the staff forms nothing and hands in a decision to give no order whose reason gives, for each base, its distance and the reach of each bomber type it has (or that it has no bomber group it may use), and, where the side has a carrier force, what each was (taken by another order, not free, or with no station), with a `cannot_meet` notice resting on it. It does this every morning on which no base qualifies. The standing interrupt `cannot_meet` fires on it (Interrupts, above).
2. **A base qualifies where a raid stands.** Where a raid task from that base at that target is in force under the intent, whoever made it, the staff forms no other. It hands in a decision to give no order whose reason names the raid that stands. It does this every morning after the first on which the raid has been formed.
3. **A base qualifies and no raid stands.** The staff forms one: a `form_intent` command (parent the neutralise intent, kind raid, the base, the target), with a reason that gives the distance and the bombers that reach. It passes intake as any staff command does. The raid doctrine runs for the new sub-intent on the same morning, on the state as the formation will leave it, so its orders are handed in the same morning and name the new intent.

Where the target is not an enemy base on the side's map when the staff looks (it has been taken, or the picture no longer shows it), the staff forms nothing, hands in a decision to give no order that says so, and no notice. A raid task the staff has formed stands until it is withdrawn with the intent above it: the staff does not withdraw it. [0120 items 6 and 7]

**Its measure.** A neutralise intent is met on a day on which no base of the side was raided from the air. The measure is worked by the agent interface from the side's own view and nothing else, stored nowhere (`sim_agent::neutralise_measure`). It gives:

| Part | What |
| --- | --- |
| Days in force | The days from the day the intent was set to the last day resolved, both counted; or, for an intent withdrawn, to the day before it was withdrawn. 0 where the intent was set after the last day resolved |
| Raids flown | The distinct pairs of a day and a pulse, in the days in force, in which a group of the side launched a base strike at the target (from the side's own `BaseStrikeLaunched` events). Two groups launching in one pulse are one raid |
| Claimed on the ground | The aircraft the side claims to have destroyed on the ground, from the air, at the target's hex, in the days in force (the side's `ClaimReceived` events). It is a claim, as the briefing's are: never below what happened and never corrected. Where the target has changed hands, the hex is the side's own base's |
| Raids on its own bases | The air raids on the side's bases in the days in force, summed over every base of the side (`air_raids_on`). A side cannot tell where a raid on it came from, so they all count |
| Days met | Of the days in force, those on which none of the side's bases was raided from the air |

It is shown in `command_state` (as `measure` on the neutralise intent) and in `report` (as `neutralisations`), with words. It changes nothing and no rule reads it. [0120 item 8]

**A proposal.** `propose` for a neutralise intent runs the air doctrine for the tree of its root as if its plan were released (Holding a place, A proposal). [0120]

A side sees: its own `StaffReason`, `StaffNotice`, `IntentFormed`, the `CommandTaken` of each staff command and the measure, whole. The enemy sees none of it. [0120]

## Raiding a base

The task "raid a base" and the doctrine at the base's level that serves it. It is run last in the staff's morning, after the cover doctrine (The day's order, below). It draws no random number. A raid is launched, escorted, met and resolved by chapter 5 as it stands: this doctrine only sets the orders of the groups. [0120 items 9 to 16]

**The task.** A raid intent names a base of the side and a target (a base on the side's map that is not the side's). It has no date. It is given to the base. The staff forms it under a neutralise intent naming the same target, and a commander may state it himself by `set_intent` with the kind `raid`, when it is a root with a plan of its own, made ready and released by the leaves. Where it is the staff's, it belongs to its root's plan. A raid task is a sub-intent, so its limits narrow as the tree says (The intent tree, above). [0120 item 9]

**The groups it serves.** Each morning, for each raid intent in force whose plan is released, in priority order, the staff reads the groups that:

- the raid may use (The six limits, above): named in every forces limit on its chain, or, with none, the side's groups at its base;
- are at the raid's base;
- are not held as a whole (Holds and the override, above);
- are not taken by a raid served earlier the same morning; and
- are the raid's by rule 16, below.

They are taken in group number order. A group is a **bomber** if it is not a fighter group, its type carries a weapon, and it can reach the target on normal reach (its type's strike radius at least the distance from the base). A group is a **fighter group** and has a **fatigue** and a number of aircraft **able to fly**, as the cover doctrine reads them (Covering a base from the air, above). [0120 item 10]

**The risk level** is the raid's risk limit read up the tree, balanced where none is set. It gives five figures:

| Set by the risk level | Preserve the force | Balanced | Press |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A group rests above a fatigue of | 30 | 45 | 60 |
| Share of its bombers a group holds back | A third | None | None |
| The least a raid sends | 12 | 6 | 1 |
| A raid without an escort | Does not go | Goes | Goes |
| A group stands down after losing | A tenth of what it sent | A quarter | Never |

The fatigue levels are the cover doctrine's. The first two rows are decision 0113's; the last three are placeholders that rest on no row. [0120 item 14 and the table]

1. **Which bombers go.** A bomber group goes unless its fatigue in the morning is above the level its risk gives (a fatigue equal to it goes), or it stands down by rule 3. [0120 item 11]
2. **Whether the raid goes.** What a group can send is the aircraft it is able to fly less its held share, cut to a whole aircraft: the held share is the one the risk gives this morning, whatever the group has set now. The raid goes on a morning when the bombers that go can send, between them, at least the least weight the risk asks, and more than none, and, at preserve the force, when the escorts the fighters at the base can give this morning are above 0. A fighter the raid has gives every aircraft it is able to fly; a fighter of the base's cover gives what the patrol share the cover wants this morning leaves of the aircraft it is able to fly, cut to a whole aircraft, and none where the cover rests it. At balanced and press the raid goes without escort. Where the raid does not go, every bomber rests. [0120 item 12]
3. **What the last raid cost.** A bomber group stands down for a morning when its last base strike launch was on the day before, and the aircraft it lost in the pulse of that launch, shot down or ditched (the side's own `AircraftLost` and `AircraftDitched` events of that pulse), come to at least the share of the aircraft it sent that the risk gives (a tenth, a quarter). A group that lost less, or that flew its last raid on any earlier day, does not stand down; at press none does. A group stands down on the morning right after that raid only. [0120 item 13]
4. **The orders.** A bomber is set to the strike mission at the target (`BaseStrike`) if it goes and the raid goes, and to rest otherwise. Its held share is set to the share the risk gives, if the group has another. A fighter group the raid has is set to the combat air patrol mission with a patrol share of 0, so that every aircraft it can fly is an escort; it is given no rest order and flies only on a morning a raid goes. A fighter group of the base's cover is given no order. Any other group is given no order, and the reason says so. An order is given only where the group's mission or setting differs from what is wanted, so a morning on which nothing changed has no order for a group. Orders are handed in in group number order, a mission before a setting. [0120 item 10; 0114]
5. **When it cannot.** On a morning when no bomber group the raid may use and is not held can reach the target, or, at preserve the force, the base has no fighter group it may use or draw escorts from (a fighter of the cover counts, though the cover rests it), the staff hands in a decision to give no order whose reason says it cannot meet the intent and why, with a `cannot_meet` notice resting on it. A raid that is not sent for want of weight, fatigue or escort is no failure: its bombers rest and the reason says why. [0120 item 15]
6. **Reasons.** An order's reason says the group, its kind, its fatigue against the level, what the group has and what is wanted, the weight the bombers that go can send against what the risk asks, and the escorts the fighters can give. A decision to give no order is handed in on a morning on which a group got no order, or the raid cannot be met, or the raid has no group at the base that the staff may order: it lists those groups and says whether the raid goes. [0120]

**Whose a group is (rule 16).** A cover and a raid task may both be in force at one base and both may use a group. A group has one owner, decided by its kind and not by the rank of the intents, so that a base both defended and raiding does both:

- a fighter group is the cover's where a cover of its base that may use it is in force, and the raid draws the escorts the cover's patrol share leaves (rule 2); with no such cover it is the raid's and the raid sets it (rule 4);
- a group that can bomb the target is the raid's, though it can also search: the cover leaves it out (Covering a base from the air, A raid's bomber). A bomber a strike-ships task in force may use is that task's where the task ranks above the raid, and the raid leaves it out (Striking ships from a base, above);
- any other group is the cover's if a cover that may use it is in force (it may search), and otherwise no one's;
- rank decides only between two tasks of the same kind that may use one group: a group serves one cover, and one raid, the first in priority order.

[0120 item 16, amending 0115, 7]

A side sees: its own orders, their acceptance or refusal, reasons and notices, whole. The enemy sees none of it. [0120]

## Denying an area

The end "deny an area" and the doctrine at the area's level that serves it. It is run after the neutralise doctrine and before the cover doctrine, on the same morning (The day's order, below). It forms sub-intents and gives no order to a unit. It draws no random number. [0123 items 1 to 3]

**The end.** A deny intent names a hex and a distance in whole hexes. It names no base. It has no date: it stands until withdrawn. Its code is 6. It is given to the area. It is a root, stated by the commander with `set_intent` and the kind `deny`, and its plan is drafted, made ready and released by the leaves as a hold intent's plan is (Holding a place, above). `set_intent` refuses a hex that is not on the map as "Not on the map"; so does `form_intent`. Under it the staff may form the tasks "strike ships from a base" and "search waters from a base" and, since decisions 0124 and 0125, a stand task or a carrier task for each task force its forces limit names (A task force as an addressee, below), and, since decision 0202, where its forces limit names no task force, a carrier task for a free carrier force at a station the staff chooses (Carriers serving an order for an end, below), and nothing else. [0123 items 1 and 11; 0124 item 8; 0202 item 1]

**The waters** of a deny intent are the hexes on the map within the distance of its hex, by hex distance, in hex order. A hex off the map is no part of them. With a distance of 0 the waters are the hex alone. [0123 item 1]

**What it may use.** As the neutralise intent: with no forces limit on it or above it, every base of the side and the air groups at each; a forces limit that names air groups narrows it to the bases where a group it names is, and only the groups named count there. A forces limit that names task forces and no air group leaves it no base at all: only the named forces serve it. That oddity stays, known (decision 0202, What it does not do). [0123 item 3; 0202]

**Each morning, for each deny intent in force whose plan is released,** in priority order, and for each base of the side that the intent may use, in base number order, the staff reads the bomber groups and the groups that can search that the intent may use at the base:

- A **bomber** is a group that is not a fighter and whose type carries a weapon. Its **reach** is its type's strike radius, on normal reach.
- A group that can **search** is a group that is not a fighter and whose type has a search range above 0. Its **range** is that search range.
- A reach or a range **touches** the waters when, from the base's hex, a hex of the waters is within it by the engine's own test of an air distance (chapter 5, Air distance).

Then, for the base:

1. **Striking ships.** Where a bomber whose reach touches the waters is there, and no strike-ships task from that base is in force under the intent, whoever made it, the staff forms one: a `form_intent` command (parent the deny intent, kind strike ships, the base), with a reason that gives the base's distance from the nearest hex of the waters and the bombers that touch them. Where one stands, the staff forms no other and hands in a decision to give no order that names it.
2. **Searching the waters.** Where a group that can search whose range touches the waters is there, and no cover of that base is in force (under any intent), and no search-waters task from that base is in force under the intent, the staff forms one in the same way. Where a cover of the base is in force the staff forms none: the cover's arc takes the waters as threats (Covering a base from the air, above), and the staff hands in a decision to give no order that says so. Where a search task stands it forms no other.
3. **No base reaches.** Where, over all the bases, no bomber's reach and no group's range touches the waters, and no carrier force serves it (below), the staff forms nothing and hands in a decision to give no order whose reason gives, for each base, its distance from the nearest hex of the waters and the reach of each bomber type and the range of each group that can search (or that it has none the intent may use), and, where the side has a carrier force, what each was, with a `cannot_meet` notice resting on it. It does this every morning on which no base reaches. The standing interrupt `cannot_meet` fires on it (Interrupts, above).

After the bases, the staff forms the tasks of the task forces the intent's forces limit names, at the centre of the waters (A task force as an addressee, below). A task formed for a force, or one that stands, counts as serving the intent, and so does the carrier task of a free carrier force (Carriers serving an order for an end, below), so the notice of case 3 is not handed in on a morning when one does. The new sub-intent's orders are handed in on the same morning, as for a raid (Neutralising a base, above). A task the staff has formed stands until it is withdrawn with the intent above it: the staff does not withdraw it. [0123 items 2 and 3; 0124 item 8]

**The waters first** (decision 0190). A commander may set a targets limit with `waters_first` true on a deny intent or an intent beneath one (The limits, above). The staff then orders the bombers of the deny's tasks to strike a contact in the waters before any outside them, as Striking ships from a base, What to strike first, below, says; a carrier force that the deny's forces limit names has the waters added to its search arc and closes on a contact in them (Operating a carrier force, below). A deny with no such limit does none of this: its waters are as they were. [0190 items 14, 27 to 29]

**Its measure.** A deny intent is met on a day that ends with no contact of the enemy held in its waters. The measure is worked by the agent interface from the side's own view and nothing else, stored nowhere (`sim_agent::deny_measure`), as a neutralise intent's is. It gives:

| Part | What |
| --- | --- |
| Days in force | The days from the day the intent was set to the last day resolved, both counted; or, for an intent withdrawn, to the day before it was withdrawn |
| Contacts held | The distinct contacts the side held in the waters (hexes within the distance of the hex, by hex distance) at the end of a day in force. A contact is held from the report that made or moved it to the one that dropped it |
| Strikes launched | The `StrikeLaunched` events of the side's own groups in the days in force that are now at a base which was given a strike-ships task under this intent, on a day that task stood, or now aboard a ship of a task force given a carrier task under it, on a day that task stood (decision 0202, 22). They count wherever the strike went, not only at the waters: a base given the task strikes what its groups reach. A group rebased since counts for the base it is at now |
| Claimed sunk, claimed hits | The ships the side claims to have sunk, and the hits it claims, from the air (`ClaimReceived`, from the air) at a hex of the waters in the days in force. A claim is what the side's people believe: never below what happened and never corrected |
| Days met | Of the days in force, those that ended with no contact held in the waters |

It is shown in `command_state` (as `measure` on the deny intent) and in `report` (as `denials`), with words. It changes nothing and no rule reads it. [0123 item 4]

**A proposal.** `propose` for a deny intent runs the air doctrine for the tree of its root as if its plan were released (Holding a place, A proposal). [0123]

A side sees: its own `StaffReason`, `StaffNotice`, `IntentFormed`, the `CommandTaken` of each staff command and the measure, whole. The enemy sees none of it. [0123]

## Striking ships from a base

The task "strike ships from a base" and the doctrine at the base's level that serves it. It is run after the raid doctrine, on the same morning (The day's order, below). It gives orders to air groups and draws no random number. A strike is launched, sized, met and resolved by chapter 5 as it stands: this doctrine only sets the standing orders of the groups. [0123 items 5 to 9]

**The task.** A strike-ships intent names a base of the side. Its code is 7. It has no date. It is given to the base. The staff forms it under a deny intent (the engine also lets one stand under a hold intent for the same base, but no doctrine forms it there); a commander may state it himself by `set_intent` with the kind `strike_ships`, when it is a root with a plan of its own, made ready and released by the leaves. A task the staff formed belongs to its root's plan. Its limits narrow as the tree says (The intent tree, above). [0123 items 5 and 11]

**The groups it serves.** Each morning, for each strike-ships intent in force whose plan is released, in priority order, the staff reads the groups that the task may use (The six limits, above), that are at its base and that are not held as a whole (Holds and the override, above), in group number order. A group is left out that a raid served earlier the same morning has, or a strike-ships task served earlier. Each group is one of:

- a **bomber**: not a fighter, and its type carries a weapon. It is the task's, unless a raid task in force may use it as a bomber and ranks above this task, in which case the raid has it (Raiding a base, above). A cover leaves a strike-ships task's bomber out as it leaves a raid's;
- a **fighter group**: it is the base's cover's where a cover of the base is in force, and the task gives it no order; with no cover it is the task's;
- **any other group**: no order.

A task ranks above another by the rank read up the tree, 1 first, then the unranked, each by number. [0123 items 7 and 8]

**The risk level** is the task's risk limit read up the tree, balanced where none is set. It gives, for a bomber:

| Set by the risk level | Preserve the force | Balanced | Press |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A bomber rests above a fatigue of | 30 | 45 | 60 |
| Oldest contact it launches on, in pulses | The figure `strike_contact_max_age_pulses` | The figure | The figure and two more, to 4 at most |
| Its target list | Carrier, battleship, cruiser | Every kind | Every kind (where a targets limit binds the task, its kinds at every risk: What to strike first, below) |
| Its reach | Normal | Normal | Extended |
| Share it holds back | A third | None | None |

The rows are decision 0113's table, as the raid doctrine takes its fatigue levels and held share. [0123 item 7]

**The orders.** A bomber is set to the naval strike mission, or to rest when its fatigue in the morning is above the level its risk gives (equal goes). Its oldest contact, target list (the targets limit's kinds where one binds, What to strike first, below), waters to strike first (those of the denies that bind it, and none where none does), reach and held share are set to what the risk gives, and its strike sizing is set on (Strike sizing, below). A fighter group the task has, with no cover at the base, is set to the combat air patrol with a patrol share of a half, since a base that strikes will be struck. A fighter of the base's cover and any other group are given no order. An order is given only where the group's mission or setting differs from what is wanted, so a morning on which nothing changed has no order. Orders are handed in in group number order, a mission before the settings in the order above. A decision to give no order is handed in on a morning on which a group got none, or the task has no group the staff may order, and lists those groups. [0123 items 7 and 8]

**What to strike first** (decision 0190). Two things the commander's targets limit gives a bomber, in place of what the risk gives or beside it, both set by orders handed in with the others and neither holding the group.

1. **The list.** Where a targets limit binds the task, read up its chain (the nearest set), each bomber's target list is set to the limit's kinds in the limit's order, at every risk, in place of the list the risk gives. A kind the limit leaves out is not launched at. This task and a carrier task (Operating a carrier force, below) are the two that a targets limit binds. For a carrier force with no targets limit on its own chain, the lowest-numbered deny in force whose plan is released, whose nearest forces limit names the force and whose chain carries a targets limit, gives it. [0190 items 3 and 28]
2. **The waters.** A deny binds the bombers of a task when its plan is released and the targets limit read for it has `waters_first` true: a deny above the task (the nearest targets limit on the task's chain decides), and, for a carrier force, a deny whose forces limit names the force, under whatever task the force has. Each morning, for every bomber the staff may order under the task, it works the waters of all the denies that bind the group, their hexes together, and orders the group's waters to strike first (chapter 5, Air group orders) where they differ from what the group has. The empty set is among them: a deny withdrawn, or its limit changed or cleared, clears the waters the next morning. The order comes after the target list and before the reach. A group the staff may no longer order keeps the waters it has, as it keeps a target list: that is a known limit. [0190 items 14, 27, 28 and 33]

The reason of an order that sets the list names the targets limit and the intent it is set on; the reason of an order that sets the waters names the denies and the limit, and says a contact in them is struck before every one outside. The waters order is handed in under the task the group serves, which the engine's forces check reads, and its reason serves the lowest-numbered deny that binds the group, with that deny's plan; an order that clears the waters serves the task (the architect's ruling on decision 0190, rule 33; `docs/rules/differences.md`). With no targets limit the staff sets the list the risk gives and no waters, and no order or reason differs from before. [0190 items 6, 33; the architect's ruling]

**Strike sizing (rule 9).** Every bomber of the task has its strike sizing set on, whatever the risk. A group under a task to strike what comes within reach has more to hit than one force, so its strikes are sized by doctrine (chapter 5, A strike sized to its target), which sends everything at a kind it gives no figure for. The reason of this order names the air group as the level that decided it (A doctrine belongs to a level, below). The task never sets sizing off. [0123 item 9]

**Reasons.** An order's reason gives the group, its kind, its fatigue against the level, what it has and what is wanted. [0123]

A side sees: its own orders, their acceptance or refusal, reasons and notices, whole. The enemy sees none of it. [0123]

## Searching waters

The task "search waters from a base" and the doctrine at the base's level that serves it. It is run last in the staff's morning (The day's order, below). It gives orders to air groups and draws no random number. [0123 items 11 to 13]

**The task.** A search-waters intent names a base of the side. Its code is 8. It has no date. It is given to the base. It stands under a deny intent only, and the staff alone forms it: a commander cannot state it, and `set_intent` refuses it as "Not a root". Where a cover of the base is in force the staff forms none (Denying an area, above). [0123 items 11 and 13]

**The groups it serves.** Each morning, for each search-waters intent in force whose plan is released, in priority order, the staff reads the groups the task may use that are at its base and not held as a whole, in group number order. A group that can search (not a fighter, its type's search range above 0) and that no strike-ships task in force may use as a bomber is the task's. A group that is a bomber of a strike-ships task is left to it. A fighter, and a group that cannot search, are given no order. [0123 item 12]

**The orders.** The risk level is the task's risk limit read up the tree, balanced where none is set. A group of the task rests when its fatigue in the morning is above the level the risk gives (30, 45, 60), with no exception for a search group; otherwise it is set to the search mission, and to search once a day at preserve the force and at balanced, in both daylight pulses at press. Its search arc is set to the arc the waters give, at its type's search range. Where no group of the task can only search, a strike or raid task at the base with two or more bomber groups that can search and fly lends it the least tired, as it lends a cover (Covering a base from the air, A bomber that takes a turn at the search). The arc is that of decision 0115, item 9, from the base's hex, with the hexes of the waters of the deny intent above the task as the threats: the narrowest arc, in steps of ten degrees, that holds every hex of the waters by the engine's own test of an arc, the lowest start where two are as narrow, widened by 30 degrees at each end, and the whole circle where that comes to 360 or more. An order is given only where the group's mission, setting or arc differs, in that order. A decision to give no order is handed in as for a strike task. [0123 item 12; 0131 items 6 and 8]

**The cover's arc.** Where a cover of the base is in force, the cover's search groups search an arc whose threats are its own and, beside them, the waters of every deny intent in force whose plan is released and whose waters a range of the cover's search groups at the base touches. A deny intent whose waters none of those groups reaches adds nothing. The base's searchers have one arc, the cover's (Covering a base from the air, above). [0123 item 13]

A side sees: its own orders, their acceptance or refusal, reasons and notices, whole. The enemy sees none of it. [0123]

## A task force as an addressee

The task force is a level that an intent is addressed to, and three kinds of task are given to it: standing at a hex by night, operating a carrier force, and bombarding a base (below; the third is formed under neutralise and take a place, Bombarding a base). The staff forms them, at the level above, from the forces limit. This section is the rules of being addressed and of forming; each task's own doctrine follows. It draws no random number. [0124 items 6 to 8; 0125 items 1 and 2]

**What it commands.** An intent addressed to a task force of the side may use that force's ships and nothing else, and the air groups aboard those ships where every forces limit on its chain names the group or has the force inside it. The addressee has code 4 and holds the force's number. Two things follow from it:

- A staff's order to the force is inside the forces limit of the intent it serves, whatever limits stand above the intent (The forces limit on a staff's orders, above).
- A commander's order to a force an intent in force is addressed to holds it, as an order to a force in a forces limit does (Holds and the override, above).

**Forming.** Under a deny or a hold intent in force whose plan is released (and, for the carrier task alone, under a "take a place", the third parent, decision 0148), the doctrine of that level forms a task for each task force named in the nearest forces limit on the intent's chain (its own, for a root), in task force number order, where no task of that kind for that force is in force anywhere on the side, whoever made it and wherever it stands in the side's trees (a commander's own task stated as a root counts):

- a **stand task** for a force on the surface combat mission with a ship afloat, at the end's hex: the centre of the waters under a deny intent, the base's hex under a hold intent;
- a **carrier task** for a force on the carrier mission with a carrier afloat that has an air group aboard, at the hex the force is in that morning under a deny or a hold intent. The staff does not choose where a force named in the forces limit stands under those two. A free carrier force it takes for a commander's deny or neutralise whose forces limit names no task force it does station (Carriers serving an order for an end, below). A commander who wants it elsewhere states the task himself, as a root, or moves the force. Under a "take a place" the staff does choose, and forms it at the covering hex (Operating a carrier force, Under take a place, below), on the morning the plan is released or, to look at the place, while it is preparing (item 25 there).

With no task force named by the commander in a forces limit, no such task is formed. A force on any other mission is given no task. The staff hands in a `form_intent` command and a reason for each task it forms; where a task stands it hands in a decision to give no order that names the task, who made it and where it stands, and forms no other. So a force has one task of each kind, and no group is ordered twice for one force. Under a deny intent this runs after the bases' tasks, and a task formed or standing counts as serving the end; under a hold intent it runs first. A task formed is a sub-intent as any is (The intent tree, above), and a commander may state either kind himself as a root: it then needs a plan of its own, made ready and released by the leaves. [0124 items 6 to 8; 0125 items 1 and 2; the architect's ruling of Nov 16, 2026, made on the loading check of the second proof (decision 0128)]

A side sees: its own `IntentFormed`, `StaffReason` and the `CommandTaken` of the command, whole. The enemy sees none of it. [0124]

## Standing at a hex by night

The task "stand at a hex" and the doctrine at the task force's level that serves it. It is run after the search doctrine, before the carrier doctrine (The day's order, below). It gives orders to a task force and draws no random number. The engine's half is one rule of movement, the night stand (chapter 2, Halting at a marked waypoint and steaming on). [0124 items 1 to 5 and 9 to 15; 0192]

**The task.** A stand-at intent names a task force of the side and a hex, the **station**. Its code is 9. It has no date. It is given to the force (A task force as an addressee, above). It may stand under a deny intent where the station is the centre of the waters, and under a hold intent where the station is the base's hex; a commander may state it himself as a root. [0124 item 7]

**The force's morning.** Each morning, for each stand task in force whose plan is released, in priority order, and whose force is not held (a force held, or with a ship held, is given no order), the staff decides for the force, at its level, from the side's view:

1. **The threat.** There is a threat when the side holds a contact of ships within 15 hexes of the station, counted in whole hexes from the contact's estimated hex. At preserve the force, only a contact that reports no more ships than the force has afloat counts. The nearest that counts is named, the lowest number where two tie; at preserve the force a nearer contact that does not count is named with its ships against the force's.
2. **Whether it sorties tonight.** At press, every night. At balanced and at preserve the force, on a day with a threat.
3. **The speed and the route.** The force must be stopped at its **home port**: not under way, not halted and on no leg, at its own home port where it has one, else at a base of the side, which an order that morning sets as its home port. A force with a home port that is not stopped there is **away from home**: it is given no order, and the reason says where it is and when it is expected; but one stopped at sea, at no base of the side, with no waypoint left on its route, is routed home at cruise, one way, with no mark, and the reason gives when the engine's measure has it there (decision 0192). A force with no home port that is not stopped at a base of the side stands from where it is (A force with no home port, below). A force stopped at its home port is routed to the station and home again, the station marked to stand at. The engine's own measure of the passage out (the passage forecast of chapter 2) is worked at cruising speed and then at full speed, and the first that brings the force to the station within the night's 12 hours is the speed ordered. Where neither does, there is no sortie, and the notice that the intent cannot be met is handed in. Where the station is the force's own hex, it is given no route.
4. **Fuel comes first.** The fuel the sortie needs is, for each ship, the engine's measure of the route to the station and home with no mark at that speed, times the margin the risk gives (table). A force with a ship that has less aboard, or that the engine's measure has run dry short of the route (the measure gives no more burnt than is aboard), does not sortie [0130 item 4]. Its reason gives the two figures and the day from which the force may be used: the day after it will have fuelled where it lies (the hours fuelling takes there by chapter 2, Refuelling in port: the longest ship's lack of a full load at the berth rate where the port has a berth, else its lighter rate), the day counted from the start of the day and never today, or no day where the base has less fuel stock than the ships lack of their need or gives no rate, and then the notice that the intent cannot be met. The notice is handed in on each morning that holds. Ammunition is read in the same pass (Ammunition as policy, below): a force short of it does not sortie either, and the day it may be used is the later of the two.
5. **Readiness and rules of engagement.** On a day it sorties, or stands at its own hex on a day it would sortie, the force is set to battle readiness; on a day it does not, to normal. Its rules of engagement are set once, from the risk: the stance engage and the break-off level of the table; the straggler instruction and the keep share are left as they are.
6. **The order of the orders** is home port, rules of engagement, readiness, speed, route, each only where it differs from what the force has. A reason with no order is handed in on each morning the force is not routed; a morning with an order has its reasons on the orders.
7. **Reasons** carry the contact and its distance, the fuel aboard against the fuel needed, the hours to the station and the level, written "decided at the level of task_force:N".

The risk is the task's risk limit read up the tree, balanced where none is set.

| Set by the risk level | Preserve the force | Balanced | Press |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| It sorties | On a threat it is not outnumbered by | On a threat | Every night |
| Fuel needed, of the passage out and home | One and a half times | One and a fifth | Once |
| Break-off level | A quarter | A half | Three quarters |

The fuel and break-off rows are decision 0113's. The 15 hexes are a placeholder: a day's steaming at 25 knots. The force steams for home at the morning and is at sea, within reach of the enemy's aircraft, for as long as the passage takes. [0124 items 9 to 15]

**A force with no home port.** A stand's force that has no home port and is not stopped at a base of the side is no longer away from home: it stands from where it is (decision 0192, as amended on review). A home port that is no longer the side's counts as none, and the reasons say so; the engine clears a home port taken by the enemy (chapter 7, A base that changes side), so in play this is a force whose home port was cleared. Each morning:

1. **Preserve the force keeps clear.** At preserve the force, while a contact of ships that reports more ships than the force has afloat is within 15 hexes of the station, the force is given nothing, not even readiness or rules of engagement; the reason names the nearest such contact (the lowest number on a tie) and the two counts. A force already under way to its station is not stopped by it.
2. **Under way, it is left alone.** A force under way whose route ends at the station is given no route.
3. **Going to the station.** A force not at the station and not under way to it is routed to the station, one way, with no mark, on every risk. The speed is cruise where the engine's measure of the passage at cruise arrives within the night's 12 hours, and otherwise full, whether or not the passage at full speed arrives; it goes though the night is not enough, since it has no port to wait in. Where ships cannot get from where it is to the station by sea, it is given no route and the notice that the intent cannot be met is handed in.
4. **Fuel and ammunition, in one pass.** Its fuel is weighed to the **base it goes in to**: the nearest base of the side by the engine's sea route from the station that holds fuel above its reserve and can fuel a ship (its port fuels at a rate given, or with no limit where none is given, chapter 2, Port size), the lower number on a tie. The fuel each ship needs is the engine's measure of the route to the station and on to that base, at the speed chosen, with no mark, times the risk's margin (the table above). A force with a ship short of it, or that the measure runs dry, or that is short of ammunition (Ammunition as policy), does not go; the reason gives the figures, and no notice is handed in. With no such base the fuel is not weighed, and the reason says that no base of the side holds fuel above its reserve and can fuel it.
5. **At the station it lies stopped.** It is given no route, and lies there by day and night: it burns no fuel there, and is open to the enemy's aircraft by day and fights any force that enters its hex.
6. **Going in.** At the station, where a ship has less than the engine's measure of the passage from the station to the base it goes in to, at cruise, times the risk's margin (or the measure runs it dry), or the force is short of ammunition, the force is routed to that base at cruise, one way, with no mark, and the base is set as its home port by order. From then on it is a force with a home port, and stands as above. The reason names each short ship. With no such base it stays.
7. **Readiness and rules of engagement.** The test of a sortie (item 2 above) decides its readiness, not whether it goes: battle readiness on a day it would sortie, normal on one it would not. Its rules of engagement are set from the risk as above.
8. **The order of the orders** is home port, rules of engagement, readiness, speed, route, each only where it differs from what the force has; a morning with no route has a reason with no order. Every reason says the force has no home port and stands from where it is.

A force the commander holds is given nothing, as above. Nothing here changes a force with a home port, but the one stopped at sea that is routed home. The branch adds no state, no event and no draw. [0192]

**Radio silence.** At preserve the force, and where report figures are loaded, the doctrine of the task force sets the force to radio silence, by the order `set_radio_silence`, on a morning it is not set already (chapter 1, Commands and their pictures, Radio silence). At balanced and at press it leaves the setting as it finds it, and it never turns silence off; a commander does. The reason says the force is not on silence and that reports travel, and that silence buys nothing yet because nothing listens to radio traffic (decision 0136, rule 19). The order is not given where the force is held. [0136 items 18 to 20]

A side sees: its own orders, their acceptance or refusal, reasons and notices, whole, with the force's `TaskForceHalted` and `TaskForceSteamedOn`. The enemy sees none of it. [0124]

## Ammunition as policy

The staff's use of rearming (chapter 2, Rearming in port; decision 0122). It adds no rule of the engine and no intent: ammunition is policy, as fuel is. It is read by the stand doctrine and by the choice of a free escort, and it gives no order of its own; it gives orders no more than a force that is short of fuel does. It draws no random number. [0127]

**Short of ammunition.** A ship is short when its rounds aboard are below the share of its outfit its risk gives, or its fired tubes are more than the share of its tubes its risk gives. The risk is the task's, read up the tree, balanced where none is set. A ship whose class carries the marker for ammunition that is not limiting (chapter 2) is never short of rounds. A force is short when any ship of it is. [0127 item 1]

| Set by the risk level | Preserve the force | Balanced | Press |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A ship is short of rounds below | Three quarters of its outfit | A half | A quarter |
| A ship is short of torpedoes with more fired than | None | A half of its tubes (whole tubes, cut) | Never |

The shares are placeholders that rest on no row. A ship is short of rounds only when its rounds are below the level, not at it. [0127]

**What a short force does not do.** A force that is short does not sortie (Standing at a hex by night, rule 4) and is not a free escort (Escorting and covering a run, A free escort). Its reason gives each short ship, its rounds against the level and her tubes fired against the tubes allowed. A force whose station is its own hex is given no route and is not asked: the check is made for a force that would sortie. [0127 item 2]

**The day it may be used.** A short force stopped at a base of its side that holds ammunition and gives a rate for it is left there, and rearms by chapter 2. Its reason gives the day from which it may be used: the day after the last of its short ships will be no longer short, counted from the start of the day and never today, by the engine's own measure of the hours rearming takes there (the force line's `ammunition_lacked`, chapter 2, What the tools say): the berths as the queue would give them, a ship's fuel first, then the tons it must take to be short no longer over its ammunition rate, the longest ship's. The tons it must take are the rounds to the level, rounded up to a whole round, and for a ship short of torpedoes the whole of the rounds she lacks, because rounds are taken first, plus the torpedoes over the number allowed. Cargo is not counted, though a ship's cargo uses hours before its fuel and its ammunition: the measure is made as the tools make it. [0127 item 3]

**No day.** Where the base the force lies at holds no ammunition, holds less than its short ships need together (tons to be short no longer), or gives no rate for ammunition to a ship that needs some, the reason gives no day, and the staff hands in that it cannot meet the intent (the cannot-meet notice, on each morning that holds). It does not send the force to another base: moving a force's home is the commander's. [0127 item 4]

**Read with fuel.** Fuel and ammunition are read in one pass. A force sorties when it is neither short of fuel (Standing at a hex by night, rule 4) nor short of ammunition. A force short of both gives one reason with both, and the day it may be used is the later of the two days; if either has no day, it has none, and the notice is handed in. A force short of ammunition only gives a reason that says so and takes no order for the night. [0127 item 5]

The doctrine's version, `supply-13`, is since this section. Shortness reads the ships alone: it does not ask whether the base holds ammunition or the scenario gives weights and rates. A force whose ships are short, at a base that cannot rearm them, therefore does not sortie and has no day, and the staff hands in that it cannot meet the intent, as a force short of fuel at a base with none does. A force whose ships are full of ammunition is never short, so a game in which no ship has fired and none starts short is the game it was. [0127]

A side sees: its own orders, reasons and notices, whole, as for any doctrine. The enemy sees none of it. [0127]

## Operating a carrier force

The task "operate a carrier force from a hex" and the doctrine at the task force's level that serves it. It is run last in the staff's morning (The day's order, below). It gives orders to a task force and to the air groups aboard its ships, reuses the cover, strike and search pieces of the base's doctrines, and draws no random number. It adds no rule of the engine. [0125 items 1 to 11]

**The task.** An operate-carriers intent names a task force of the side and a hex. Its code is 10. It has no date. It is given to the force. It may use the force's ships and the air groups aboard them. It may stand under a deny intent (one under "control an island" among them, decision 0151), under a hold intent and, since decision 0148, under a "take a place", and a commander may state it himself as a root. [0125 item 1; 0148 item 1; 0151 item 6]

**The groups.** Each morning, for each operate-carriers task in force whose plan is released, in priority order, and whose force is not held, the staff decides for the air groups aboard the force's ships that are not held, in group number order. The risk is the task's risk limit read up the tree, balanced where none is set.

1. **Fighters fly the patrol,** with the patrol share the risk gives (three quarters, a half, a quarter of the aircraft), raised by a quarter and not above 1 on a morning when the force was attacked from the air on any of the three days before (read from the side's own events: an air attack on a ship of the force). The rest of a fighter group are its escorts.
2. **Who searches.** A group that can search (not a fighter, its type has a search range) and cannot attack a ship (its type carries no weapon) searches. Where the force has none, the least tired of the groups that can do both and can fly, then the lowest numbered, searches, chosen each morning, and the others strike; where none of them can fly, the lowest numbered of them (decision 0131, 7, amending decision 0125, 4). A group that searched yesterday and strikes today is given the missions in turn by orders. The reason names each such group's fatigue and the aircraft it can fly. A searching group is set to search once a day at preserve the force alone, and in both daylight pulses at balanced and at press (decision 0131, 6, as amended on Nov 16, 2026: a carrier force searched in the afternoon as well as the morning), the setting ordered after the mission and before the arc, and only where the group's setting differs; the reason says which applied ("at balanced risk a carrier force searches in both daylight pulses"). A search is flown at the type's search range over the arc of the cover's rule (Covering a base from the air, below), taken from the force's hex, with these threats: the enemy's bases on the side's map, the contacts the side holds, and, under a deny intent, the hexes of its waters; and, for a task whose station the staff chose (Carriers serving an order for an end, below) while the force goes there, the way ahead, in the words "the way ahead to the station (q,r) at (q,r)". Where a deny that names the force (its nearest forces limit names it) is not above the force's task and its targets limit has `waters_first` true, its waters are threats too, in the words "the waters to strike first of intent:N at (q,r)" (decision 0190, 29).
3. **The rest strike ships,** by the strike table (Striking ships from a base, above), with strike sizing on: a carrier has more to hit than one force. Where a targets limit binds the force's task, each such bomber's target list is the limit's kinds at every risk, and its waters to strike first are those of the denies that bind it, as Striking ships from a base, What to strike first, says (decision 0190, 3 and 28).
4. **Rest.** A group rests for a day when its fatigue in the morning is above the level the risk gives (30, 45, 60). The last fighter group that can fly is never rested, and neither is the last group that searches; a striking group rests above the level with no exception.
5. **Who is left.** A group that is none of these is given no order.

**The force.** The force's orders come before its groups', in the order readiness, rules of engagement, speed, route, then each group's mission first.

6. **Its place.** Where the force is not at the task's hex and not on its way home, it is routed there, with a speed order first where the speed it has is not the speed of item 11, and given no readiness order. At the hex it is given no route and is set to battle readiness. A force **on its way home** is under way, or halted, with its home port's hex as its last waypoint; it is given no route. A force of a task the staff formed under a commander's deny or neutralise (decision 0202) may be given more than the one point of its hex, and while it is under way on such a route it is given no route and no speed (The way a force takes, below). [0125 item 7; 0130 item 1; 0203 items 23 and 24]
7. **Fuel.** The fuel the force needs is, for each ship, the engine's measure of the passage to the home port at cruise, times the margin the risk gives (the table of Standing at a hex by night). A ship the engine's measure has run dry short of a passage is short of the fuel for it, here, for the passage back and in the sortie of a stand task (Standing at a hex by night): the measure gives no more burnt than is aboard. On a morning a ship is short, the force is routed home at cruise at every risk, with a speed order first where it has another speed and no readiness order, and the reason gives the figures. At home and not at its hex, it fuels (chapter 2, Refuelling in port), and on the first morning every ship has what the passage to the hex and home again needs, at the speed of item 11, with the margin, it is routed back; else it stays. Its home port is the force's own; else the base it is stopped at; with none, the fuel is not weighed and the reason says so. [0125 item 8; 0130 items 1 and 4]
8. **Rules of engagement.** The stance is avoid, since a carrier force does not seek a gun action, and the break-off level is the risk table's.
9. **When it cannot.** On a morning the force has no carrier afloat, or no air group aboard able to fly, the staff hands in that it cannot meet the intent, and gives no order that morning. On a morning a ship of the force has no fuel aboard, or the engine's forecast refuses the passage to the task's hex as out of fuel, the force cannot move: the staff gives it no route and no speed order and does not weigh closing, hands in that it cannot meet the intent with a reason that names the ship out of fuel and the hex where the force lies, and gives the air groups their orders as on any morning (the architect's ruling of Nov 16, 2026, recorded in decision 0131). The stand doctrine has no such hole: it routes a force only on a sortie whose fuel it has measured, and a ship that runs dry is short. [0125 item 10; 0131]
10. **Reasons** carry each group's fatigue against the level, the share and what raised it, the threats and the arc, the fuel aboard against the fuel needed, and the level, "decided at the level of task_force:N".

**Speed and closing the range.** Two rules of the force's commander, after the second proof of stage 2. [0130]

11. **Speed.** A force routed to its hex, to a hex it closes to, or back from its home port to its hex steams at cruise where the engine's measure of the passage at cruise brings it there within the 12 hours of the night, and otherwise at full speed; at preserve the force always at cruise. A speed order is given only where the force has another speed. The fuel for the passage back from home, and for closing (item 14), is measured at the speed chosen. The passage home for fuel is at cruise (item 7). [0130 item 1]
12. **A contact to close on.** On a morning the force is not held, not on its way home and not fuelling at home, and a bomber group aboard (not a fighter, its type carrying a weapon) has an aircraft able to fly, the staff weighs the contacts of ships the side holds. The **reach** is the least strike radius (normal reach) among those bomber groups. A contact is **fresh** when it is no more than 3 pulses old. The kinds closed on are: nothing at preserve the force, a carrier at balanced, any kind at press. A fresh contact whose estimated hex is in the waters to strike first of a deny that binds the force (Striking ships from a base, What to strike first, above) is closed on whatever its kind, at balanced and at press and not at preserve the force (decision 0190, 15). Of the fresh contacts of a kind closed on, or in the waters, that are within a night at full speed (the force's full speed by the engine's measure, times 12 hours) and the reach, by air distance from the force's hex, the staff takes the first: one in the waters before any outside them, then the kind first in the order of importance, then the nearest, then the lowest number. At balanced, then, a force closes on a fresh contact in the waters beyond its reach even where an enemy carrier outside them is within it: that is what the order says. At press a contact in the waters is taken first. With no such deny the waters are empty and the rule is as it was. Where that one is within the reach the force does not close; where it is beyond, the force closes on it. With fresh contacts of a kind closed on and none within a night and the reach, it is too far, and a find may be looked for (items 28 to 41). [0130 item 2; 0214]
13. **Where it goes.** The first hex along the shortest sea route from the force to the contact's hex that is within the reach of the contact: it goes no nearer than its bombers need. It is routed there at the speed of item 11. [0130 item 3]
14. **Fuel first.** It does not close where a ship is short (item 7) of the engine's measure of the passage to that hex and from there to its home port, at the speed chosen, times the risk's margin. With no home port the fuel is not weighed. [0130 item 4]
15. **It stays while something is in reach.** A force that has left its task's hex to close (one not at its hex whose last route ends at a hex that is neither the task's nor its home port's) is given no route on a morning the first contact of item 12 is within the reach, and stays; where that contact is beyond the reach it closes again. On a morning it holds none, in reach or to close on, it is routed back to its task's hex by item 6. [0130 item 5]
16. **Its arc, readiness and reasons.** Its search arc is taken from the hex the force is in that morning. It is set to battle readiness on a morning it closes or stays, as at its hex. The reason of a closing route gives the contact, its kind and age, its distance against the reach and against a night at full speed and the reach, the hex chosen, its distance and the hours to it, and the fuel; on a morning it does not close the reason says why: preserve the force closes on nothing, nothing fresh, nothing of a kind it closes on, already in reach, too far, no sea route, not the fuel, or no bomber group that can fly. These words join the reason it stays on its task where there is one. Where the contact is in the waters the words say so, "in the waters of intent:N that the targets limit set on intent:M puts first". [0130 items 6 to 8; 0190 items 6 and 15]

**Initiative.** One rule reads a commander's rating (chapter 1, Commanders), and it is this one. [0137]

17. **A contact of his own.** In item 12 each fresh contact is weighed at a level of risk: one that is the force's own (chapter 1, A contact of its own) at the level the table gives, one it was told of at the task's risk, whatever the rating. The kinds closed on are read at that level, contact by contact (and the waters to strike first likewise: a contact in them is closed on at any level but preserve the force), and the first contact is taken from those of a kind closed on at their own level. At preserve the force the force closes on nothing unless a fresh contact is weighed at a bolder level. With command levels off, no commander or a steady one, every contact is weighed at the task's risk, and the rule is items 11 to 16 as they were. [0137 items 7 and 8]

| The commander | A contact of his own is weighed at |
| --- | --- |
| Cautious | One level more careful than the task's risk: press as balanced, balanced as preserve the force (which closes on nothing), preserve the force as itself |
| Steady, or none named | The task's risk |
| Bold | One level bolder: preserve the force as balanced, balanced as press, press as itself |

18. **Nothing else moves with it.** The speed (item 11), the fuel and its margin (items 7 and 14), where it goes (item 13), its search, its patrol share, its strike settings and its break-off level are the task's risk's, as before; a bold commander at preserve the force who closes goes at cruise and measures his fuel with preserve the force's margin. He stays inside his task's forces. Nothing here is a refusal. [0137 item 9]
19. **The reason says so.** Where a rating changed how a fresh contact was weighed, the reason for closing or for not closing names the commander, the command, his rating, that the contact is the force's own, the level it was weighed at against the task's risk and the kinds closed on at that level; the binding of a closing route says that a commander of that rating weighs a contact of his own at that level. Where no rating changed how a contact was weighed, no reason's words change. [0137 item 10]

A task already standing is not withdrawn when the force's carrier is lost: item 9 hands in the notice. [0125 items 3 to 11]

**Radio silence.** The force's morning, at preserve the force and where report figures are loaded, begins with the order `set_radio_silence` on for a force not on silence, as Standing at a hex by night, above, has it; at balanced and at press the setting is left as found. [0136 items 18 to 20]

**Under take a place** (decision 0148). Cover for an operation by carriers. It adds no rule of the engine and no state; it draws no random number.

20. **Forming.** Under a "take a place" whose plan is released, the staff forms "operate a carrier force" for each task force on the carrier mission with a carrier afloat that has an air group aboard (a force before its day is left out) that the nearest forces limit on the end's chain names, in task force number order, where no carrier task for that force is in force anywhere on the side, whoever made it. The hex is the **covering hex**: the first hex along the shortest sea route from the hex the force is in that morning to the place's hex that is within the reach, the reach being the least strike radius, at normal reach, among the bomber groups aboard (item 12, with the place for the contact); with no bomber group aboard, or the force already within the reach of the place, the hex the force is in; with no sea route, none is formed and the reason says so. The reason gives the place, the force's hex, the reach and the group that sets it, the hex chosen and its distance from the force and from the place. Since decision 0218 the hex along the route is the first within the reach that lies outside submarine waters the area's picture holds, the reason naming each hex passed over; where all lie in them, the first, and the reason says no place outside serves (Patrolling waters with submarines, rule 16). [0148 items 1, 2 and 9; 0218, rule 55]
21. **The force is then the task's** by the carrier doctrine as it is (items 1 to 19): routed to its hex at the speed of item 11, searching, flying its patrol, resting its groups, weighing its fuel and closing on a contact; the enemy's bases on the side's map, the base at the place among them, are threats of its search already. The task is served after the landing's morning (The day's order), so that on the morning the plan is released it is formed and served at once. [0148 item 3]
22. **The strike on the place.** A bomber group that item 3 sets to strike ships is left to it on a morning the force's picture holds a fresh contact of ships (no more than 3 pulses old) within the group's reach of the hex the force is in. Where a targets limit binds the group, only a contact of a kind on its list counts: a fresh contact of a kind left off the list does not keep the group from the raid. With no targets limit every fresh contact in reach does, as before (decision 0190, 17). On a morning with none, while the task stands under a "take a place" that is not met, the enemy holds a base on the side's map in the place's hex, and that hex is within the group's reach of the force's hex, the groups the raid's rules 1 to 4 send (Raiding a base: which bombers go, whether the raid goes at the task's risk, what the last raid cost) are set to the base strike mission at that base, the lowest-numbered there, and given the raid's held share. A fighter group aboard is the force's cover: it is given no order by this, and the escorts it gives, at preserve the force, are what its patrol share leaves. Where the raid does not go (at preserve the force a group holds back a third and can send 6, fewer than the 12 the risk asks), no group is set to the base strike and item 3's orders stand; the reason gives what the bombers can send against what the risk asks. [0148 items 4, 5 and 9; 0190 item 17]
23. **Back to ships.** On a morning a fresh contact is within a group's reach again, the group is given item 3's naval strike mission again; when the base changes side the engine clears the mission (chapter 7, A base that changes side) and the staff gives no order for it. A fighter's patrol covers its own force and is not flown over a beach. [0148 items 6 and 7]
24. **When the place is taken.** On the first morning the end's plan is complete (the intent is met) the force is routed to its home port at cruising speed, where it has one and is not there or on its way, and the task is given no order after: its groups are left as they are. With no home port it is given no order, and the reason says the commander must move it. A force held by the commander is given none. [0148 item 8]
25. **Moved to look** (decision 0159, item 14, as the architect ruled it on Nov 18, 2026). The task of item 20 is formed sooner, while the end's plan is preparing, on a morning when all of these hold: the need to know is not met (Taking a place, The need to know; so never where none is asked, at press or by the knowledge limit); the side has no fighting man ashore at the place and no group the end may use is on reconnaissance of it; no air group the end's forces limit names that can search reaches the place from where it lies; and the limit names a group that can search, that the commander does not hold, aboard a ship of a carrier force the limit names. The force is the first such in task force number order that may be used, is not held and has no carrier task anywhere; one force a take is moved to look. Its hex is the covering hex of item 20 where such a group aboard reaches the place from it, and otherwise the first hex along the shortest sea route from the force to the place within the longest search range of those groups; with no such hex none is formed and the reason says so; the hex along the route is the first outside submarine waters, as in item 20 (decision 0218, rule 55). So the force moves once, to the hex it would cover the landing from. At balanced (the end's risk) the staff forms it without asking; at preserve the force only where leave to launch is given, and without it it forms nothing, gives the force no order and gives a reason each morning that says the force could look from that hex, the passage and how near the place it would then be, and that the staff waits for leave to launch. The reason of the forming gives what is held and asked, the scouts aboard with their range, the hex and why, the hours of the passage and the distance from there to the place. [0159 item 14]
26. **Before the release.** While the plan is preparing the task is served by items 1 to 19 as on any morning, and the strike on the place (item 22) is not given. A group the take's look keeps on reconnaissance of the place, or sends there that morning, is given no order by the task: its line says it is looking. When the need is met the look does not rest a group aboard the force moved to look: it is left to the task, which sets it as item 3 says. The force stays at its hex to cover the landing and is sent home by item 24, not when the need is met. On the morning the plan is released the task stands and item 20 forms no other. [0159 item 14]

**Under "control an island"** (decision 0151, item 8a). The carriers are the island's, not a take's: the take under an island takes no carrier force (Taking a place, Under "control an island"), and a force is never sent home when a take is met (item 24 does not apply).

25. **Forming.** The staff forms "operate a carrier force" under the island's denial, for each carrier force it may take (decision 0149, item 5) with a carrier afloat that has a group aboard, at the covering hex of item 20 reckoned to the island's first place, in task force order, where no carrier task for the force is in force anywhere on the side (Controlling an island, The tree each morning). [0151 items 6 and 8a]
26. **Served after the landing's morning,** with the carrier tasks of the takes (The day's order, below), by the carrier doctrine as it is (items 1 to 19). It weighs its fuel and not its rounds (Not built). [0151 item 8a]
27. **The strike on the place** (items 22 and 23) applies to such a task for the first take of the island's tree, in the island's order, whose plan is released, whose place is not the side's and is within the reach of a bomber group aboard; the groups are set to the base strike at the enemy's base in that hex as item 22 says. [0151 item 8a]

**A find, and going after it** (decision 0214; the figure `pursuit_nights`; rules label 27, doctrine `supply-53`). The closing rule leaves a contact that is too far for one night without a word. This has the staff look at it again, and ask the commander's leave, or take it, to go after it. It adds one figure, one leave of the commander's, three notices, one standing interrupt, two commands and a stop before the day; it draws no random number. [0214]

28. **What a find is, and where it is looked for.** On a morning a carrier force's closing rule runs (items 12 to 16: the force is not held, not on its way home, not fuelling at home, nothing is out of fuel, and a bomber group aboard has an aircraft able to fly) and gives **too far**, the staff looks for a **find**: the contact the closing rule would take were the night's steaming taken as `pursuit_nights` nights. The rule is worked again whole, with that one thing lifted, and nothing else of it lifted: the kinds the risk closes on (and the commander's rating that reads them contact by contact, items 17 to 19), the freshness of 3 pulses, the reach, the fuel to go and to come back at the risk's margin and the sea route are as they stand. Where the closing rule as it stands closes on a contact, stays or finds nothing fresh of a kind it closes on, no find is looked for. A force that stands under a "take a place" or under the denial of an island has no find: a carrier force that serves a take or an island stays with it. [0214, rules 1 and 3]
29. **Too far, the one kind of find.** The night's steaming is taken as `pursuit_nights` nights: the closing rule is worked again with the force's night at full speed times that many. The contact it then closes on, one beyond the reach and within those nights and the reach, is the find. With `pursuit_nights` at 1 (the frozen step 1 figures) this lifts nothing. A find is of this one kind and is one contact.
30. **What a find must pass.** The contact it gives is no find unless:
    - **It lies inside the order's area limit,** read up the tree from the task: where an area limit is set on the chain, its hexes must hold the contact's estimated hex. The contact is taken first and the area read after: where the one the rule takes lies outside the area the staff looks for no other, and there is no find. Where no area is set there is no limit. The closing rule as it stands does not read the area (`docs/rules/differences.md`); a find is held to it and a close is not.
    - **The force can go there:** the same sea route to a hex within the reach of the contact and the same fuel to go and to come back, at the speed chosen, as a close needs (items 13 and 14), with the speed of item 11. A contact the force cannot go to for want of a sea route or of fuel is no find, and the reason of the day says why it does not close, as it did.
    A find never crosses a hard limit of the order but the area; the forces limit binds as it does. [0214, rules 1 and 2]
31. **Who gives the leave.** For a find the staff reads, in this order, whose leave there is:
    1. **A pursuit of the force by the commander's answer** already stands (item 34): the force pursues the find, and no notice is handed in.
    2. **A pursuit the staff took earlier** stands and the force goes on with it, and no notice is handed in.
    3. **The commander has refused the contact** (item 34): the force goes on with its order, and the reason of the day says that the commander refused leave to pursue that contact. This binds at `go` too.
    4. **The leave to pursue** (The three leaves, above), read up the tree from the task: `stay`, nothing at all changes, no notice and no word beyond the closing rule's own; `go`, the find is pursued by leave taken (item 33); and `ask`, the staff asks (item 32). [0214, rules 4, 5 and 10]
32. **Asking leave.** The staff hands in the notice `asks_leave_to_pursue` (Notices, above) with a reason, and the force is given no order toward the find: it is served as though the contact were too far, so a force at its hex stays and one away from it is routed back to its hex by item 6 (item 15), and nothing of the order is withdrawn or changed. The reason says it is given no route and no speed order today, which is so only for a force at its hex. The staff asks only for a contact the commander's picture holds: the contact's number in the notice is the commander's, which with command levels off is the force's own; with command levels on it is the area command's number for a contact of the same estimated hex and kind, and where the area command holds none the staff does not ask and does nothing. The notice fires the standing interrupt `asks_leave_to_pursue` for the force (Interrupts, above), and a seat that runs time is stopped in the evening before it (chapter 1, The stop in the evening). With no answer there is no leave: the force goes on with its order, and the staff asks again at the next dusk if the find still stands. An unanswered asking never becomes a yes. [0214, rules 6 to 9]
33. **Taking the leave.** At `go`, for a find, and with no pursuit of the force and no refusal of the contact, the staff pursues it. It routes the force as a close does (items 13 and 16, at the speed of item 11) and hands in the notice `pursues` once, on the first morning, with a reason that says the leave to pursue was taken and where it comes from ("the leave to pursue of intent:N is go", or "no leave to pursue is set, so by default it is go").
