It begins the force's pursuit, taken, at that notice. On the later mornings the pursuit goes on with no notice. [0214, rule 10]
34. **The commander's answer.** `answer_pursuit` names a force, a contact in the commander's number and yes or no. It is taken in at the first step of the day, before the staff is asked, so an answer given at the stop in the evening is in when the staff is asked that dusk.
    - **Yes** begins a pursuit of the force at once, by answer, naming the contact and the day, and the answer's `CommandTaken` event as its cause; a no kept for that contact is lifted. The staff then pursues, by that leave (item 35), and hands in no asking and no `pursues` notice for the force. The leave is the force's, not the contact's: it stands while the force has something to go after (item 37), and a contact seen again under a new number keeps it. A yes given where no asking was made, or where no find stands, begins a pursuit all the same, and it ends at that morning's test (item 37).
    - **No** keeps the refusal, by force and contact, and ends a pursuit of the force that is under way. The refusal is kept until a yes lifts it: nothing else clears it. The force goes on with its order and is never asked about that contact again; it is asked about another, and a contact seen again under a new number is another. [0214, rules 8, 10 and 12]
35. **A pursuit, and its reason.** A force that pursues is routed as the closing rule routes one (items 13 to 16): to the first hex along the shortest sea route that is within the bombers' reach of the contact, at the speed of item 11, set to battle readiness, with its search arc taken from the hex it is in. The order it leaves is not withdrawn and not changed (item 36). The reason of the closing route says it is a pursuit and by whose leave: by the commander's leave given on day N, in which case the commander's answer, the `CommandTaken` of the yes, is a cause of the order and of the reason; or by leave taken, saying where it comes from; and which order waits. A pursuit by answer is read first (item 31), so a pursuit on the commander's yes goes on at `stay` and past a later refusal of another contact. [0214, rule 10]
36. **The order the force leaves.** It is not withdrawn and not changed, and nothing is proposed in its place. The force is away from its hex; its search and the order's other forces are as the staff's rules leave them. Cancelling or changing the order is the commander's, as now. [0214, rules 11 and 13]
37. **The end of a pursuit.** A force's pursuit, by answer or taken, ends at the first morning the staff serves the force's task and the force has neither a find nor a contact to close on or to stay for: the closing rule closes on nothing, stays on nothing, and gives no find the force can go to. The staff hands in the notice `pursuit_ended` with a reason that names the day and the contact the pursuit began on and says the force goes back to its hex by item 6, and the pursuit is gone with that notice. It ends wherever the closing rule does not run as well: a force that is on its way home (as it is sent for fuel by item 7), or fuelling at home, or fuelling at sea, or with no bomber group aboard able to fly while another group is, has neither a find nor a contact to close on, and its pursuit ends the same morning with the notice. A pursuit of a force that cannot move for want of fuel (item 9), of one whose task is not served that morning (it is held), and of one with no carrier afloat or no group aboard able to fly is neither carried on nor ended, and stands until the force's task is next served and the test is made. A contact seen again under a new number within the bounds keeps the pursuit; the commander may end it sooner with a no (item 34). A force whose pursuit has ended goes on as any force of the task, and may be asked about, or pursue, another find. [0214, rule 12]
38. **The figure.** `pursuit_nights`, in `air_combat.toml` (2; 1 in the frozen step 1 figures, where the closing rule is as it stood and no find is made through the night). A placeholder, a judgement with no row: a force is not sent after a contact it could not reach in two nights. As freshness stands (3 pulses), a pursuit steams one night on a report and goes on only if the contact is seen again. A whole number, at least 1; the loader refuses a float. [0214, rule 14]
39. **Reasons and records.** The asking is a staff reason that gives the find (the contact with its kind, age, where it was reported and its distance), where the force would go and the hours there, the leave and where it was read from, and the order that waits; the pursuit and its end are reasons of their own. Each reason is handed in with the notice that rests on it, as a decision to give no order. A refusal and a leave by answer are words in the reason of the day. [0214, rules 6, 10 and 16]
40. **Where no one commands.** A side with no commander has the leave at `go`, so its forces pursue a find that is too far. In a dry run the side tried carries the settings of its orders, `set_pursuit`, and not its answers: a made-up world's contacts are numbered anew, so an answer to a numbered contact, its pursuits by answer and its refusals are not carried into it (chapter 9). The made-up enemy has no commander and goes. A player who runs time and does not answer gives no leave. [0214, "Who answers where no one is there"]
41. **A side sees:** its own asking, its pursuit and its end as notices, whole, and their reasons; its own `CommandTaken` of an answer and of a setting; its own interrupt firing. The enemy sees none of it. [0214, rule 16]

A side sees: its own orders, their acceptance or refusal, reasons and notices, whole. The enemy sees none of it. The staff reads only the side's own view: the contacts it holds, its own forces, the map and the engine's forecasts of its own passages. [0125, 0130]

## Fuelling at sea, as the staff reads it

The staff's half of decision 0201. It draws no random number. The engine's half is chapter 2, Fuelling at sea. [0201 items 19 to 21]

**The warning.** Each morning the carrier doctrine and the stand doctrine name, in a reason of the task that gives no order, a force of theirs with no home port the staff knows, one of whose ships holds less than a quarter of her class's fuel capacity, where no oiler's force holds cargo fuel to send it to: the force, where it is and each ship below a quarter with her fuel and her capacity. It is given on every morning the doctrine serves the task and the ship is below: the carrier doctrine on a morning no ship of the force is dry, the stand doctrine for a force with no home port standing from where it is. A force the staff has no task for is not named. A quarter is a figure of the doctrine, a placeholder. [0201 items 19 and 25]

**An oiler's force is a place to fuel.** An oiler's force, for the staff, is a force of the side other than the force, with a ship afloat, whose ships with the mark hold cargo fuel, that is not under the order to fuel herself, not an escort and has no escort: one the order may name. The nearest by the engine's charged sea miles from the force's hex is taken, the lower number on a tie; the side's own forces are read from the side's view. [0201 item 20]

- **The carrier doctrine** (Operating a carrier force): where a ship has less fuel than the passage home needs with the margin and the force would be routed home to fuel, it is given the order to fuel from the oiler's force instead where the passage there is less than the passage home; and a force with no home port the staff knows, with a ship below a quarter, is given it where there is any oiler's force. The reason gives the fuel, the oiler's cargo fuel and both passages.
- **The stand doctrine** (Standing at a hex by night): a force with a home port that lies stopped at sea on no route away from it, and is short of the fuel the passage to the station and home needs at cruise with the risk's margin, is given the order to fuel from the oiler's force instead of the route home where the passage there is less than the passage home; and a force with no home port lying at its station is given it where it would go in to a base for fuel and the oiler's force is nearer than that base, or where a ship is below a quarter.
- **While the order stands** the staff gives the force no route, as it gives none while a force fuels in port: the carrier doctrine notes it and sets nothing of its place, its fuel or its closing; the stand doctrine gives it no order at all. When the order ends the force goes back to its task. A stand force with a home port that lies stopped at sea on no route, whose order to fuel at sea ended on the day just resolved (its `FuelOrderEnded`, read from the side's own events) and that is not short of the passage to the station and home at cruise with the risk's margin, is routed back to its station and home from there, the station marked to stand at, at cruise where that reaches the station in the night's 12 hours and else at full speed; its reason says it has fuelled from the oiler's force. Short, or with no such event, it is routed home as decision 0192, 16 has it.

**A force with an oiler is no lift.** Where a take counts the transport forces that are its to use (a forces limit that names no task force, or none), a force that has a ship afloat with the mark is left out: an oiler and her destroyer carry no men. A commander who wants such a force to carry men names it in the order's forces, as he may name any force, and a forces limit that names it counts it. Where no ship has the mark nothing changes. [0206 rule 21]

**What it does not do.** The staff places no oiler and cannot rescue a force that has run dry: an oiler is where her commander's route puts her. Planning fuel before a ship runs low is stage D of decision 0201. [0201 item 21]

A side sees: the warning and the reasons, whole, in its own staff's report. The enemy sees none of it.

## Carriers serving an order for an end

Decision 0202. A commander's "deny an area" or "neutralise a base" uses a free carrier force of his side, and the staff chooses where it works from by the risk, the enemy and the fuel. It runs at the area's level, in one pass right after the island doctrine (The day's order, below), and forms, keeps or withdraws carrier tasks; the force's morning is then the carrier doctrine's (Operating a carrier force, above). It adds no state and no rule of the engine, and draws no random number. The doctrine version is `supply-50`; since decision 0203 it is `supply-52` (The way a force takes, below). [0202]

**Which orders, which forces** (rules 1 to 5).

1. **The orders.** Each deny and neutralise the commander stated, in force, whose plan is released, and whose nearest forces limit names no task force and no ship. One whose limit names task forces uses only those, which keep their hex (A task force as an addressee, above). A deny or a neutralise the staff formed under an island keeps the island's rule for its carriers (Controlling an island). Nothing is formed before the plan is released: with leave to launch withheld the force stays where it is.
2. **A free force** is on the carrier mission, has a carrier afloat with an air group aboard, has no carrier task in force, no intent in force is addressed to it, no forces limit in force names it, it is not held, and it may be used today: the island's test of a force the staff may take, read over every intent in force.
3. **One pass.** The orders are taken in priority order, then by number. Each first keeps the carrier tasks the staff chose for it earlier: a task stands, with its station, unless its force is held (it is let go: the staff withdraws the task) or an intent of another tree higher in priority is addressed to the force or names it in a forces limit (the task is withdrawn). Then each free force not yet given to an order this morning is tried for a station; where one is found the task is formed and the force is the order's. A force for which an order finds no station is left for the next. A force given to an order higher in priority this morning is named in a reason under each order after it: "the one carrier force task_force:N is taken by intent:M".
4. **A hold, and the commander's word.** A force held is never taken, and one taken and then held is let go the next morning; lifted, the force is free again and its station chosen afresh. A direct order to a force the staff has tasked holds it by the override (Holds and the override, above); a direct order to a free force does not.

**Where** (rules 6 to 17).

5. **The candidates** are the hexes the force can reach by sea (the map's sea distance from the hex it lies in, worked once) from which the task can be done: for a neutralise, within the least normal strike radius of the bomber groups aboard of the base, in a straight line by the engine's own test of an air distance; for a deny, within that radius less 40 nm for each hex of the waters' distance, of the centre of the waters, or, where that leaves less than one hex (40 nm), within the radius itself. Where the centre is a hex ships cannot enter, the nearest sea hex of the waters stands for it. The hex the force lies in is a candidate like any other. A force with no bomber group aboard has none.
6. **Circles of danger.** Each enemy base on the side's map casts a circle of 15 hexes (the distance at which a supply run is timed by night, decision 0145), whatever it holds; each contact of the area's picture reported as a carrier and no more than 3 pulses old casts one of 8 hexes (the distance at which a lift is turned back, decision 0191). A contact in the deny's own waters casts none against it; the base to be neutralised casts its own. A hex at exactly the radius is inside.
7. **The margin** of a candidate is counted in hex steps: the least, over the circles, of its distance from the centre less the radius. Above 0 it is outside every circle, by that much; at 0 or below it is inside, the depth in the circle that holds it deepest.
8. **The choice.** The passage to a candidate is its hex steps by sea from the force. At **preserve the force**, only candidates outside every circle: the least passage, then the greater margin, then hex order; with none, no task. At **balanced**, as at preserve where one outside every circle is in fuel; with none, the candidate least deep in danger, then the least passage, then hex order. At **press**, the least passage, in danger or out, then the greater margin, then hex order. The candidates are tried for fuel in that order, and the first in fuel is the station. So a neutralise is never served by carriers at preserve, and at balanced they stand where the base's circle is shallowest within reach.
9. **Fuel.** A candidate is in fuel when every ship has the fuel for the passage out at the speed the carrier doctrine sails at (item 11 of Operating a carrier force: cruise at preserve, and at balanced and press cruise where it arrives within the night's 12 hours, else full speed), by the engine's measure of the miles and the speed, and home from it to the force's home port at cruise (the engine's charged miles), at the class's cruise burn, together times the risk's margin (1.5, 1.2, 1). A force stopped at a base of the side that holds fuel above its reserve and fuels a ship is weighed with its ships full, since it fuels before it sails. A force with no home port is not weighed, and the reason says its fuel could not be measured against a passage home.
10. **Kept.** The station is chosen on the morning the task is formed and kept: it is not chosen again when a contact comes or goes, only when the task is formed again, after a hold is lifted or a higher order has had the force.

**The regard for submarine waters** (decision 0218, rule 55; doctrine `supply-55`). Among the candidates in fuel, one inside submarine waters the area's picture holds is passed over for the next in the risk's order that lies outside them; the reason names each one passed over and why, and where none outside serves the station is the first in fuel, the reason says so and the reason carries the caution `station_in_submarine_waters`. The way, the circles and the choice of a way are as they were (Patrolling waters with submarines, rule 15). [0218, rule 55]

**What the force does.** The station is the hex of a task "operate a carrier force" under the order (the pair under a neutralise is allowed since this decision). The rest is the carrier doctrine as it stands: its patrol, its strikes on ships, its closing on a fresh contact by its own rule (the station is where it waits, not a fence), going home for fuel. Under a deny it reads the order's waters and targets limit through the chain, as a force named in the order does (Operating a carrier force, items 2 and 3). Under a neutralise, on a morning with no fresh contact of ships within a bomber group's reach, the groups the raid's rules send are set to strike the target, by the routine of the strike on the place (Operating a carrier force, item 22), given the target for the place; ships come first. While the force goes to its station, its search takes in the hexes of its way for the coming day (the sea path, as many hexes as a day at its speed covers) and the station itself; at the station the arc is as before. [0202 items 18 to 23]

**The way a force takes** (decision 0203, stage 1). Every ship takes the engine's shortest way by sea between the points of its route; the staff has chosen where a force goes and when, and, for the force below, now chooses how. It adds no state and no rule of the engine, draws no random number and changes no hash. The doctrine version is `supply-52`. [0203]

**Whose way is chosen** (items 1 to 6, 23 to 26).

1. **A carrier task of the staff's making.** The way is chosen only for a carrier task the staff formed under a commander's deny or neutralise whose forces limit names no task force (the station of decision 0202), at balanced or preserve. At press nothing is chosen: the force is routed as it was. The way home for fuel, and closing on a contact, are the shortest way, as before, and so is every route of every other task (a station of the commander's, a stand, a supply run, a lift, a take, a bombardment). [0203 items 1 to 5]
2. **When it is chosen.** Only where the force is stopped, or its route ends elsewhere than its station or is done: on the morning the task is formed, on the morning after fuelling at its home port (where the doctrine's own test sends it back to its station), and on the morning after closing on a contact. Where the engine's forecast of the shortest way does not arrive (the order refused, no ship able to move, a ship run dry), nothing is chosen. [0203 items 1 and 6]
3. **A force under way on the one point of its station** is routed each morning as before, and no way is chosen for it. [0203 item 23]
4. **A kept way.** A force under way on a route of more than one point whose last is its station is on a kept way: it is given no route and no speed, the engine's route holds the way, and the reason says so. A force that is stopped, or whose route ends elsewhere, is routed afresh and its way chosen afresh. A kept way arrives when its forecast said; the shortest way, routed day by day, may arrive later than its forecast, because the doctrine drops a force from full speed to cruise when twelve hours or less remain, so the lateness limit below is a little stricter for it. [0203 items 24 and 26]
5. **The search on the way** (decision 0202, item 23, above) reads the engine's path through the waypoints that remain: those of the route given that morning, or those that remain of a kept way, or the station alone. The doctrine knows a force is bound for its station by the last waypoint of its route. [0203 item 25]
6. **No circle, no search.** With no circle of danger at all, the shortest way is taken and nothing is searched, since the search would give the engine's own way alone. [0203 item 17]

**The speed.** It is the one the carrier doctrine's rule gives the shortest way on the morning the route is given (item 11, above: cruise at preserve; at balanced cruise where the shortest way is twelve hours or less at cruise, else full), and every way is weighed at that speed. A longer way does not change it. [0203 item 7]

**What is weighed** (items 7 to 12).

7. **The circles** are decision 0202's, made by the same routine from the area's picture on the morning the route is given. [0203 item 10]
8. **The danger of a hex** is how many hexes inside a circle it lies, and one more: 1 at the rim, 16 on an enemy base, 9 on a carrier contact. In more than one circle it is the greatest. Outside every circle it is nothing. [0203 item 11]
9. **The danger by day of a way** is the sum of the danger of the hexes the engine's forecast has the force enter in the morning or the afternoon, but for the last. A hex entered by night adds nothing, and the station's own danger, the same for every way, is left out. The engine's forecast of a route reports, for each hex it enters, the day and pulse it enters it in (chapter 2, The path); the danger is read from that report. [0203 items 8 and 12]
10. **The limit on lateness.** A way is within the limit when the engine forecasts its arrival no later than the pulse after the one the shortest way arrives in at balanced, and no later than the same pulse of the day after at preserve. [0203 item 13]
11. **The limit of fuel.** A way is in fuel by decision 0202's test for a station (decision 0202, item 9, above), made on the way's own charged miles at the speed above, with the ships full for a force stopped at a base that can fuel it. With no home port it is in fuel, as in decision 0202. The doctrine's own test of whether a fuelled force goes back to its station at all comes first. [0203 items 14 and 15]

**The choice** (items 13 to 17).

12. **The staff's clock.** To propose ways only, and never to decide, the staff takes the force to leave at the start of the night of the day the route is given, at the knots the engine's forecast of the shortest way gives. The latest it enters the hex of step n is after 40n less 20 nautical miles. The earliest is after the straight line from the start to the hex, less 20. A hex counts as entered by night only where both hours fall in the same night pulse. A moment on the line between two pulses belongs to the earlier. The most steps searched is the most whose latest hour falls by the end of the last pulse the limit on lateness allows. [0203 item 9]
13. **The search.** For each count of steps from the shortest way's to that most, the staff finds the way of that many steps or fewer with the least danger by its clock, then the fewest steps. It runs on the steps ships can take, as the engine tests a step (`SideView::can_step`, which reads the map, which is common knowledge), from the hex the engine's forecast starts the force from. It is worked backward from the station; the way is built forward from the start, taking at each step the first direction in the engine's order that keeps the least. The station costs nothing. A proposal may pass a hex twice or double back. [0203 item 17]
14. **Routes.** Each proposal is made a route by the routine that keeps a route inside an area (a supply run's route, "Its route", above; decision 0107), the hexes of the proposal and the start being the area. Routes with the same waypoints are one. The shortest way, the one point of the station, is always among the routes. [0203 items 16 and 18]
15. **Each route is forecast by the engine** at the speed above. Its danger is read from that forecast, so what is weighed is the path the engine would steam, which need not be the proposal hex for hex; its arrival must be within the limit on lateness, and its fuel is read on its charged miles. [0203 item 19]
16. **Of the routes within both limits, the least danger is taken; then the fewest miles charged; then the fewest waypoints; then the proposal of fewest steps.** A way that is longer so that it comes to a circle after dark is a way like any other: the staff does not stop or slow the force to wait for the night. [0203 items 20 and 22]
17. **Where none is in fuel,** the shortest way is taken, as before, and the reason says that no way is in fuel. [0203 item 21]

**What the staff says** (items 27 to 29).

18. **A bent way.** The reason of the route joins the route's reason and gives the route and its miles, the hexes the engine steams through, the pulse it arrives in and the pulse the shortest way would, and, for each circle, the hexes inside it entered by day (and the nearest of them to the circle's centre) and by night, the limit on lateness, the fuel, and, where a clearer way that arrives in time is out of fuel, that it is. [0203 item 27]
19. **The shortest way taken in danger.** Where the shortest way is taken and it enters a hex inside a circle by day, the reason says so and that no way that arrives in time is clearer, or that no clearer way in fuel arrives in time. Where it is taken with nothing inside by day, nothing is said of a way. [0203 item 28]
20. **No figure of danger is shown;** the reason speaks in hexes, pulses and distances. [0203 item 29]

A side sees these reasons as any reason of the staff, its own and whole. The staff reads only its own view: the picture of the area and the map. [0203]

**Reasons.** The reason of a station gives the force and that it is free, the candidates and the reach they rest on, the rule of the risk, the station with its distance and compass point from the target or the centre of the waters, the passage there, each circle it lies outside or inside with the base's name or the contact's number and age and by how much, a nearer station passed over and why, and the fuel out and home against the least aboard; with command levels on, the force as of when its state was known; and what would change it: another risk, the contact growing old, more fuel. Where no station is found it says which circles held every candidate (and for a neutralise that a raid is flown from inside the base's own circle), or that no candidate was in fuel and which ships were short, or that none could be reached by sea. "Cannot meet" is said only when no base and no carrier force can serve the order, and its reason names both. [0202 items 26 to 29]

**A deny a carrier task serves** (decision 0217, rules 1 to 3; doctrine `supply-54`). Where no base the deny may use can strike or search its waters, and a task "operate a carrier force" is in force under the deny (one kept, or formed that morning by the carrier pass, by the island doctrine or for a force its forces limit names), the staff hands in no notice that it cannot meet the order. Its reason for giving the bases no task names each carrier task first, with its force and its hex, then says of each base what "cannot meet" says (its distance from the waters, the reach and range of each type it has, the groups coming), and does not say "cannot meet". This holds for every deny, whoever made it: the deny the staff forms under "control an island" included, whose carrier tasks the carrier pass does not read. Where no carrier task serves it, the notice and the words of "cannot meet" are as above; where a stand task alone serves it, nothing is said of the bases, as before. Nothing else of the deny changes: what it forms, the orders it gives and the days it gives them. A neutralise the island forms is not changed by this, and says it cannot be met while the carrier's bombers raid its target (`differences.md`). [0217 rules 1 to 3]

**Cautions** (decision 0217, rules 4 and 5). A reason may carry cautions: short statements of what the commander should weigh before he approves, each with its kind, the unit or place it is of, and its words. The reason of a carrier station the staff chose (Reasons, above) carries one for each circle of danger that holds the station, `station_in_danger`, of the enemy base whose circle it is (or, for a contact's circle, of the contact's hex), and one where the force has no home port the staff knows, `fuel_not_measured`, of the force, and, since decision 0218, one where the station lies in submarine waters the side holds and no place outside them serves, `station_in_submarine_waters`, of the station's hex (Patrolling waters with submarines, The staff's regard for submarine waters). The working keeps its sentences as they are. A side is shown a reason's cautions wherever it is shown the reason; `propose` gives each sub-intent's cautions as a list, and the table shows them under the list of what a plan would do. No other reason carries cautions yet. They are recorded with the reason and change no state and no hash. [0217 rules 4 and 5; 0218, rule 55]

**At the table.** The two orders' words say what may be used: aircraft from the side's bases and a carrier force that is free. The preview of a deny or a neutralise whose plan waits for a leave carries, beside the staff's decisions, the proposal as if released, which names the force and its station; the order's draft and the plan paper read it, and a plan that would form nothing, or cannot meet the order, says so where it would be called ready. A carrier force's card offers "keep this force out of the staff's plans", a hold. [0202 items 30 to 33]

A side sees: its own `IntentFormed`, `StaffReason`, the `CommandTaken` of each command and the orders, whole. The enemy sees none of it. The staff reads only its own view: no reason names what an enemy base holds. [0202]

## Readying a landing, and building up a base

Decision 0141, step 2 of stage 3. Two tasks a commander may state as roots, the first doctrines that order land units. Each is served each morning in the air doctrine's run, after the carrier forces, by the level it is addressed to: a "ready a landing" by its task force, a "build up a base" by its base. Its numbers are the ground figures (chapter 7); with none loaded it gives no order and says so. The staff reads only the side's own view.

**Leaves.** A plan of either kind takes leave to prepare as a hold's does (a draft waits for it, with a reason and the notice `waits_for_leave_to_prepare`), and works while it is preparing or released. It never asks leave to launch and the staff never releases it: it launches nothing. Nothing is ordered without leave to prepare. [0141 items 5 and 15]

**The units a task may use.** Those named in every forces limit on its chain (The six limits, above). With none on the chain, a "build up a base" may use the working units of the side with men ashore in its base's hex, and a "ready a landing" none. The same test is intake's (The forces limit on a staff's orders) and the override's. Each morning the tasks of both kinds are served in priority order (ranked first, then by number), and each leaves out, with words in its reasons: a unit before its day (A unit before its day, above); a unit that is held, "the staff plans without it"; a unit a higher task took this morning, "serves intent:N, which is higher in priority"; a unit with no man left; and, for a build-up, a unit not ashore in the base's hex. A unit serves one task. [0141 items 8, 13, 14 and 16]

**Ready a landing.** Each morning:

1. **The force's order.** Where the force's preparation is not for the task's hex with exactly the units kept as its roster, the staff gives the force `prepare_landing` for the hex with those units (chapter 7, Preparing a landing). A force that is held is given none, and a force before its day is given none and the task gets one reason. A working unit kept is on the roster and is given no order. [0141 items 2 and 4]
2. **Each fighting unit on the roster** is set to train or to stand ready (which on a roster is to rehearse), where its activity differs. Below the standard of experience, 2, it trains at preserve the force until it is at the standard; at balanced until it is at the standard, or, with a date, until the days left before the date (the date less today) are no more than the days its rehearsal still needs; at press it does not train. A unit at or above the standard rehearses, and so does one that cannot train: it has men aboard, its hex holds no base of its side, or its experience is at the training ceiling. The days a rehearsal needs are what is left to 100 of its rehearsal for the task's hex over its day's gain (`preparation_per_day` times its assault factor), rounded up. [0141 item 3]
3. **Ready.** The task is ready on a morning the force's preparation is already for the hex with that roster, the roster is not empty, and the force's loading and each fighting unit's rehearsal for the hex are at least 100 (50 at press), and at preserve the force each fighting unit is at the standard. On the first such morning the staff gives no order and hands in the notice `ready`, once. [0141 item 5]
4. **Late.** With a date, on a morning the task is not ready, the staff projects the morning it would be: today plus the longest of the days the loading needs (from the loading the force will have after today's order, at `preparation_per_day`) and, for each fighting unit, its days of training by the rule of item 2 (the days left to the standard, `training_days_per_level` a level less those counted) and then of rehearsal to the risk's mark. If that morning is after the date, or a unit can never be ready, it hands in the notice `late`, once, with the days late and what binds: the loading, a unit's training (where its days include training) or a unit's rehearsal; at a tie the loading, then the lower unit. [0141 item 6]
5. **Reasons** give each unit's experience against the standard, its days of training counted against the days a level takes, its rehearsal for the hex and the days it still needs at its gain, the force's loading, the risk and the date. A morning with no order for the task has one reason that says so. [0141 item 7]

**Build up a base.** Each morning every unit the task may use, at the base, is set to work at the job of the first want not yet met, where its activity differs: an airfield and its damage mended at `airfield`, defences at `defences`, the port at `port`, aircraft repair at `aircraft`. An airfield is met when the base has one; its damage mended when its airfield has no damage (a base with no airfield has none); defences when the base's defences are at the number or above. The port and aircraft repair are never met: they are worked while they are the first want left. With every want met the staff gives no order and says so, and on the first such morning hands in the notice `met`, once; a want that stops being met is worked again. The reasons give the work still to do on the want (for an airfield `airfield_work` less the work done; for mending the damage times `work_per_airfield_damage_point`; for defences the work at each grade's cost from the defences to the number), the work the units make in a day (chapter 7, The day's work, with the fighting share of the last night: its fed share where the figures of decision 0208 are not given, so a day's count for a unit that is mending is a little long, since it is worked on today's share), and the days that comes to, rounded up. A fighting unit is put to work only where a forces limit names it. [0141 items 8 to 12]

**Once.** A notice is handed in once for its plan: the staff reads its own side's events and hands in no notice of a kind it handed in for the plan before. [0141 items 5, 6 and 11]

A side sees: its own orders, reasons and notices, whole. The enemy sees none. [0141]

## Taking a place

Decision 0142, step 3 of stage 3. An end a commander states, served by the area each morning in the air doctrine's run: its preparing before the tasks that ready units are served, and its launching after them. It carries a landing as far as the day the place is the side's; what the men do ashore when the enemy has men there is step 4. Its numbers are the ground figures (chapter 7); with none loaded it gives no order and says so. The staff reads only the side's own view: the enemy as its public bases and the side's estimate of its men.

**The end.** "Take a place" (`take_place`, code 13) names a hex of land, coast or inland, and may have a date: the day by the end of which the place is to be the side's. A tool may name the place by a base on the side's map that is not the side's, which means that base's hex. It is a root, addressed to the area. Its forces limit may name the task forces, the land units and a source, a base of the side. Each of the three is the commander's where he names it and the staff's where he does not (With no forces named, below), and the end may have no forces limit at all: a forces limit with no source is not refused, as it is for keeping a base supplied. It is met when the side holds a base in the hex and the enemy holds none there. [0142 items 1 to 3; 0149 items 1 and 2]

**Its phase** is read, not stored: prepare while the plan is preparing; move from its release until a man of the side is ashore at the place; land from then until the intent is met. `command_state` shows it. [0142 item 5]

**Leaves.** A draft waits for leave to prepare as a hold's does. A draft whose take waits for its men (Waits for its men, below) is not made ready that morning, though leave to prepare is given: it stays a draft and is made ready on the morning the take forms its landing. A plan that is preparing is not released by the leaves: the end's own rule asks leave to launch, below. Nothing is ordered without leave to prepare, and nothing embarks or sails before the plan is released. [0142 items 15 and 25]

**Met.** On each morning, first: where the place is the side's, the staff marks the plan complete (`mark_plan`) and hands in the notice `met`, once (its words the intent's, "intent:N is met"), and orders nothing else. Stated of a place that is the side's already, that is the first morning. [0142 items 3 and 23]

**Prepare.** On the first morning the plan is approved and no "ready a landing" stands under it, the staff, where the end's forces limit names all three of the task forces, the land units and the source (otherwise With no forces named, below):

1. **Lands at the place itself.** For a place that is not a coast hex it gives no order and says it has no way there. [0142 item 4]
2. **Finds the transport force:** the lowest-numbered task force of the forces limit on the transport mission, with a ship afloat of a class that carries men, that the side may use, that the commander does not hold, and that lies stopped at the source or has men of the limit's units aboard. With none it forms nothing and says why for each. [0142 items 6 and 26]
3. **Finds the units that may land:** the fighting units of the forces limit that the side may use, that the commander does not hold, and whose men are all ashore in the source's hex or all aboard the transport force. A working unit is left out. Their order: the higher assault skill first, then the higher experience, then the lower number. [0142 items 7, 8 and 26]
4. **Gives them ships.** A ship carries men of one unit. A unit aboard keeps the ships it is in. In that order, each unit ashore takes the force's ships that carry men and are not yet taken, in number order, until their berths hold its men; a unit for which the ships left are too few is left out, and the next is tried. Which ship each unit's men go into is the load order's (chapter 7). [0142 item 9]
5. **Sizes them.** Where the side holds an estimate of the enemy's men at the place, it stops taking units once the men chosen are at least the estimate times 3 at preserve the force, 2 at balanced, 1 at press (the staff's figures, placeholders). With no estimate it takes every unit the ships can carry, and says that it goes with no count of the enemy. Where the units the ships can carry are fewer than the men wanted, it waits for its men (Waits for its men, below) where a day in reach brings them, at preserve the force and at balanced; otherwise at press it goes with what it has and says so, at balanced it hands in `cannot_bring_enough` (below), and at preserve the force it forms nothing, asks no leave, and hands in the notice `cannot_meet`, once. [0142 item 10; 0159 items 6 and 7]
6. **Forms the task** "ready a landing" under the end, for the transport force at the place's hex (`form_intent`), and sets its forces limit to the units chosen (`set_limit`). The choice is made once and kept as that limit, which the commander may change as he may any. With a date, the task's date is the morning of the end's day, less the days to embark and the days of the passage, plus one, and not before day 1. The task is served that morning (Readying a landing, above). [0142 items 11 to 13]

**Waits for its men.** Where the units chosen are short of the men wanted, none of their men is aboard, and the risk is preserve the force or balanced, the staff works the first day on which the units and forces it may then use, at the source, give the men wanted: for each day after today in turn it makes the choice again, Prepare, items 2 to 5 (or With no forces named, items 1 to 5), reading which units and forces the side may use on that day instead of today (A unit before its day, above), and nothing more of the enemy than the estimate it already reads. A day is in reach where it is no later than today plus 7 days (the staff's figure, a placeholder, `WAIT_HORIZON_DAYS`) for an end with no date; for an end with a date, where a landing formed that day, with that day's units, would be ready by the date its task would be given (Prepare, item 6), by the projection of Readying a landing (the days of loading and of each unit's training and rehearsal, at least one), the arithmetic of the notice `late`. [0159 items 4 and 6]

- **A day in reach brings them.** The staff waits for the first such day: it forms nothing and gives no order under the end that morning, and its reason says the men wanted, the men to hand, which unit or force may be used from which day, the men that day's lift gives, and that it waits for that day. It hands in no notice. The plan, where it is a draft, stays one (Leaves, above). On that day the choice gives the men wanted and the take goes on as Prepare says. [0159 item 6]
- **None does.** At preserve the force it is as Prepare, item 5, says. At balanced it does not go with what it has: it forms nothing, gives no order and hands in the notice `cannot_bring_enough`, once for the plan; each morning after, the reason says why. Its reason gives the men wanted, the men to hand, and each unit or force named (or, with none named, of the side) that may be used later with its day and why it does not serve: too late to land by the date, beyond the last day looked at, or not enough with it; or that none is coming. It waits for the commander, who may set the risk to press, change a limit or the date, or leave it; a new estimate or a unit's day coming into reach is read the next morning. [0159 item 7]
- Waiting for men aboard a force, a later lift (A second lift, Forces with men aboard, below), readying the men to hand while it waits, and landing at a beach away from the enemy are not in it: a lift with men aboard, a later lift and a second lift are under Not to starve, below. [0159 items 6 and 8]

**Not to starve.** At balanced, on a morning the staff would form or send a lift of men under the end, it lands men short of the men wanted, with too few in reach, only where it can feed them (decision 0169). `crates/sim-staff/src/take.rs`, `starve_check`.

1. **Where it is worked.** The first lift where any unit chosen is aboard (a lift as it stands among them), worked in Prepare before the task is formed; each later lift as it stands, worked as it is sent (Forces with men aboard, item 3); and a second lift (A second lift, below), worked on each morning it would be given an order or its units added to the limit. A first lift whose men are all ashore at its source is under Waits for its men, above, unchanged. A landing already formed is not stopped by it at its release. [0169 item 1]
2. **Short.** The lift is short where the fit men of the operation ashore at the place that can attack (Ashore, items 1 to 3, below) and the fighting men the lift carries are together fewer than the men wanted (Prepare, item 5). With no estimate held nothing is short and the lift goes as before. [0169 item 2]
3. **Nothing in reach.** The men in reach are those of item 2 and the fighting men aboard every other transport force the end may send as a later lift as it stands (Forces with men aboard, item 3), counted where the force may first be used on or before the end's last day: its date where one is set, else today and the 7 days after it, the horizon of Waits for its men. Where they are the men wanted or more, the lift goes as before. [0169 item 3]
4. **Fed.** Where the lift is short and the men in reach are too few, the men it lands can be fed where both hold: the place has a hold of a base in its hex with a source (Holding a place, The check), and the check that the place can be held, worked for this lift's force and every man aboard it, finds nothing short; and the supply the lift will have aboard when it sails, what is aboard and what it will load at its source, is at least the check's daily need times its days until supply follows, without the margin. With no such hold nothing follows the men, and they cannot be fed. [0169 item 4]
5. **Not fed.** The end forms no landing for that lift and gives its force no order; a later lift is not added to the limit, nor the units of a second lift. It hands in `cannot_bring_enough` once for the plan, and waits for the commander. Its reason gives the men wanted, the men the lift gives, the men in reach and the day reckoned to, and what of item 4 failed with its figures. Each later morning it reads all of it again, and goes on the first on which the lift is not short, enough men are in reach or the men can be fed. A plan not lifted stays as it was: a draft made ready on leave to prepare stays preparing. The men stay where they are. [0169 items 5 and 9]
6. **Fed: it goes** as before, and its reason says it goes short and that the men can be fed where they wait. [0169 item 6]
7. **The risk.** At press the lift goes. At preserve the force the end is as it was: Prepare, item 5, and the check of Holding a place stop it. [0169 items 7 and 8]

**With no forces named.** Where the end's forces limit does not name all three, or there is none, the staff chooses what the commander left out, on the same first morning, inside what he named, in the order a staff works in. `crates/sim-staff/src/assemble.rs`. [0149]

1. **What it may take.** A task force, a land unit or a base of the side that the side may use that day (A unit before its day, above), that the commander does not hold, and that is not named in the forces limit (read as it binds) of an intent in force served before the end that morning, in another tree, nor the task force a task of one is addressed to. A base so named is one that is that intent's source. [0149 item 5]
2. **The enemy.** The side's estimate of the men at the place and the men wanted by the risk, as in Prepare, item 5. With no estimate, as many as can be carried. [0149 item 6]
3. **The source.** A base can be the source if it is the side's, the staff may take it (and it is the commander's source, where he named one), a transport force it may take lies stopped there with a ship afloat that carries men, and a fighting unit it may take has all its men ashore in its hex and none aboard. For each such base, in base order, the staff works the lift it could make: the transport force of item 5 and the units of item 4 there. The passage is the engine's forecast for that force at cruising speed to the place. The source is the base whose lift gives the men wanted with the shortest passage, then the lower number; where none gives them, the base whose lift gives the most men, then the lower number; with no estimate, the most men, then the shortest passage, then the lower number. [0149 item 7]
4. **The units,** of those at the source it may take (and the commander named, where he named any), in the order and by the ships of Prepare, items 3 to 5, until the men wanted. [0149 item 8]
5. **The transport force,** of those it may take stopped at the source (and the commander named, where he named any): the lowest-numbered whose berths hold the men wanted; where none does, or with no estimate, the one with the most berths, then the lowest numbered. One force lands an operation. [0149 item 9]
6. **The risk on the way.** Every force on the bombardment mission that it may take that lies stopped at a base of the side, every force on the carrier mission that it may take wherever it lies, and every force on the escort mission that it may take that lies stopped at the source, is taken. They are given their tasks by the rules as they are: a bombardment task by Bombarding a base, a carrier task by Operating a carrier force, Under take a place, and the escort by Escorting and covering a run. [0149 items 10 and 11]
7. **The limit kept.** The staff forms the task "ready a landing" for the transport force at the place, as in Prepare, item 6, and sets its forces limit to what it chose of what the commander did not name: the transport force and the forces of item 6, where he named no task force; the units chosen, where he named no unit, or where it chose other units than those he named; and the source, where he named none. With all three named it is decision 0142's limit, the units chosen alone. [0149 item 3]
8. **Read as it binds.** Wherever the rules of this end read its forces limit for the task forces, the land units or the source (the transport force, the units and the source of the move and the second lift, the bombardment and carrier tasks, the escort), they read the commander's where he named it and the staff's limit on the "ready a landing" where he did not; and a forces limit on an intent under the end reads what it does not name of the three from the end, so read. The engine reads it so too, for the staff's orders, and a staff's `set_limit` on the task it formed may name under the end any of the three the end's limit does not name. [0149 item 3]
9. **What it could not find.** The reason of the choice gives each step in that order with its numbers: the estimate and the men wanted; each base that could be the source with its transport force, its berths, the men its lift gives and the hours of its passage, each base that could not and what it lacks, and the source and why; what the staff may not take and why (a unit or force that serves a higher intent is named with that intent); the units and the berths; where the lift falls short of the men wanted, by how many men; and the forces to bombard, to cover and to escort, or that it has none. With no base that can be the source the staff forms nothing, hands in `cannot_meet` once, and gives the reason each morning, with what is missing: a transport force it may take stopped at a base of the side, or a fighting unit beside it. With fewer men than wanted it does as Prepare, item 5, and Waits for its men: it waits for the day that brings them where one is in reach (at preserve the force and at balanced); otherwise at preserve the force it forms nothing and hands in `cannot_meet` once, at balanced it hands in `cannot_bring_enough` once, and at press it goes with what it has and says so. A lift as it stands is never waited for. A lack of a force to bombard, cover or escort stops nothing beyond what those rules already say. [0149 items 12 to 15]
10. **Chosen again.** On the first count of the enemy (The need to know, item 3) the units are chosen again at the source chosen and for the transport force of the task formed, from the units the end's limit binds and, where the commander named no unit, every fighting unit the staff may take ashore at the source. The source and the force stand: the task formed is the force's. [0149 item 4]

**Under "control an island".** A "take a place" that the staff forms under an island (Controlling an island, below) is the end of this section with these differences. [0151 items 4, 8a, 8c and 9]

- **A plan of its own.** It is drafted when the take is formed (`PlanDrafted`, after `IntentFormed`), is made ready on leave to prepare on the morning the take is formed, and is released and marked complete by this doctrine as a root take's is. It is not the island's plan; the island's plan ends it when the island is withdrawn (complete where released, else cancelled).
- **No carrier force and no bombardment force.** The take takes none (With no forces named, item 6 is set aside for carriers and for forces on the bombardment mission): the carrier tasks stand under the island's denial (Operating a carrier force, Under control an island), and the bombard tasks under its neutralising (Controlling an island, The bombardment forces); where a force on the bombardment mission lies stopped at a base of the side, the reason says "the bombardment forces are the island's, under its neutralising". The escort forces are taken as before. [0151 items 8a and 8c]
- **It chooses inside the island's limit.** With no forces limit of its own it chooses, by With no forces named, from what the island's limit (read as Controlling an island, Forces limits under an island, says) allows; its staff limit, on the "ready a landing", then names the whole choice: the transport force and the other forces, the units and the source.

**Ready.** A "ready a landing" whose force does not lie stopped at a base of its side can gain no loading and its units no rehearsal: the task is ready as it stands, whatever its loading and rehearsals, and its reason says what it goes without. So is one whose force lies at a base with every man of every unit on its roster aboard it and none ashore, a lift as it stands (below): it needs no making ready wherever it lies, and its reason says it is a lift as it stands. [0142 item 14; 0152 item 1, the architect's ruling of Nov 18, 2026]

**The need to know.** A take-a-place whose plan is preparing needs a count of the enemy at the place, by its knowledge limit where one is set on it or above it, and otherwise by its risk, each read up the tree (decision 0147, items 13 to 20, and decision 0159, item 12, `crates/sim-staff/src/look.rs`). The count asked for: at preserve the force an estimate of the place *seen from the air* or *by men in contact* (a *told* opening estimate never counts), dated no more than 3 days before the morning (the 3 days are a placeholder); at balanced such an estimate of any day; at press anything or nothing, and none is ever sent for. A knowledge limit asks as its setting says: a recent count as preserve does, with its own days; a count as balanced does; none as press does. Everything below that turns on what is asked turns on the setting where one is set, and the risk still sets the men wanted and the check of the men the ships carry at preserve the force (item 4).

1. **Who is sent.** On each morning the need is unmet and the side has no man of a fighting unit ashore in the place's hex, the staff sets one air group to the reconnaissance mission for the place (an order to the group, serving the end), where none the end may use is set so already: the lowest-numbered of the air groups the end's forces limit names, read up the tree, that the side may use, that the commander does not hold, that no cover, raid or strike task served earlier in the morning has taken, that can search (not a fighter, a search range above 0) and whose search range reaches the place from where it is based. Only the groups the limits name are read, never the groups aboard task forces they name. The order is given after the landing task is served, so on the forming morning the landing order comes first. The staff does this too where it could not form the landing for want of ships to carry the men wanted (below), so that the count can come. [0147 items 14 and 15]
2. **When the need is met** each group the end may use that is on reconnaissance of the place and that the commander does not hold is set to rest, by one order, and is the staff's to use again; but a group aboard the carrier force moved to look (Operating a carrier force, item 25) is not rested: it is its carrier task's. [0147 item 15; 0159 item 14]
3. **The choice made again.** On the first morning the staff holds a count seen from the air or by men in contact (dated the day before), while the plan is preparing and no man of a unit of the task is aboard, the units are chosen again as in Prepare, items 3 to 5, on that count. Where the choice differs the staff sets the forces limit of the "ready a landing" to it (`set_limit`), and that task orders the landing again with the new roster the same morning, at the cost the rules give a change of roster (chapter 7; the loading keeps half where a unit is added). An estimate of 0 asks for one unit, the first in the order. At preserve the force a choice that is short of the men wanted is not made: the limit is left as it is and the launch is held back (below). [0147 items 16 and 17]
4. **Not launched without it.** The landing is ready for leave and the release only when the need is met as well. Where a group is sent or flying, the day's reason says the landing waits for the count; so it does where a carrier force is moved to look and none of its groups reaches the place yet (Operating a carrier force, item 25). Where nothing can look (no group the end names that the side may use and the commander does not hold can search and reach the place, or the side's own men ashore there have given no count): where a recent count is asked (at preserve the force, or by the knowledge limit) the plan is not released, the staff hands in the notice `cannot_meet` on the first morning the landing is otherwise ready, once for the plan, and gives the reason each morning after, which says that nothing it may use can look and why; where a count is asked (at balanced, or by the limit) the plan goes, and the reason of its release says that it goes on no count, or on what it was told, or on the count it holds, with its grade and day. [0147 items 18 and 19]
5. **Reasons** give the estimate with its grade and its day, the count the risk asks for, the group sent with its range and its distance from the place, and what a new count changed in the choice. Where the knowledge limit sets what is asked they name it and the intent it is set on, and give the estimate's age in days ("150 men, seen from the air, of day 1, 2 days old"). A count held but older than asked is not met, and a group is sent again on that morning as on any morning the need is unmet; the units are chosen again only on the morning after a count is first held, and a second count of the same men changes no choice. [0147 item 20; 0159 item 12]
6. **The need as data.** On each morning a take's plan is preparing the staff works its need to know as data, from the side's own view and stored nowhere (decision 0159, item 13): the plan and the take; the place; what is asked (none, a count, or a recent count with its days) and the intent the knowledge limit is set on, or none where the risk sets it; what is held, the side's estimate with its men, grade, day and age in days on the morning, and what it could be in truth: where the count was seen from the air or by men in contact, from the grade's error range in the ground figures (`reconnaissance_error_low` and `_high`, `report_error_low` and `_high`; a report is the truth times a factor between the two), the truth lies between the count over the high end, cut to a whole man, and the count over the low end, raised to a whole man (none above where the low end is 0); none for a count that was told or where the figures give no range; who is looking after the morning's orders: where the need is met a group the commander holds on the mission, and otherwise the first group on reconnaissance of the place, with the day its order was given, or else the group the staff sends that morning, from that day, unless men of the side's own are ashore there or no count is asked; the carrier force moved to look and its hex, where its task stands under the take while the need is not met, or is formed that morning (Operating a carrier force, item 25); and whether the need is met. A plan that is released is no longer asked; a take that waits for its men as a draft (Waits for its men) has no need worked and nothing looks for it. A seat reads it in `plans` and `command_state` (The tools, below). [0159 item 13]

**Told to wait.** A take is not released while a limit "not before", "wait for" or "after" read up its tree is unmet (The three limits that tell a plan to wait, above; decision 0159, items 9 to 11; `crates/sim-staff/src/told.rs`). The staff reads them on each morning the landing is ready, the plan is preparing and the need to know is met (above), after the check that the place can be held (below) and before leave to launch is asked. [0159 items 9 to 11]

1. **Unmet.** "Not before" a day, on a morning before that day. "Wait for" a task force, until the side may use it (A unit before its day, above) and it lies stopped at the source the end's forces limit names, as it binds (With no forces named, below); a land unit, until the side may use it and it has men ashore in the source's hex. "After" another end, until, on a morning, its place is the side's (for an island, every place of it), read from the side's own view as Met reads it. [0159 item 9]
2. **Held.** While any is unmet the staff has readied the landing as it does with none (Prepare, Ready, The need to know) and does not release the plan: it stays preparing, leave to launch is not asked and no notice `waits_for_leave_to_launch` is handed in, and the morning's reason names each limit waited for, the intent it is set on where that is not the take, and what it waits for, with the day where there is one ("not to go before day 6"; "task_force:2 may be used from day 5"; "the place of intent:3 at (0,6) is not yet the side's"). On the first morning every one is met the take goes on as Leave to launch says. [0159 item 10]
3. **Never.** A limit that can never be met as it stands, an "after" naming an intent that is withdrawn and whose place is not the side's, or a "wait for" naming a task force the side no longer has (or one with no ship) or a land unit with no man ashore or aboard, is said once: the staff hands in the notice `cannot_meet`, once for the plan, its reason naming the limit, and gives the reason each morning after. The plan stays preparing and is not released until the commander clears or changes the limit. [0159 item 11]

**Leave to launch.** On each morning its landing is ready, the need to know is met (above), no limit tells it to wait (Told to wait, above) and the plan is preparing, the staff releases the plan (`approve`) where leave to launch is given; where it is withheld it gives no order and hands in the notice `waits_for_leave_to_launch`, with a reason that says the landing is ready and when, with leave on a morning, it would sail and land. Before the landing is ready the day's reason gives the loading, each unit's rehearsal and the morning it expects the landing to be ready, and no such notice is handed in. [0142 item 15]

**The check that it can be held.** Where a hold of a base in the place's hex is in force with a source in the forces limit on its chain (Holding a place, above), the staff works the check on each morning the landing is ready and the plan is preparing, before leave is asked or the plan released. The daily need is the daily charge of the men chosen. The days until supply follows are, in whole days: the days to land the men (the men's weight, `handling_t_per_man` a man, in the fullest ship, at the place's rate for a ship, the berth rate where its port has a berth and the lighter rate where it has none, rounded up to days and one at least), the passage back to the hold's source, one day to load, and the passage out, each passage at the cruise speed of the transport force's slowest ship, rounded up to days and one at least. The margin is the hold's, in days (10 at preserve the force, 5 at balanced, 0 at press). The supply that lands with the men is the daily need times those days and the margin, in place of the 15 days of the move below. The first run is taken to carry the daily need for 15 days. It can be held if the source's stock above its reserve is at least the supply that lands with the men and the first run together, and the room of the transport force (its ships' cargo capacity less any fuel carried as cargo) is at least each. The reason of the release, or of the morning it waits for leave, gives the check: the need, the days, the margin, the supply, the source's stock and the first run. Where it cannot be held the reason says what is short and by how much; at preserve the force (the end's risk) the staff does not ask leave and does not release: it hands in the notice `cannot_meet` once and gives the reason each morning the check fails; at balanced and at press it goes and says so. With no such hold nothing follows the landing, no check is made and the supply is the 15 days. [0144 items 10 to 17; the ruling on decision 0142's reading 8]

**Move.** Each morning the plan is released and no man of the side is ashore at the place, the staff takes one step, the first not yet done:

1. **The supply.** The force is to have aboard the daily charge of the men chosen (their men, times `daily_supply_t_per_man`, times each unit's weight factor) for 15 days, the interval of decision 0107, or for the days of the check that the place can be held where it is made (above); cut to what is aboard plus the source's stock above its reserve (none where the force does not lie at the source) and to what is aboard plus the ships' room. What is aboard counts toward it. Where more is to be taken the force is ordered to load supply to that weight (`load_to`); while that order stands and the load is not done it gives no order. With nothing to take it goes on to the next step the same morning. [0142 item 16]
2. **The men.** The lowest-numbered unit chosen with men ashore is set to attack, where it is not on attack, and the force is ordered to load it with no cargo; while that order stands and the unit still has men ashore it gives no order. A unit aboard already needs neither. [0142 item 17]
3. **The passage.** With every unit aboard and nothing more to take, the force is given a route from where it lies to the place and on to the source, marked to act at the place, and the unload order. The route and the area are as for a run: inside the intent's area read up the tree, where one is set. Each ship must have the fuel for the miles out and back at its class's rate (its fuel capacity over its endurance), as for a run. An escort is given before the route as for a run (Escorting and covering a run, above), with the place as the destination. A ship without the fuel, no route, or a run held for want of an escort stops it, and the reason says which. [0142 items 18 and 19]

So a force released on the morning of day R sails on day R plus the days to embark (one for the supply where there is any to take, one for each unit ashore) and is due on that day plus the days of the passage (its miles out at the cruise speed of its slowest ship, rounded up to whole days, at least one) less one. Once the force's route acts at the place, or it is landing there, it is given nothing more, and the "ready a landing" under the end is given nothing either, nor from the first morning a unit chosen has men ashore at the place: the landing spends what it made ready. [0142 items 18 and 20]

**A lift turned back.** Decision 0191, as amended on review and in the build (`crates/sim-staff/src/turn_back.rs`). Each morning, before the step of Move that routes the first lift to the place, the staff reads the danger to that lift and whether it has been hurt, and by the risk turns it back, keeps it back or lets it go on. It keeps no state: each morning is read from the force, the contacts and the side's events. [0191 items 1 to 23]

1. **Which lift.** The first lift (the force of the "ready a landing") of a take whose plan is released, with men aboard, not landing, and no man of a unit chosen ashore at the place. A supply run, a later lift and a force of a hold are not read. [0191 items 1 and 14]
2. **The danger** is a contact of the picture the take reads (decision 0134, 13) reported as a carrier, no more than 3 pulses old (the staff's figure for a fresh contact), within 8 hexes (320 nm, the staff's figure, a placeholder) of the lift's hex or of any hex of the engine's sea path from it to the place, along the route the take would give it. Each hex of the path counts, not only its corners; the way home does not. The nearest such contact makes the danger, then the newest, then the lowest number. A contact reported as another kind makes none, whatever it is in truth. The 8 hexes are this rule's and not the supply run's threat of 15 hexes, which is read at a destination. [0191 items 2, 17 and 18]
3. **Hurt.** The lift has been hurt when, since the plan was last released (its last `PlanStateChanged` to released), a ship has been sunk (`ShipSunk`) of the lift's own force, or of a force that keeps station on it as its escort (its charge now, or a `StationKept` on it since the release), or any carrier of the side. A ship put out of action does not count. It is read from the side's own events, and only at balanced. [0191 items 15 and 16]
4. **By the risk read up the tree:**

   | | Preserve the force | Balanced | Press |
   | --- | --- | --- | --- |
   | The lift turns back | On the danger | On the danger, once it has been hurt | Never |

5. **Turning back.** A lift under way to the place (the place ahead on its route) that turns back is given a route from where it is to its source (the end's source as its forces limit binds), not marked to act anywhere, cruising speed where it has another, and the order to do no cargo work (chapter 2, Cargo orders), which takes its unload order away. Its men stay aboard. The plan stays released and the intent in force. The staff hands in the notice `lift_turned_back` that morning. A lift turned back that reaches its source lies there with its men aboard. [0191 items 5, 9 and 13]
6. **Turned back** is read, not stored: a lift under way with men aboard whose route no longer has the place ahead. Each morning such a lift is kept back, with no order and no notice, while a contact that makes the danger is fresh, and on the first clear morning after: while one that would make it has been reported in the last 6 pulses and the risk would turn it back. On the second clear morning running Move's own step routes it to the place again from where it is, timed by night as a lift is, with the unload order. [0191 items 12, 14 and 19]
7. **Before it sails.** At the step of Move that sends a first lift with men aboard on its passage (from its source, or from the sea where it began there), the same test holds it: where a contact that makes the danger has been reported in the last 6 pulses and the risk would turn it back, it does not sail and the reason says it is held for the danger. So at preserve the force a lift does not first sail into danger, nor on the first clear morning after it; at balanced it sails unless it has been hurt; at press it sails. [0191 item 20]
8. **Not turned back.** On a morning a lift under way to the place is threatened and not turned back, the staff gives no order and says why: at press a lift is never turned back; at balanced it has not been hurt. [0191 item 8]
9. **Held.** A lift the commander holds is given nothing: on a morning the rule would turn it back or keep it back, the reason says it is held. [0191 item 10]
10. **The commander overrules** by setting press on the intent (under an island, on the island's intent, since a take's risk cannot be set bolder than the island's), or by an order of his own, which holds the force. The standing interrupt `lift_turned_back` stops the clock on the morning the notice is handed in. [0191 items 9, 21 and 22]
11. **Reasons.** The turning back gives the contact, "reported as a carrier", its age in pulses, its distance in hexes and miles, the risk and, at balanced, each ship lost with her force and day. The keeping back gives the contact and whether the danger stands or is within the last 6 pulses. The going on says the danger has passed for two mornings and gives the newest carrier contact near the way and its age, or that none is held. [0191 item 8]

**Land.** The landing is the unload order's (chapter 7). The phase begins when a man of the side is ashore at the place. [0142 item 21]

**Ashore.** On each morning the plan is released, the place is not taken and a unit chosen (one in the forces limit of the "ready a landing" under the end, a later lift's included) has men ashore at the place, the staff judges:

1. **The units of the operation** are the units chosen with men ashore at the place that the side may use and the commander does not hold. A unit held, by a hold or by an order of the commander's, is named in the reason and not counted.
2. **A unit can attack** if it is on attack already, or its fatigue is at or below the mark for the risk: a third of `attack_fatigue_limit` at preserve the force, two thirds at balanced, the limit itself at press, each cut to a whole number (the staff's shares, placeholders).
3. **Its fit men** are its men ashore times 100 less its fatigue over 100, cut to a whole man.
4. **The men wanted** are the side's estimate of the enemy's men at the place times 3, 2 or 1 by the risk, as for the choice, while more is coming to the place; with nothing more coming they are fewer (items 7 and 8). [0143 item 4; 0193 items 2 and 3]
5. **Attack.** Where no estimate is held, or the fit men of the units that can attack are at least the men wanted (items 4 and 8), each unit that can attack and is not on attack is set to attack, with no instruction for when it is spent. With no estimate the reason says it attacks with no count of the enemy. With nothing to order, one reason says so.
6. **Wait.** Otherwise, except where item 9 holds them in, each unit of the operation on attack is set to defend, and nothing else is ordered of the men; with none on attack, one reason. Where nothing more is coming, the staff hands in the notice of item 10. A unit the rules sent back to the defence is under the same judgement, and a unit that lands is under it from its first morning ashore.
7. **Is more coming.** On a morning an estimate is held and the fit men of the units that can attack are fewer than the men wanted while more is coming, the staff works whether more is coming to the place; on any other morning it does not, and the judgement is as items 1 to 6 say. More is coming where any of these holds, counted as Not to starve counts what is in reach:
   1. **A lift of the take has fighting men aboard a ship afloat:** its first lift, or a force the forces limit of its "ready a landing" names, whatever its route (under way, turned back or waiting).
   2. **A lift as it stands with fighting men aboard** that the end may send as a later lift (Forces with men aboard, item 3) may first be used by the end's last day.
   3. **A fighting unit of the take's forces limit** (the units chosen, and the units of the end's limit as it binds) is not ashore at the place, has no man aboard, is not held, is ashore at the source, can be used by the end's last day and can be carried. It can be carried where the first lift has a ship afloat, is bound for the source (the last hex of its route, or where it lies) and, where it lies stopped at the source, its ships can berth the units (A second lift) and, at balanced, Not to starve does not forbid that lift.

   The end's last day is its date where it has one, or the game's last day where that is earlier or the end has none: what is coming ends with the game. A unit or force before its day counts from its day: it is in time only where its day is no later than the end's last day; a world with no last day and an end with no date take every such unit as in time (the staff's seven days are counted from its day). A unit left out by the game's end is named in the reason with its day and the game's last day ("may be used from day 8, after the game's last day, day 6"); the reason's words of what was looked by name the end's date as before. A lift the commander holds is not counted. The arrival of a lift under way is not reckoned: a lift of the take with fighting men aboard counts whatever its passage. [0193 items 1, 10 and 11; 0194, 10]
8. **The men wanted with nothing more coming** are the estimate times 3 at preserve the force, 1 at balanced and 0 at press (the staff's figures, placeholders). The units that can attack are set to attack where their fit men are at least that, so at press every unit that can attack is set to attack whatever the estimate, and at preserve the force nothing changes: [0193 items 2 to 5]

   | | Preserve the force | Balanced | Press |
   | --- | --- | --- | --- |
   | Men wanted while more is coming | 3 times the estimate | 2 times | 1 times |
   | Men wanted with nothing more coming | 3 times: it waits | 1 times | None: it attacks |

9. **Once sent in, they stay in.** With nothing more coming and the fit men still fewer than the men wanted, at balanced and at press a unit on attack that the staff sent in under item 8 is not set back to defend, and no other unit is set to attack; one reason says the units on attack are committed, and no notice is handed in. The staff keeps no state for this: it reads its own reasons in the side's log, and a unit is sent in under item 8 where the staff's last order to it that the log holds is an attack whose reason is bound "sent in with nothing more coming". Any other unit on attack, a unit landed on attack by a lift made at a base among them, is set to defend as in item 6. The engine's own rule sends a spent unit back to the defence, and it is then judged again. At preserve the force the units on attack are set to defend as in item 6. [0193 items 12 and 13]
10. **The notice.** Where it waits with nothing more coming and no unit is committed, the staff hands in `cannot_bring_enough` on each morning that holds, not once for the plan, and once a morning for the plan (a second such notice that morning, from Not to starve, gives only its reason). Its standing interrupt fires as its table says: once for the plan, on the first such morning. At preserve the force, where units on attack are set to defend, the notice rests on a reason of its own. A commander may then set press, send more, or withdraw the intent. [0193 items 6 and 11]
11. **The last days.** The days left on a morning are the game's days (chapter 1, What a side may know) less the days resolved, never below 0: on the morning of the last day, 1; in a trial played past the game's end (chapter 9), 0. With fewer than 3 days left (the staff's figure, a placeholder) the staff begins no attack ashore, because an attack begun so late only costs men: [0194, 2 and 7 to 9]
    1. **No unit on defend is set to attack,** whatever item 5 or item 8 would do: whether the men are enough at the risk's number, nothing more is coming, or no estimate is held. One reason gives what the judgement gives, then the days left ("2 days of the game are left, fewer than 3") and that the staff begins no attack so late. No notice is handed in for this wait; a wait of item 6 with nothing more coming still hands in the notice of item 10.
    2. **A unit on attack whose side's attack at the place failed the day before** (chapter 7, An attack that fails: the side that failed is told so, cut to the hex) is set to defend, and its reason says that the attack failed and that the game is in its last days.
