This holds where item 5 would leave it on attack and where item 9 would keep it in.
    3. **A unit on attack whose attack did not fail stays in.** Where item 6 sets units on attack to defend for its own reason, it still does.
    4. **Nothing else of the staff reads the days left.** A lift still sails, a second lift and a later lift still go, a strike still flies. A unit set to attack as it goes aboard a first lift (A second lift) is set so whatever the days left, and is judged here on its first morning ashore.

    With 3 days left or more, and in a world given no length, the judgement and its words are as items 1 to 10 say. [0194, 7 to 9 and 11]

The reason gives the estimate and its day, the men wanted, each unit with its men, posture, fatigue against the mark and fit men, the fit men of those that can attack and how many are short, and what is coming. Where the test of item 7 finds nothing more coming, it adds what was looked for and that nothing more is coming (with what was not counted and why), the men wanted with nothing more coming and the fit men against them, and that the units attack, wait or are committed. [0143 items 1 to 8 and 15; 0193 items 6 and 11]

**A second lift.** The units left behind are the fighting units of the end's forces limit not chosen, that the side may use, that the commander does not hold, and whose men are all ashore at the source. On a morning of the judgement where the place is not taken, the transport force lies stopped at the source with nothing aboard and no estimate is held or the fit men of the units that can attack are fewer than the men wanted, the staff takes the units left behind in the order of the choice (the higher assault skill, the higher experience, the lower number), each by the force's ships in number order until their berths hold its men, until the fit men that can attack and the men taken are at least the men wanted or no ship is left; with no estimate, every unit the ships can carry. It adds them to the forces limit of the "ready a landing" by `set_limit` that morning, so the choice is made once and the commander may change it; at balanced, where the lift is short with too few in reach and its men cannot be fed, it adds nothing and orders nothing (Not to starve, above). The lift then goes one step a morning as the first did (Move, above): supply for its men for 15 days, a unit a morning by a load order, then the route and the unload order with an escort as for a run. A unit of a second lift is not set to attack before it sails, and the force goes as it stands, with no landing made ready, and the reason says so. Nothing more is sent once the place is taken or no unit is left behind; where the men are short and none is left, the reason says so, and the staff works whether more is coming and acts on it (Ashore, items 7 to 10). [0143 items 9 to 14; 0193 item 10]

**Forces with men aboard.** A **lift as it stands** is a transport force the staff may take (With no forces named, item 1) with men aboard a ship afloat. Its units are the units aboard it, whole; its source, for its way back and for the check that the place can be held, is its home port where that is the side's, else the nearest base of the side by the engine's sea path, the lower number on a tie. It is taken only when it is free: it has no route left to follow and is not landing, so a force under way on an order keeps it until it stops. [0152 item 1; the architect's ruling of Nov 18, 2026]

1. **The first lift.** With no forces named, the lifts as they stand with a fighting unit the staff may take aboard come before any base: the first lift is the lowest-numbered such force that is free, is not the lift of another take whose plan has not ended and was not sent by another intent that morning, and the base rule (item 3) is used only where there is none. The units chosen are its fighting units aboard, whole, and the men counted against the men wanted are theirs; the working units aboard land with them, are not units of the operation for the judgement Ashore, and the reason names them. [0152 items 2 and 3]
2. **Everyone aboard lands,** and the supply is reckoned for every man aboard. A lift that does not lie at its source, or whose source the staff may not take (decision 0149, item 5), takes nothing more: it goes with what is aboard, and the reason gives what it is short. A lift as it stands is ready as it stands (Ready, above). [0152 item 4]
3. **Later lifts.** On a morning the plan is released and the place is not taken, once the first lift is no longer on its way there (it is not landing there and the place is not ahead on its route, or it is lost), and no estimate is held or the fit men of the units that can attack are fewer than the men wanted (with no man of the operation ashore, none), the staff sends each other lift as it stands with a fighting unit aboard that is free, that no other take has as its lift, that no intent sent that morning and that is inside what the commander or the island named, in force order, on the first morning the side may use it, save at balanced one short of the men wanted with too few in reach whose men cannot be fed (Not to starve, above), which is not sent that morning; then the units left behind, as A second lift has it. Each later lift is given a route from where it lies to the place and on to its source, marked to act at the place, and the unload order, that morning: it has nothing to load. Its force and its fighting units are added to the forces limit of the "ready a landing" by `set_limit`, the force too, since its orders are checked against that limit. Its units are not set to attack before they sail. Each lift is its own force and goes when it can. While the first lift is on its way the later lifts wait, so each take in priority order is served on the first morning with one lift. On a morning the takes are served in priority order for every lift as it stands: the later lifts a take higher in priority sends that morning are set aside before any take lower in priority chooses its first lift, and a lower take forms nothing from them; its reason names each as a later lift of the higher take. A first lift with no ship afloat is lost, whether or not the engine still lists its force, and so is no longer on its way. The reason of the judgement gives each force with men aboard and why it is or is not sent. [0152 items 5 to 7, 5a and 5b]
4. **By night.** A later lift goes by night as a lift does (A run by night). [0152 item 9]
5. **Where a lift goes when it has landed.** A lift of "take a place", the first or a later one, whether as it stands or made at a base, and a force a hold sends (Holding a place, Forces with men aboard) is routed to the place and on to the source of the hold of that place, where a hold of a base in the place's hex is in force with a source in the forces limit on its chain (the lowest-numbered such hold) and every ship of the force has the fuel for the passage to the place and on to that source, by the engine's measure at the speed it goes (by night, the speed the night's choice gives, else cruising speed; by day, its own). Else it is routed back to its own source, where it came from, as before; where the two are one base nothing changes. The reason of the route names the base it goes on to and why, with each ship's fuel against the burn to the place and the burn on; where it goes back for want of fuel it gives the same figures. The source the island names on a morning is read by the take and the hold of that same morning, since the island's tree is formed first. Once it lies at the source with nothing aboard it joins the hold's pool (Controlling an island, The source kept, and its pool); a force with men left aboard or supply it did not land does not. Nothing else is done to it: a take whose first lift has gone on to another base than its own source makes no second lift with it from there. [0163 items 4 to 6]

**Reasons** carry the numbers in the order a staff works in: the estimate and the men wanted; each unit in its order with its assault skill, its experience and its men; the berths and the ships; each unit left out and why; the supply in tons; the days to embark and of the passage. **A proposal** shows the task formed, its units, ships, supply and days, and goes no further. [0142 items 27 and 28]

A side sees: its own orders, reasons and notices, whole. The enemy sees none. [0142]

## The plan order

Decision 0159, rule E (items 15 to 17), step 6 of the opening of stage 4, widened by decision 0164. For each intent of the commander's in force, and for each "take a place" in force that the staff formed (under a "control an island", say), the staff hands in a **plan order**: what it already works out of the plan, as data. It is worked when asked, from the side's own view and command state and the staff's own sums of that morning, and stored nowhere: it is not state, is not hashed or saved, and changes no order the staff gives. Asked at the start of a day it is the morning of that day's staff step, before that morning's decisions: the plan's state, its phases and its tasks are read from the command state as the morning finds it; what it waits for, the men, the day ashore and the need to know are the staff's working of that morning. [0159 items 15 and 16]

It has:

1. **The intent and its mission,** the intent in words as the staff's reasons state it; **the plan** and its state.
2. **The phases,** in order, each with the condition that ends it and whether it is done, under way or to come. A take's: **prepare**, ended by the release; **move**, ended by the first man of the side ashore at the place; **land**, ended when the place is the side's. A draft has all three to come; a plan preparing has prepare under way; released, move is under way until a man is ashore and land then, and all three are done once the place is the side's or the plan is complete. A hold of a base that is not the side's: **wait**, ended when the base is the side's, then **hold**, which does not end; a hold of the side's own base has hold alone. A hold's draft has its phases to come. Every other kind has no phases. [0159 item 16]
3. **What it waits for:** the things that must be so before a take's plan is released, while it is a draft or preparing, each with its kind, whether it is met and the day it is expected to be met where the staff can work one. Only what the staff waits on is listed, in this order: **men** (Waits for its men, above: the men wanted and to hand, expected on the day that brings them, with none where no day in reach does); **knowledge** (the count asked, where a count is asked and the staff holds the landing for it: met, or not met with a group looking, a force moved to look or a recent count asked; never with a day); the limits **a day** ("not before", expected that day), **a force** ("wait for" a task force or a land unit, expected on the day the side may use it where it lies at the source, else with no day) and **an intent** ("after", with no day), met or not, as Told to wait reads them; **leave** to prepare (of a draft) or to launch (of a plan preparing), where the commander withholds it, with no day. A count asked where nothing can look and the take goes all the same is not listed, nor a leave that is given. Once the plan is released it waits for nothing. [0159 item 15]
4. **The men wanted and to hand,** where the side holds a count of the enemy at the place: the count times 3, 2 or 1 by the risk (Prepare, item 5), against the men ashore and aboard of the units of the take's "ready a landing", or, before it is formed, the men to hand that the wait for men found. With no count, none. [0159 item 15]
5. **The day it expects to be ashore,** by the sums a take uses for a latest day: the morning the landing would be ready (Ready, and the projection of Readying a landing; for a take that waits for its men, a landing begun on the day that brings them, with that day's lift), or the latest day a thing waited for is expected where that is later, plus the days to embark and of the passage (Move), less 1. A released plan counts from the morning asked, with the days to embark it then has. None where a thing waited for has no day, where men of the side are ashore at the place or the place is the side's.
6. **The need to know,** for a take whose plan is preparing, as The need to know, item 6, gives it.
7. **The tasks:** the staff's intents in force under it, each with its kind and its phase: a "ready a landing" under a take is the prepare phase's, a "bombard a base" the move phase's, any other the phase under way.

A day on which a task had no order is not in the plan order: it stays in the task's reasons. [0159 item 16]

The plan order of a take the staff formed is worked exactly as that of a commander's take, items 1 to 7: it has its own phases, what it waits for, its men, its day ashore, its need to know and its tasks. No other intent of the staff's has a plan order. The orders are given in the number order of their intents, the commander's and the staff's takes together. The order of the commander's own intent is unchanged by it, and still lists the take among its tasks. [0164 items 1, 2 and 4]

The tool `order` gives it (The tools, below) and the table's plan sheet shows it. It tells a side nothing it does not already have. [0159 item 17]

## Bombarding a base

The task "bombard a base" and the doctrine at the task force's level that serves it. It runs last in the staff's morning (The day's order, below), after "take a place", whose landing force's due day it reads. It gives orders to a task force and draws no random number. The engine's half is the shore bombardment (chapter 4, Shore bombardment) and the halt at a marked waypoint (chapter 2). [0146]

**The task** (`bombard`, code 14) names a task force of the side and a base on the side's map that is not the side's, the target; intake refuses a base that is not (`not_an_enemy_base`) and a force not the side's. It has no date. It is addressed to the force. A commander may state it as a root; it then needs a plan of its own, made ready and released by the leaves. [0146 item 1]

**Forming.** Under an end whose plan is released, for each task force on the bombardment mission with a ship afloat that the nearest forces limit on the end's chain names, in task force order, where no bombard task for that force is in force anywhere on the side (a force before its day is left out): under **neutralise a base**, at that base; under **take a place**, at the enemy's lowest-numbered base on the side's map in the place's hex, and none where there is none (intake refuses one whose target is not in the place's hex, `cannot_stand_under`). Where one stands, a decision to give no order names it. A force on the bombardment mission is given no stand task. Under "control an island" a neutralising formed under the island takes no force by this rule: the bombardment forces are the island's (Controlling an island, The bombardment forces). [0146 items 2 and 3; 0151 item 8c]

**The force's morning.** Each morning, for each bombard task in force whose plan is released, in priority order, the staff decides at the force's level from the side's view, as the stand doctrine does (Standing at a hex by night, above), with these differences:

1. **The force** gone, before its day, held by the commander, or with no ship afloat: one reason and no order, and for a force gone or with no ship afloat the notice that the intent cannot be met.
2. **The target.** Where the target is not a base of the enemy's on the side's map (it is the side's own now, or gone from the map), the force is given no route, is set to normal readiness where it is not, and the reason says so. No notice is handed in. [0146 item 4]
3. **Radio silence, the home port, away from home and the rules of engagement** are the stand's: silence at preserve the force where reports travel; the base it lies at stopped as its home port where it has none; no order away from its home port; the stance engage and the break-off level of the risk, set once. [0146 items 8 and 9]
4. **Whether it sorties tonight.** As a root or under neutralise: at press and at balanced every night; at preserve the force every night on which the force's own picture holds no contact of ships within 15 hexes of the target that reports more ships than the force has afloat. Under take a place: on the morning of the day the landing force is due at the place, and on no other. That day is, on the morning the landing force is routed (an order of the same morning), that day plus the days of the passage at the cruise speed of its slowest class less one (decision 0142, item 18); while it is under way to the place, the day the engine's forecast brings it there; before it is routed it is not known and the force waits; once it has come to the place there is none. [0146 item 5]
5. **The speed and the route.** From its home port, routed to the target's hex and home, the target's hex marked to act at; at cruise, then full speed, the first that brings it there within the night's 12 hours, ordered where it differs from the force's. Where neither does, there is no sortie and the notice that the intent cannot be met is handed in. [0146 item 6]
6. **Fuel and ammunition first,** by the stand doctrine's rule (the passage out and home, by the engine's measure, times the risk's margin) and decision 0127 (Ammunition as policy, below): a force short of either does not sortie, and its reason gives the day from which it may be used, or none and the notice that the intent cannot be met, on each morning it holds. [0146 item 7]
7. **Readiness:** battle on a morning it sorties, normal on one it does not. The orders are in the stand's order: silence, home port, rules of engagement, readiness, speed, route. [0146 items 8 and 9]
8. **It fires and comes home by the rules as they are:** the force halts in the target's hex, fires in the night's bombardment phase, and steams on at the start of the morning pulse. [0146 item 10]
9. **Reasons** give the target and its distance, the hours to it, the fuel aboard against the fuel needed, each ship's rounds against the level of the risk, and, at preserve the force, the contact that stopped it. [0146 item 11]

A side sees: its own `IntentFormed`, staff reasons, orders and notices, whole. The enemy sees none of it.

## Controlling an island

Decision 0151, step 8 of stage 3, second part. The end a commander states alone, from which the staff draws the tree of ends the earlier steps built: take a place, hold a place, keep it supplied, deny the waters, operate the carriers and neutralise the enemy's bases near. Its state is one kind of intent; the engine gains three things for it, each the architect's addition on the build's finding: the staff's withdrawal of an intent it formed, plans of their own for two intents formed under an island, and the reading of forces limits under one (below). It draws no random number. The staff reads only the side's own view: its bases, forces and units, its map of the enemy's bases, and every intent in force and its plan. [0151]

**An island** is a scenario's data. A `[[island]]` table has a `number`, a `name` and `places`, in order, each a base, `{ base = N }`, or a coast hex, `{ hex = [q, r] }`. A place that is a base means that base's hex. An island is on both sides' maps, as the map is: it is map data, it never changes in a game, and it is not state and not in the hash. The loader refuses an island with no place, a number used twice, a base place that names no base of the scenario, a hex place off the map or not a coast hex, and a place with both keys or neither. [0151 items 1 and 2]

**The end.** "Control an island" (`control_island`, code 15) names an island and may have a date, `by_day`. It is an end addressed to the area and a root. `set_intent` of an island that is not the scenario's is refused as "No such island" (Every reason a command is refused). It needs no forces limit. Its plan is drafted, prepared and released by the leaves, as a hold's is (Holding a place), and stands until the intent is withdrawn: **the intent is never marked complete**. Under it the staff may form a take a place, a hold a place, a deny an area and a neutralise a base, and under the denial an operate a carrier force. Each take is formed with the island's `by_day`, and the rule of a take's date is decision 0142's. The staff sets no risk: each intent beneath reads the island's, up the tree. [0151 items 3, 4 and 7, 12]

**Plans of their own.** A tree has one plan, its root's, but for two intents formed under an island, each of which has a plan of its own, drafted when it is formed (`PlanDrafted`, after `IntentFormed`). Everything else formed under the island shares the island's plan. So the rule that a sub-intent serves its root's plan reads: the plan of the nearest intent on its chain that owns one. [0151 item 9]

| Intent | Its plan |
| --- | --- |
| A "take a place" formed under the island | Made ready on leave to prepare the morning it is formed, and released and marked complete by the take's doctrine as a root take's is (Taking a place) |
| A "keep supplied" formed under a "hold a place" that stands under the island | Made ready and released by the supply doctrine on the leaves, as a root supply intent's is. With no forces limit of its own, its forces limit and source, and those of a run, are read up the tree: the hold's, set by the staff |

Withdrawing the island ends each such plan: complete where it was released, else cancelled. [0151 item 9]

**Forces limits under an island.** The commander may set one on the island or on anything beneath it, and the staff's choices are inside them. [0151 item 4]

- Each limit above an intent binds only what it names of the task forces (with ships), the land units and the source, as under a take. The narrowing check of decision 0149 is used under an island too.
- When a chain is read, the island's own limit fills what it does not name from the nearest limit beneath it, and with none beneath it is read as set.
- A forces limit that names no air group does not bind the air groups: a hold's cover and every doctrine under the island that uses the groups at a base use the groups based there as with no limit. A limit that names groups narrows them as before (decisions 0117 item 8, 0120 item 7, 0123 item 3).
- A take under an island with no limit of its own chooses inside the island's limit, and its staff limit then names the whole choice: the transport and other forces, the units and the source (Taking a place, Under "control an island").

**The tree each morning.** The doctrine runs right after the leaves and before every other air doctrine (The day's order). Each morning the island's plan is released the staff forms what is missing, by `form_intent`, in the island's place order and in this order. An intent of the same kind and place in force anywhere on the side, whoever made it, counts: none is formed beside it, and where it stands in another tree the reason names it, with its number and whose it is. [0151 items 5 and 6]

| Order | Formed | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1, for each named place in the island's order | **Take a place**, with the island's date | The side holds no base at the place, and no take of it is in force with a plan that has not ended |
| 2, for each named place | **Hold a place** | Of the side's lowest-numbered base at the place; else the enemy's lowest-numbered base there on the side's map, which waits until it is taken (Holding a place, A place not yet the side's). A bare hex has no hold until the side has a base there |
| 3 | **Deny an area**, once | Within 3 hexes of the island's first place. The 3 hexes are a placeholder |
| 4, under the denial | **Operate a carrier force**, for each force | Each carrier force the staff may take (decision 0149, item 5) that has a carrier afloat with a group aboard, at the covering hex of decision 0148, item 2, reckoned to the island's first place (Operating a carrier force, item 20) |
| 5 | **Neutralise a base**, for each enemy base in base order | The enemy base is on the side's map within 15 hexes of a named place. The 15 hexes are the placeholder of decision 0124 |
| 6, in the bombard step, under the first neutralising of an enemy base at a named place | **Bombard a base**, for each force | Each force on the bombardment mission the staff may take, with a ship afloat, stopped at a base of the side (The bombardment forces, below) |

Under one root nothing is kept from a sibling: a unit or a force serves the first intent, in this order, that takes it that morning, and a ship serves the run or the lift that has it until it is back. The rule of what the staff may take (decision 0149, item 5) keeps a unit, a force or a base from an intent of another root only. What follows is the built doctrines': each take is prepared, launched on leave and landed; each hold puts its men on the defence, covers its base and forms its supply; the check that a place can be held is made before a take is launched; the denial and the neutralising use what their doctrines find and say so where they find nothing. A base in a named place's hex that a take of this tree is to take is neutralised too, until it is taken. [0151 items 5 to 9]

**The staff withdraws.** On the first morning a base the staff is neutralising under the island is the side's, and before the tree is formed that morning, the staff withdraws the intent it formed for it (`withdraw_intent` by the staff, on an intent the staff made). [0151 item 8b]

**The source of supply** of the island's holds is the commander's, where the island's forces limit names one, whatever it holds. Else it is the side's base that is in no named place's hex and that the staff may take, with the most supply above its reserve; then the lower number. A base with nothing above its reserve cannot be the source, and no transport force need lie there. On the morning it forms a hold, the staff sets that hold a forces limit of the source and the transport forces stopped there with nothing aboard, which may be none. With no such base it sets none, says so in its reason, and the hold forms no supply (Holding a place, The supply that follows). With no ship in the limit, a "keep supplied" formed under the hold is served and says that no ship of its pool is afloat, and sends nothing. [0151 item 8; 0163 items 1 to 3]

**The source kept, and its pool.** Where the commander names no source, the source is chosen on the first morning a base fits, which with any base of the side holding supply above its reserve is the first morning of the island, and kept: on later mornings the source of a hold the staff formed under the island, while it is the side's base, is the source. Each morning the staff sets the forces limit of each hold it formed under the island to the source and the transport forces it may take that lie stopped there with nothing aboard, where that differs from the limit the hold has. The pool is the transport forces stopped at the source with nothing aboard (no supply, fuel or man in any ship); on the morning the source is named it may be none. A force found there on a morning joins by a command of that morning, and a "keep supplied" served that morning reads the limit as it stood, so its first run with that force is ordered the morning after. The lifts that have landed go on to the source to join it (Taking a place, Forces with men aboard, item 5). The pool only grows: a force once named stays while it has a ship afloat, so that a ship that serves a run, loaded or under way, stays inside the limit its orders are checked against; a convoy the side may use from a later day joins the pool on that day. [0152 items 10 and 11; 0163 items 1, 2 and 5; the growing pool, the architect's ruling of Nov 18, 2026]

**The carriers** are the island's. A take under an island takes no carrier force, and the force is not sent home when a take is met. The carrier tasks under the island's denial are served after the landing's morning, with the takes' carrier tasks, by the carrier doctrine as it is; and the strike on the place applies to such a task for the first take of the tree (Operating a carrier force, Under "control an island"). [0151 item 8a]

**The bombardment forces** are the island's too. A take under an island takes no force on the bombardment mission. Each morning, in the bombard step (Bombarding a base), under the island's **first neutralising of an enemy base at a named place** (for each place in the island's order, the neutralising in force under the island of the lowest-numbered enemy base on the side's map in its hex), the staff forms "bombard a base" for each task force on the bombardment mission with a ship afloat that the staff may take (decision 0149, item 5; inside the island's forces limit where it names task forces) and that lies stopped at a base of the side, in task force order, where no bombard task for that force is in force anywhere on the side. A force the staff may not take, one before its day among them, is named with the reason; a force that may be used only from a later day is so taken on that day's morning. A bombard task the island's own tree formed stands without a word; one in another tree is named. No other neutralising under the island takes a bombardment force, so a base off the island is not bombarded by this. The task is then the bombard doctrine's as it is: whether it sorties, its speed, its fuel and its rounds. When the base is taken, the neutralising is withdrawn (The staff withdraws) and the bombard task with it. [0151 item 8c]

**Its measure.** It is worked from the side's own view and events, stored nowhere, as a hold's measure is (Holding a place, Its measure), and no rule reads it. A day is **controlled** when, at its end, every named place is the side's (a place that names a base is the side's when that base is: a base formed by a landing beside the enemy's does not make it so; a bare hex is when the side holds a base there), and in each place the summed supply of the side's bases there is above the reserves of those bases, and the side's days of supply in the hex is at least the floor of the "keep supplied" under the place's hold (5 days, a placeholder, where there is none). A place holds a base of the side on a day where the side's own events show `BaseAtDayEnd` that day for a base in the hex. The days of supply are the stock over today's daily use of the hex, and today's use is applied to past days. [0151 items 10 to 12]

| Part | What |
| --- | --- |
| Days in force | As for a hold's measure |
| Days controlled | Of those days, the controlled days |
| Each place | Its hex, whether it is the side's, and its days of supply today |

It is shown in `command_state` (as `measure` on the island intent) and in `report` (as an entry of `islands`), with the words "controlled on N of M days in force", then each place with its hex, whether it is the side's and its days of supply today. [0151 item 11]

A side sees: its own `IntentFormed`, `PlanDrafted`, staff reasons, orders, notices and the measure, whole. The enemy sees none of it. [0151]

## Patrolling waters with submarines

Decision 0218, stage 3. The end "patrol the waters with submarines" and the doctrine at the area's level that serves it: it gives each submarine the order may use a station in the waters and orders her there. It draws no random number, keeps no state of its own and adds no rule of the engine but the ones the next paragraphs name (a hold on a boat, a boat in a forces limit). The boats and their station order are chapter 10's. The doctrine version is `supply-55` and the rules label 30. A side with no patrol intent gives the orders and reasons `supply-54` gave. [0218, rules 11, 12, 34 and 54]

**The end.**

1. **The statement.** A patrol intent (code 16, `patrol`) names a hex and a distance in whole hexes and no base, as a deny does. It has no date and is never marked complete. It is given to the area. Its words are "patrol the waters within N hexes of H with submarines" ("1 hex" for a distance of 1). A hex not on the map is refused as "Not on the map". [0218, rule 11]
2. **A root only.** A commander states it; the staff cannot form it and nothing stands under it. A `form_intent` of a patrol is refused as "Cannot stand under", for it stands under no kind, and no kind stands under it. A patrol is not a deny: no doctrine of a deny, a strike or a cover reads its waters, and no other doctrine reads it. [0218, rule 11]
3. **Its plan and leaves** are any root's: a draft is made ready on leave to prepare and released on leave to launch, and the doctrine serves the order only when its plan is released. Until it is released the staff gives no order for the patrol. The ranking among patrols is the rank read up the tree, 1 first, then those with none, each by number (The intent tree, Order among intents). [0218, rule 11]
4. **The waters** are the hexes on the map within the order's distance of its hex, land and sea, in hex order (q, then r). Only those that ships can enter take a station.
5. **The boats of the order.** The forces limits on the patrol's chain that name at least one boat bind it, and a boat is one the order may use when every one of them names her: the intersection. A forces limit that names no boat does not bind the boats (as one that names no air group does not under an island, Controlling an island). Where no limit on the chain names a boat, the boats of the order are every boat of the side's own. They are taken in boat number order, and the boat of place n, counted from 1, has the nth station of the waters. A boat the side does not own is never named: such a limit is refused as "Not your submarine". [0218, rules 12 and 34]
6. **Only a patrol may use a boat.** An order to a boat from an intent that is not a patrol in force, or from a patrol that may not use her, is refused as outside the forces limit (Holds and the override; The forces limit on a staff's orders). [0218, rule 34]

**The stations** (the written order of the waters' hexes, decision 0218, rule 54). From the navigable hexes of the waters (hexes ships can enter), in hex order:

7. **The first station** is the centre of the waters where ships can enter it; else the navigable hex of the waters nearest the centre, the lowest hex where they tie. **Each next station** is the navigable hex of the waters not yet a station that is farthest from the nearest station already given, the lowest hex where they tie. When every navigable hex of the waters is a station and boats remain, the stations begin again from the first: the boat of place n then has station ((n - 1) mod the number of stations) + 1. No station is on land. [0218, rule 54]
8. **Waters with no navigable hex** give no station: the staff gives no order and one reason says so. **An order with no boat** (the side has none, or the limits name none the side owns) gives no order and says so. [0218, rule 54]

**The boats it passes over.** Each boat of the order is tried in place order. A boat that cannot serve is passed over, and **the stations do not move up**: her station goes to no one, and the boat after her keeps the place she had. A boat cannot serve this morning if she is:

- sunk, or home (a boat gone home is off the map; she still holds her place in the count);
- not yet to be used: held apart until a later day ("enters on day N") or before the day she may be used from ("may be used from day N");
- damaged, or going home;
- held from the staff: a hold made by a `hold` command or by the commander's order, with the hold's words;
- used by a patrol order served before this one this morning ("used by intent:N"): the patrols are served in priority order, and a boat that a higher patrol gave a station to, whether it ordered her or she kept it, is passed over by the lower; or
- where this order names no boat, named in the forces limit of another intent in force ("used by intent:N"): a boat a limit names is another order's. Where the order does name its boats, it keeps one a limit of another intent names too. [0218, rules 12, 34 and 54; the code, differences rows 69 to 72]

**The orders.**

9. **A boat whose station already is the station her place gives her** is given no order. Every other boat the order uses is given a `station` order to her station, issued by the staff, serving the patrol, with a reason that names the boat, her place, where she is and her station, the waters and how the station was chosen ("the centre of the waters", "station 2 of the waters, 2 hexes from the nearest before it", "station 3 of the waters again, the boats being more than the hexes"). The engine takes it in as any station order (chapter 10, The station order): she goes by the sea route a force takes. [0218, rule 54]
10. **One decision to give no order**, with one reason, names every boat that keeps her station and every boat passed over with why and the station that went to no one. It is given only where there is at least one of either. [0218, rule 54]
11. **A boat that has gone home is not sent out again:** she is off the map (the words of the first record on sending her out are withdrawn). A boat that is damaged is not repaired; she is passed over until she is sunk or goes home. [0218, rule 54]

**The preview and the proposal.** A preview or a `propose` of a patrol order, as if released, gives in its proposal `stations`: for each boat that is given a station or keeps hers, in place order, the boat, her station and her place. A boat passed over is not in the list; her reason is in the decision to give no order. The lines of the proposal say each boat "would be ordered to station H (how chosen)" or "keeps her station at H". [0218, rule 54; the agent interface, version 48]

**Holds and boats** (decision 0218, rules 12 and 34).

12. **A boat may be held.** A `hold` or `release` names a submarine of the side's own, not sunk; a boat that is not the side's, does not exist or is sunk is refused as "Not your submarine" (one reason for all), a boat already held as "Already held", and `release` of a boat not held as "Not held". A hold on a boat is a record like any hold: it keeps the staff from ordering her, it moves and stops nothing, and the engine refuses a staff's order for her as "held" before the check of the forces limit. [0218, rule 34]
13. **A commander's order to a boat holds her** from that morning, by the override, when some intent in force may use her (a patrol in force whose forces limits allow her; the plan's state is not looked at) or any intent in force names her in its forces limit. The boat gets a `HeldByOrder` event, citing the order and its acceptance. An order to a boat that no intent in force may use or names holds nothing: the staff has no claim on her. Inside a day the same holds as for any unit (Holds and the override). [0218, rule 12]
14. **A forces limit names boats** with `set_limit` forces and `submarines`. Any intent in force may carry a limit that names a boat; only a patrol may use her (rule 6), and a limit that names a boat on another intent makes her another order's for a patrol that names none (rule 9). A limit on an intent with a parent that names a boat is refused as "Wider than its parent" unless every boat is named in the parent's limits read up the tree, or no limit on the parent's chain names a boat. [0218, rules 12 and 34]

**The staff's regard for submarine waters** (decision 0218, rule 55; doctrine `supply-55`). **Submarine waters,** as the deciding command's picture holds them, are the hexes inside any suspected waters it holds (within the waters' distance of their hex, by hex distance), and the hexes within one hex of any report of a submarine it holds. The picture is the area command's, which is where the carrier pass reads: a belief the command was not told is not read. With no belief of submarines no hex is in them, and nothing here changes a word. Three places a doctrine chooses are regarded, and no other:

15. **A carrier station** (Carriers serving an order for an end). The candidates are tried in the risk's order and the first in fuel is the station. With the regard, a candidate in fuel that lies in submarine waters is passed over for the next in that order that is in fuel and lies outside them. The reason names each candidate passed over and why ("H passed over: inside the suspected submarine waters within N hexes of H0" or "within one hex of a report of a submarine at H0, day D"). A candidate that is not in fuel is not tried for the waters. Where no candidate in fuel lies outside them, the station is the first in fuel, as the rule gave it; the reason says "no place outside submarine waters serves, so the station is H" and why it lies in them, and the reason carries the caution `station_in_submarine_waters` (Cautions, below). A nearer station passed over is not named as passed over for the danger of its circle where it was passed over for the waters. [0218, rule 55]
16. **The covering hex** (Operating a carrier force, item 20, and under "control an island", item 25): the first hex along the shortest sea route to the place within the reach is taken as the first not in submarine waters; those passed over are named in the reason; where all of them are in them, the first, and the reason says no place outside serves. Where the force already is within the reach (the covering hex is the hex it lies in) there is no other hex, and the reason says so where that hex is in submarine waters. [0218, rule 55]
17. **The hex a force is moved to look from** (Operating a carrier force, item 25 under "take a place"): the first hex along the route within the longest search range, regarded as the covering hex is. [0218, rule 55]
18. **What is not regarded.** The way a force takes (decision 0203, The way a force takes) and every route are as they were: a route is not bent for submarine waters, and the hexes it passes are not regarded. The hex a force closes on a contact at is not regarded. No doctrine chooses the hex a force stands at by night: the stand's hex is the order's, so the regard has no place there. [0218, rule 55; differences row 73]

**Reasons and the proposals.** A patrol order's reasons say the waters and the count of their hexes, each boat and her place, and why a boat is passed over; none shows an enemy boat's truth. The staff reads only the side's own boats and the area command's picture. [0218, rules 54 and 55]

A side sees: its own patrol intent, the staff's `station` orders for its boats and their reasons and refusals, its own holds on a boat and `HeldByOrder`, whole, and, in a `propose` or a preview, the stations a patrol order would give. The enemy sees none of it. [0218, rules 13, 16, 54 and 55]

## The tools

A seat whose side has a staff commands through these tools beside the others, in `sim-agent` and through the MCP server and `play`. Every seat of a game those open has one; the staff does nothing for a side until an intent is set, and for a supply intent until it has a forces limit. A session made with no staff has none of them. The agent interface is version 24 since decision 0151 (below), and was version 23 from decision 0149, 22 from decision 0147, 21 from decision 0146 and 20 from decisions 0143 and 0144. It was version 19 since decision 0142, which adds the kind `take_place` (`hex`, or `base` for an enemy base on the side's map, and `by_day`) and an intent's `phase` in `command_state`. It was version 18 from decision 0141, which adds its two kinds (`ready_landing` with `task_force`, `hex` and `by_day`; `build_up` with `base`, `wants` and `defences`), a forces limit's `land_units`, a hold's `land_unit` and the notices `ready`, `late` and `met`. It was version 11 (version 7 had the four tools of step 4, and `interrupts` in `command_state`; version 8 added the intent tree, the kinds `cover` and `hold`, air groups in a forces limit, a hold on a whole air group, a reason's chain and the measure of a hold intent; version 9 added the kinds `neutralise` and `raid`, the `target` of an intent, an intent's `base` that is null for a neutralise, a reason's `level` and the measure of a neutralise intent; version 10 added the kinds `deny`, `strike_ships` and `search_waters`, a deny intent's `hex`, `distance` and `measure`, and the air group order `set_strike_sizing`; version 11 adds the kinds `stand_at` and `operate_carriers`, with a `task_force` and a `hex` and a `base` that is null, a route order's `stand_at` and a force line's, a reason's `level` that may be a task force's, and the `pulse` a halt or steam-on choice is asked for). [0107 items 46, rulings 25 and 36 to 37; 0117; 0120; 0123; 0124; 0125]

Since decisions 0143 and 0144 (agent interface version 20): `set_intent` of a `hold` may name an enemy base on the side's map, which the staff waits for; the staff's `form_intent` may be `keep_supplied` under a hold, written and read with `floor_days` (0 allowed) and `interval_days` where set; the command report and the export's `staff_notice` event name the intent of a take-a-place's `met` notice, whose words are "intent:N is met". [0143; 0144; the ruling on decision 0142's reading 4]

Since decision 0159, rule C (agent interface version 25, fields added): `set_limit` takes `limit` `not_before` with `from_day`, `wait_for` with one of `task_force` and `land_unit` (in the side's numbers; a number naming none of the side's is refused as for a forces limit), and `after` with `other_intent`; each with its other key left out clears the limit. `command_state` gives on every intent's own `limits` `not_before`, `wait_for` (`{"task_force": n}` or `{"land_unit": n}`) and `after`, each null where unset, and in `limits_read_up` `not_before` with `not_before_from` (the latest day on the chain and the intent it is set on) and `wait_for` and `after` as lists, nearest first, each with the intent it is set on (`from`). [0159 item 9]

Since decision 0190 (agent interface version 35): `set_limit` takes `limit` `targets` with `kinds` (one to six kinds of ship by name, each once, most wanted first; left out or empty clears the limit) and `waters_first` (true or false, false where left out; true only on a deny or an intent beneath one). `command_state` gives an intent's own `limits` a `targets` (`{"kinds", "waters_first"}`) and its `limits_read_up` `targets` and `targets_from`, the nearest on the chain and the intent it is set on, each left out where none is set. The air group order `set_strike_first_waters` with `hexes` is in chapter 5, Air group orders, and an air group's line shows `strike_first_waters` when it is not empty. [0190]

Since decision 0159, rule D, items 12 and 13 (agent interface version 25, fields added): `set_limit` takes `limit` `knowledge` with `knowledge` (`none`, `count` or `recent_count`) and, with `recent_count` alone, `within_days` (3 where left out); `knowledge` left out clears it. `command_state` gives on every intent's own `limits` `knowledge` (`{"setting", "within_days"}`, null where unset) and in `limits_read_up` `knowledge` with `knowledge_from`, the nearest on the chain and the intent it is set on. `plans` and `command_state` give every plan `need_to_know`: for a take whose plan is preparing that morning, `intent`, `place`, `asked` (`setting`, `within_days` and `from`, null where the risk sets it), `held` (`men`, `came_from`, `day`, `age_days` and `could_be` with `lowest` and `highest`, or null), `looking` (`air_group` in the side's numbers and `since_day`, or null), `met` and `text`, the need in one line; null for any other plan. [0159 items 12 and 13]

Since decision 0151 (agent interface version 24): `set_intent` takes `intent_kind` `control_island` with `island` (required; the island's number in the scenario) and `by_day`, and no `base`, `target`, `task_force` or `hex`; an island the scenario does not have is refused as "No such island" ("island N is not an island of the scenario"). `command_state` gives `island` on every intent (null but for a control_island) and, for a control_island, its `measure` (Controlling an island, Its measure). `report` gains `islands`, the measure of each island intent in force with its intent, its island and its words. The staff's `withdraw_intent` is in a hand-in, on an intent it made. [0151]

Since decision 0214 (agent interface version 43): `set_pursuit` takes `intent` and `leave` (`ask`, `go` or `stay`; left out or null clears it) and `answer_pursuit` takes `task_force`, `contact` (the commander's number of the contact the asking named) and `allow` (true for yes, false for no); each queues one command for the coming day, taken in at its first step and answered queued or refused with the engine's reason (an intent not in force; a force that is not the side's). `set_interrupt` takes the standing kind `asks_leave_to_pursue`, and `acknowledge` takes it with `task_force`. `command_state` gives each intent's `pursue` (the leave as read up the tree, `from`: the intent it is set on or `"default"`, and whether one is `set`), a `pursue_meaning` in words, the side's `pursuits` (each force, whether `answer` or `taken`, the contact and the day it began) and its `pursuit_refusals` (force and contact). The notices `asks_leave_to_pursue`, `pursues` and `pursuit_ended` are in `report` and the briefing, each force's a matter of its own, with the force, the order and the find. `run` and `advance` stop in the evening with `stopped` of `asks_leave` and an `asking` that lists each asking: the `task_force`, the `intent` it would leave, the `contact`, its `kind` and `age_pulses`, where it was `reported_at`, where it would go (`to`), the `hours` there and the `interrupt`; chapter 1, The stop in the evening. `run` does not stop after a day for the firing of that interrupt. A trial carries the settings and not the answers (item 40). The tools are the interface's, not rules. [0214, rule 18]

- `command_state`, `plans`: what stands, as the coming day will find it, with each intent's figure and first day below, and yesterday's staff decisions with their numbers for `why`. `command_state` lists every intent, each root by number with the intents beneath it after it, and gives each its kind and base, its `parent`, `maker`, `addressee`, `children` and `depth`, its `limits` as set on it and its `limits_read_up` (each of forces, risk, area and priority as the staff reads it, with the intent it is read from and, where none is set, what then holds), for a hold intent its `measure` (Holding a place, above), for a neutralise intent its `measure` (Neutralising a base, below), for a deny intent its `measure` (Denying an area, above), its `hex` and `distance` for a deny intent (`base` is null for a deny too), its `target` for a neutralise or a raid intent (`base` is null for a neutralise), its `task_force` (the side's number; null for the other kinds) and its `hex` (the station) for a stand-at or an operate-carriers intent (`base` is null for these too), and notes for a supply intent given to the area with no forces limit and for each intent the staff formed. It gives each intent in force its place in the priority order (`served`, by the rank read up the tree, as the doctrine serves it) and what displaces it today (`displaced_by`), and the side's `holds`: each with its target, the day, whether a `hold` command or an order made it, and that order's number. It gives too the side's `interrupts`: each interrupt set with its base, its number of days, the day it was set, whether it is armed, the day it last fired, whether that is acknowledged and whether it needs acknowledging; each standing record that has fired, by its kind and its plan or ship; and the kinds switched off. Those fired and not acknowledged come first.
- `set_intent` (the side's next free number when none is given; `intent_kind` is `keep_supplied`, the default, `hold`, `cover`, `neutralise`, `raid`, `deny`, `strike_ships`, `search_waters`, `stand_at` or `operate_carriers`; `floor_days` and `interval_days` belong to `keep_supplied` alone; `neutralise` takes a `target`, the number of an enemy base on the side's map, and no `base`; `raid` takes a `base` of the side's and a `target`; no other kind takes a `target`; `deny` takes `hex` as `[q, r]` and `distance` in whole hexes and no `base`; `strike_ships` takes a `base` of the side's; `search_waters` is refused as "Not a root" and is the staff's alone; `stand_at` and `operate_carriers` take a `task_force`, one of the side's own forces in its numbers (an enemy force and one that does not exist are refused alike), and a `hex` as `[q, r]` and no `base`; no other kind takes a `task_force`, and no other kind but these and `deny` takes a `hex`; only `deny` takes a `distance`), `withdraw_intent`, `set_limit`, `set_leave`, `hold`, `release`, `approve`, `cancel_plan`, `set_interrupt` and `acknowledge`: each queues one command for the coming day, taken in at its first step. It is checked at once, by taking it in on a copy of the world after the commands already queued, and answered queued or refused with the engine's reason. `set_limit` forces takes `ships`, `task_forces`, `air_groups` and `source`, each optional, and `set_limit` works on any intent. `hold` and `release` name exactly one of a ship, a task force, or an air group, which is held as a whole with no setting and by its search arc alone with the setting `search_arc`, in the side's own numbers, and a number that is not the side's is refused as the engine refuses it. `set_interrupt` takes `interrupt` (the side's next free number, one past the highest ever set or queued, when none is given), `base` and `below_days`; or `standing` (`cannot_meet`, `waits_for_leave` or `ship_lost`) and `on`; a table that mixes the two is refused. `acknowledge` takes `interrupt`, or `standing` with one of `plan` and `ship`, in the side's own numbers.
- `preview`: what a changed intent or limit would do, for `set_intent` and `set_limit` only, worked on two copies of the world as the coming day will find it (the side's queued commands taken in, and the same with the changed command last). It stores nothing: no state, no event, no hash, nothing queued. It sets what the staff would do on the coming day for the intent as things stand beside what it would do with the change (its orders, commands and decisions to give no order, the next run and the runs over 30 days, the first day below the floor and what binds), and says which ships are held and so not free, what each run still in hand would do (a run that has sailed goes on to the base it was sent to and lands there; a run still loading is sent to the new base; a change that is not of the base leaves every run as ordered), what is lost that was made ready (nothing: stock aboard a ship stays aboard and is carried by its next run, and stock landed stays where it lies), and what other plans give up. For a deny or a neutralise whose plan waits for a leave, the decisions to give no order carry one more, the proposal's: what the staff would do were the plan released, a carrier force and its station among it (decision 0202, 24). A command the tool would refuse is answered as the refusal and is not numbered. [0107, rulings 25, 27 and 28; 0202]
- `approve` with a `preview` number in place of a plan queues exactly the command that preview showed, answered as any queued command is. A preview is **void** once the side has queued an order or a command, or emptied its queue, or a day has been resolved, since it was made; approving a void preview is refused and says why (what was queued after it, or that a day was resolved). It is the session's, not the world's. [0107, ruling 26]
- `propose`: the staff's proposal for an intent, with what it is displaced by and what it displaces (`displaced_by`, `displaced_intents`, and in words `displaces`). For a hold, a cover, a neutralise, a raid or a deny intent it also gives the `tree` the staff would form (Holding a place, A proposal): the sub-intents, each with its number as the side's next free numbers give it, parent, maker and reason; the air groups each intent would order with the order and its working; the search arcs with their threats; and the decisions to give no order. It is null for a supply intent.
- `staff_orders`: what the staff would hand in at the start of the coming day, each with its reason, asked on a copy of the world with the day's first three steps taken (`World::at_staff_step`, which takes the override too). Only the session holds that copy; the staff and the tools are given its side's view.
- `run`: resolves up to `days` days (a whole number, at least 1), each exactly as `end_day` does, with the orders and commands queued going in on the first day. It stops after a day on which any interrupt of the side fired, or at the end of the game, or at its number of days, whichever comes first. The clock stops at the end of the day of a firing, not before it: nothing is ordered inside a day and the commander acts the next morning. The one exception is a force's asking leave to pursue (Interrupts, above): `run` stops in the evening before the day the staff would ask in, with `stopped` of `asks_leave` and the asking in its answer (chapter 1, The stop in the evening), and does not stop after that day for the same firing. It gives the staff's orders for the first day, worked before that day is resolved as `staff_orders` works them; the days resolved; why it stopped (`interrupt`, `game_over` or `days`); and everything that fired on the last day, each with its day, its event number, whether it is acknowledged and the `why` numbers of the reasons its notices rest on, with the result when the game is over. It takes an optional `reason`, which must be text and changes nothing. It is offered only where exactly one seat of the game is held by a player; with none or more it is not among the tools, and a call is refused, saying to use `end_day`. [0107 item 29, ruling 36]
- `report`: the command report, with no argument. It is worked from the side's own events and stored nowhere, so it is the same after a save and a resume. Its matters: a **matter** is a plan, a run where there is one and a kind of notice (the two leaves are two kinds), as the staff handed it in. It is open on the day last resolved if its notice was handed in that day, and closed otherwise, from the day after its last notice; handed in again later it is the same matter, with a gap in its history. Each matter gives its plan and intent, run, kind, whether it is open, the day it was first handed in and the day it closed, and its history: one entry for each day a notice of it was handed in, with the `why` number of the reason that day's notice rests on, so that `why` reads each day's reason. Open matters come first, then closed ones, each by the day first handed in, then plan, run and kind. The report also gives the interrupts as `command_state` does, the firings of the day last resolved, `holds` (the measure of each hold intent in force, with its intent, its base and its words), `neutralisations` (the measure of each neutralise intent in force, with its intent, its target and its words), `denials` (the measure of each deny intent in force, with its intent and its words) and a summary in words, which ends with a sentence for each hold intent and for each neutralise intent. [0107 item 30, ruling 37; 0117 item 14]
- `order`: the plan order of one intent of the commander's, or of a "take a place" the staff formed, by its number (The plan order, above), worked from the view the coming day finds (the queued commands taken in) and stored nowhere. Any other intent of the staff's, or one the side does not have, is refused as `propose` refuses an intent not in force. Since decision 0159, rule E (agent interface version 26; the answer kind `plan_order`); a take the staff formed since decision 0164, with the answer's shape and the interface version unchanged.
- `why`: one `StaffReason` by its event number, whole, with the order or command it is for, or as a decision to give no order; its `serves` is the statement in words, then "decided at the level of" the area or the base (its `level`, null for a reason of a game saved before it was handed in), and its `chain` lists the intents above the one served, nearest first, each as "under intent N (the commander's)" with its statement, the last the commander's own. `staff_orders` gives the same chain in each reason's `serves`.
- `queued` and `take_back` (decision 0221, rules 15 to 18; agent interface version 46) are tools of every seat, with or without a staff, and are the session's. `queued` lists what the seat has queued, the orders for the next pulse, the orders for dusk and the commands, each with its place, its unit and order or its tool and command in the side's numbers, when it acts, what is taken back with it and what a hold rests on, and an `as_of`. `take_back` withdraws the entry at a place, given the latest `as_of`, with what goes with it: the holds queued on a unit when the last queued order to it goes, and the commands queued for a new intent when the `set_intent` that makes it goes. It is refused, and nothing changes, where another queued entry would fail without it, naming that entry and its place. A preview made before a take back is void after it. Neither is in the state, the hash or a saved game.

A tool is not a rule: what it shows is worked from the side's view, and the agent's schemas say each answer's shape (`crates/sim-agent/schemas/`).

## What is saved

A game is saved as it has always been, as what was handed in, and from version 3 of the saved game it holds what step 1 of stage 1 adds (chapter 1, What a saved game holds); version 4, since step 2 of stage 2, adds the intent tree to the commands and the hand-ins (below): the commander's commands by day, and each side's staff hand-in by day, whole. A hand-in is the orders with their intent and reason, the staff's commands and the decisions to give no order with their reasons, and, since step 4, the notices (each its kind's name, its plan, its run or none, and the reason it rests on, as the number of a command or of a decision to give no order of the same hand-in). A hand-in with no notice is written with no `notices` key, as it always was, and a hand-in saved without the key reads as having none; the saved game is still version 3. Only the days and sides that handed in something are kept. The saved game also records the doctrine version beside the rules label. Version 4 writes in a command `set_intent` with `intent_kind` (`cover` or `hold`; left out for keeping a base supplied), the staff's `form_intent` (`parent`, `intent_kind`, `base`), a forces limit with `air_groups` and a `source` that may be left out, and a hold or release of a whole air group (an `air_group` and no `setting`), and in a reason's `serves` the `chain`. A saved game of version 3 or before is refused by the reader, not converted. It holds no command state: the state is rebuilt by taking the commands in again, the commander's `hold` and `release` among them, and the staff's `end_run` among its hand-in, and a commander's `set_interrupt` and `acknowledge` among the commands. It holds no interrupt's status: it is rebuilt by taking the commands in and testing each morning again, and a replay hands the saved notices in as the staff gave them. It holds no override either: the commander's orders are saved as handed in, and the override is taken again when a replay takes them in at step 2. [0107 items 9, 11 and 12]

Three versions name what a saved game was played under. The state's layout was `flagplot-state-v26` from step 4, `flagplot-state-v27` from decision 0114 (an air group's two strike settings, chapter 1, The state hash and saved games) and was `flagplot-state-v28` from decision 0117 (an intent's parent, maker and addressee after its kind, the kinds cover and hold, a forces limit's air groups and its source as optional, a risk limit that may be unset, and the hold of a whole air group; `flagplot-state-v29` from decision 0120, which adds the kinds neutralise and raid, `flagplot-state-v30` from decisions 0118 and 0123, which add an air group's strike sizing and the kinds deny, strike ships and search waters, `flagplot-state-v31` from decisions 0124 and 0125, which add a route's marks to stand at after its marks to act at, a halt's work `night`, the addressee task force (code 4) and the kinds stand at a hex (code 9) and operate a carrier force (code 10), each with its force and its hex, and `flagplot-state-v32` from decision 0121, which adds a task force's charge, the force it keeps station on and the order that gave it, and `flagplot-state-v33` from decision 0122, which adds a ship's tubes fired and a base's ammunition, and is `flagplot-state-v34` since decision 0131, which adds an air group's search once a day) (a side's interrupts: those set, the standing records that have fired and the kinds switched off, written after its holds; `flagplot-state-v25` before it, since step 3, wrote a side's holds after its commitments and a run's day of ending after its free day). The rules label was 10 from step 3 and stayed 10 in step 4 (it was 12 from decision 0114, 13 from decision 0118, a strike sized to its target, was 14 from decision 0124, the night stand: a route's marks to stand at, which a game saved before could not hold; decision 0125 left it at 14, and is 15 since decision 0121, the escort: a task force's charge and the escort order that gives it, which a game saved before could not hold; decision 0126 left it at 15; it is 16 since decision 0122, rearming in port, which a game saved before could not hold; decision 0127 left it at 16; neither the intent tree nor decision 0120 raised it: no game without a hold, cover, neutralise or raid intent plays differently, and the level of a reason is text): a commander's order to a unit inside a forces limit holds it and the staff's saved orders to it are refused (step 3), so a game saved under the old rules in which the commander ordered such a unit could play differently, and is refused by `replay` before it runs. An interrupt and a notice change nothing a unit does, no order, stock or chance, and so no game saved under label 10 plays out differently for them: a replay hands in the saved orders, commands and hand-in, and the interrupts are tested on what that game has. What they add to a game's log is events (a notice, a firing), in a game that hands in a notice or sets an interrupt. A game saved under layout v25 is refused by its layout version. The doctrine version was `supply-4` then, and is `supply-13` since decision 0127 (ammunition as policy: a force short of ammunition does not sortie and is not a free escort; a side with no force short of ammunition gets the orders `supply-12` gave), `supply-12` from decision 0126 (escorted and covered runs; a side whose forces limits name no task force gets the orders `supply-11` gave), `supply-11` from decisions 0124 and 0125 (`supply-10` from decision 0123; `supply-9` from decision 0120; `supply-8` from the air doctrine; `supply-7` from the soak ashore, decision 0093, item 10; `supply-6` from decision 0112) (The doctrine version, below). The saved game is version 5 since decision 0120, which adds in a command `set_intent` the kinds `neutralise` and `raid` (with their `target`, and no `base` for a neutralise), the staff's `form_intent` of a raid (`base` and `target`), and in a reason's `serves` the `level`; a saved game of version 4 or before is refused, not converted. It is version 6 since decisions 0118 and 0123, which add the kinds `deny` (`hex`, `distance`), `strike_ships` and `search_waters` (`base`), the order `set_strike_sizing` and a reason's `level` of an air group; a saved game of version 5 or before is refused. It is version 7 since decisions 0124 and 0125, which add the kinds `stand_at` and `operate_carriers` (each its `task_force` and `hex`), a route order's `stand_at` and a reason's `level` of a task force; a saved game of version 6 or before is refused. It is version 8 since decision 0121, which adds the escort order, `Escort`, with its charge or none, to a hand-in's orders; a saved game of version 7 or before is refused. Decisions 0122 and 0127 leave it at 8: they add no order and no command, and the games they cannot hold are refused by the state layout and the rules label. It is version 10 since decision 0134, which adds no order and no command: its state layout is `flagplot-state-v35` (the commands and their pictures, chapter 1) and the keys of the file are unchanged; a saved game of version 9 or before is refused. It is version 11 since decision 0135, which adds no order and no command either: its state layout is `flagplot-state-v36` (the reports each command has heard and the reports in flight, chapter 1) and the keys are unchanged; a saved game of version 10 or before is refused. Decision 0136 makes the state's layout `flagplot-state-v37` (what the area command holds, the state reports in flight and radio silence, chapter 1) and the saved game's version 12, which adds the order `set_radio_silence` and keeps the file's keys; a saved game of version 11 or before is refused. The doctrine version is `supply-18` since it: the area's doctrines read the side's units as last reported, and at preserve the force a carrier force and a surface force that stands by night are set to radio silence, where report figures are loaded; a side with no report figures gets the orders the version before gave. The rules label stays 18: no game without a commander plays differently. The rules label stays 18: no game without a day plays differently. The agent interface is version 24. The rules label stays 19: no game without the kind plays differently. An island is map data, not state, and is not in the hash. The doctrine version is `supply-40`; a side with no supply run by night gets the orders and reasons `supply-39` gave. A game saved under an earlier layout or version is refused, not converted. The rules label was 24 (a game with men ashore short of the men wanted and nothing more coming plays differently) and the doctrine version `"supply-46"`. Decision 0194 adds no state, no order and no field a seat reads: the game's days are configuration, not hashed (chapter 1, What a side may know), and the state layout, the saved game's version and the agent interface's version stay. The rules label is 25 (a game with men ashore in its last days plays differently) and the doctrine version `"supply-47"`.
