Decision 0214 makes the state's layout `flagplot-state-v55` (a side's command state gains, after everything else, the leave to pursue of each order with its code, each force's pursuit with its way and, for an answer, the answer's event, its contact and its day, and the commander's refusals by force and contact; the standing kind `asks_leave_to_pursue`, code 6, the standing subject task force, code 3, the notices' codes 10 to 12 and the two commands are added at the end), the saved game's version 24 (it writes the commands `set_pursuit`, with `intent` and an optional `leave`, and `answer_pursuit`, with `task_force`, `contact` and `allow`; in a hand-in's notice an optional `pursuit`; and in a hand-in's reason an optional `answer_of`, a force number, for the staff's reason that rests on the commander's answer; an older saved game is refused, not converted), the agent interface version 43, the rules label 27 (a game with a carrier force and a fresh contact beyond a night and within `pursuit_nights` nights plays differently) and the doctrine version `"supply-53"`; a side with no such find, or with the leave at stay, gets the orders and reasons `"supply-52"` gave. A replay takes the saved answer in as a saved command and hands in the notices the staff gave, which begin and end the pursuits as they did. A refusal of a contact is kept until a yes lifts it. [0214, rules 15 and 18]

Decision 0218, stage 3, makes the rules label 30 (a patrol intent, a hold on a boat and a forces limit that names boats, which a game saved under the old rules could not hold), the agent interface version 48 and the doctrine version `"supply-55"`. The state's layout stays `flagplot-state-v56`: a patrol intent writes its kind's code 16, its hex and its distance only where there is one; a hold on a boat writes the target code 6 and the boat's number only where there is one; and a forces limit writes its boats (a tag byte 6, the count, each boat) only where it names one, so a game with none hashes as it did. The saved game's version is as it was: a patrol is saved as a `set_intent` with `intent_kind` `patrol`, a hold as a `hold` with `submarine`, a forces limit with `submarines`, and a staff's station order as any order is. [0218, rules 12, 34 and 54]

**Replay.** A replay hands the saved orders, the saved commands and, before each day, the saved hand-in of each side into the engine (`World::submit_staff`), and asks no staff. So a replay of a staffed game works when the doctrine has since changed, and gives every daily hash that was saved. [0107 item 9]

**`replay --check-staff`.** It asks the doctrine's staff as well. On each saved day, each side that the doctrine gives a staff is asked on the view the engine then holds, and what it gives is compared part by part, orders, then commands, then decisions to give no order, then notices, with what was saved. The saved hand-in is what is handed in, whatever the staff gave, so the replay is the saved game and its hashes are still compared. The run stops at the first day and side where they differ and says what differs. A saved hand-in for a side the doctrine gives no staff is a difference too. A difference in the staff is reported before a hash that differs on the same day. If there is none, it says so, naming the doctrine now and the one the game was saved under. It is the test that a staff is a function of the view. [0107 item 10]

**The doctrine version.** The doctrine has a version of its own, `DOCTRINE_VERSION` in `sim-staff` (`"supply-56"` from decision 0222, rule 1: the reason of a hold with no source names the bases of the side that could be one, or says that none could and the place lives on what it has (Holding a place, The supply that follows); the orders are those `"supply-55"` gave; `"supply-55"` from decision 0218, stage 3: the patrol order gives each boat it may use the station of her place and orders her there, and the staff passes over a station inside submarine waters the side holds where another serves (Patrolling waters with submarines); a side with no patrol intent and no suspected waters or report of a submarine held gets the orders and reasons `"supply-54"` gave; `"supply-54"` from decision 0217: a deny no base serves but a carrier task under it does hands in no notice that it cannot be met, and a carrier station's reason carries its cautions; the orders are those `"supply-53"` gave; `"supply-53"` from decision 0214 (A find, and going after it, above); `"supply-52"` from decision 0203: a carrier force at a station the staff chose is given the way of least danger by day that arrives in time and is in fuel (The way a force takes, above), a way that bends given once and kept; a side with no carrier task formed under a deny or a neutralise, or one whose force's shortest way is in fuel and enters no hex inside a circle of danger by day, gets the orders and reasons `"supply-51"` gave; `"supply-51"` from decision 0201 (fuelling at sea, and the warning of a force with no home port with a ship below a quarter) and `"supply-50"` from decision 0202 (a carrier force's station chosen by the risk, the circles of danger and the fuel); `"supply-47"` from decision 0194: no attack ashore begun in the game's last days, and nothing coming after the game's end (Taking a place, Ashore, items 7 and 11); a side with no take whose men are ashore in the game's last days, and none that waits on a unit or a lift due after the game's end, gets the orders and reasons `"supply-46"` gave; `"supply-46"` from decision 0193: men ashore with no more coming (Taking a place, Ashore, items 7 to 10); a side with no take whose men ashore are short with nothing more coming gets the orders and reasons `"supply-45"` gave; `"supply-45"` from decision 0190: a targets limit gives the bombers of a strike-ships task or a carrier task their target list, and, with `waters_first`, their waters to strike first, adds those waters to a carrier force's search arc and has it close on a contact in them (Striking ships from a base, What to strike first; Operating a carrier force); a side with no targets limit gets the orders and reasons `supply-44` gave; `"supply-44"` from decision 0192: a stand's force with no home port stands from where it is, and one with a home port stopped at sea goes home (Standing at a hex by night, A force with no home port); a side with no such stand gets the orders and reasons `supply-43` gave; `"supply-43"` from decision 0191: the first lift of a released take turned back, kept back and held for enemy carriers near its way (Taking a place, A lift turned back); `"supply-42"` from decision 0169: at balanced a lift of a take with men aboard, a later lift as it stands or a second lift, short of the men wanted with too few in reach, goes only where its men can be fed, and where they cannot the take does not lift and hands in `cannot_bring_enough` once (Taking a place, Not to starve); a side whose takes hold no estimate, or whose lifts are not short, gets the orders and reasons `supply-41` gave; `"supply-41"` from decision 0163: under "control an island" the source of supply is the base with the most supply above its reserve, named with no ship lying there, and a lift that has landed goes on to the source of the hold of the place where its ships have the fuel (Controlling an island, The source of supply; Taking a place, Forces with men aboard, item 5); a side with no island and no hold whose source differs from where its lifts came from gets the orders and reasons `supply-40` gave; `"supply-40"` from decision 0162: a supply run by night names in its load order the level, the least of its ships' limits, and is cut to the sum of what its ships may take; its speed is read on the load as spread (A run by night, items 2 and 3); a side with no supply run by night gets the orders and reasons `supply-39` gave; `"supply-39"` from decision 0159, rule D, item 14, as the architect ruled it: a take whose plan is preparing, whose need to know is unmet and none of whose named groups reaches the place, moves a carrier force its limit names, with a named scout aboard, to the covering hex to look (Operating a carrier force, items 25 and 26); a side with no such take gets the orders and reasons `supply-38` gave; `"supply-38"` from decision 0159, rule D, items 12 and 13: a "knowledge" limit read up a take's tree, and not the risk, says how much must be known before its landing goes, and where nothing can look a recent count holds the launch and a count does not; a side with no such limit gets the orders and reasons `supply-37` gave; `"supply-37"` from decision 0159, rule C: a take whose landing is ready is not released, and leave to launch is not asked, while a limit not before, wait for or after read up its tree is unmet, and a limit that can never be met as it stands is said once with `cannot_meet`; a side with no such limit gets the orders `supply-36` gave; `"supply-36"` from decision 0159, rule B: a take whose first lift would sail short of the men wanted, at preserve the force or balanced, waits, a draft staying a draft, for the first day in reach that brings them, and at balanced, where none does, hands in `cannot_bring_enough` once and forms nothing; a side with no take short of men gets the orders `supply-35` gave; `"supply-35"` from decision 0159, rule A: a hold of a base that is not the side's, where the side has a base of its own in that hex and men ashore there, forms "keep supplied" for the side's own base under the hold as it does for a base that is the side's, and its reason says through which base the men are fed; a side with no such hold gets the orders `supply-34` gave; `"supply-34"` from decision 0152, item 5b: on a morning a take higher in priority that sends a later lift as it stands is given it before a take lower in priority chooses its first lift, and a first lift with no ship afloat is lost and so no longer on its way; a side with at most one take in force, and none whose first lift has lost every ship, gets the orders `supply-33` gave; `"supply-33"` from decision 0151, item 8c: under an island a take takes no force on the bombardment mission, and each such force stopped at a base of the side is given a bombard task under the island's first neutralising of an enemy base at a named place; a side with no island, or none with a force on the bombardment mission, gets the orders `supply-32` gave; `"supply-32"` from decision 0152: a force with men aboard is a lift as it stands, taken by "take a place" as its first lift before any base and sent as a later lift once the first is in, sent by "hold a place" where no take can use it, and ready as it stands wherever it lies; the source under an island's holds is kept and each hold's forces limit set each morning to a pool that only grows; a side with no force with men aboard and no island gets the orders `supply-31` gave; `"supply-31"` from decision 0151: the island doctrine, a plan of its own for a take and a supply intent under an island, the staff's withdrawing of a neutralising that has done its work, the forces limits read under an island and the carrier tasks under its denial; a side with no "control an island" gets the orders and reasons `supply-30` gave; the versions from `supply-20` to `supply-30` are in What is saved, above; `"supply-19"` from decision 0137: a carrier force's cautious or bold commander weighs a contact of his own a level more careful or bolder than the task's risk in the closing rule, and says so; a side with no such commander gets the orders and reasons `supply-18` gave; `"supply-18"` from decision 0136: the area's doctrines read the side's units as last reported, and a carrier force and a surface force that stands by night are set to radio silence at preserve the force where report figures are loaded; `"supply-17"` from the amendment of decision 0131's rule 6: a carrier force's searching group searches once a day at preserve the force alone and in both daylight pulses at balanced and at press; the cover and the search of waters are as `supply-16` had them, and a side with no carrier task at balanced gets the orders `supply-16` gave; `"supply-16"` from decision 0131: a search is flown once a day at preserve the force and at balanced, a bomber group takes a turn at the search on a carrier force and at a base, and a carrier force out of fuel is given no route; every staffed game in which a group searches gives other orders; `"supply-15"` from decision 0130: a carrier force is routed at cruise or at full speed as the night allows, closes where the first fresh contact it wants within a night and its bombers' reach is beyond that reach, stays while it is in reach and goes back to its hex when there is none, goes home for fuel at cruise, and a ship the engine's measure has run dry short of a passage is short of the fuel for it in the carrier and stand doctrines; a side with no carrier task, and no stand task whose ship runs dry short of a sortie, gets the orders `supply-14` gave; `"supply-14"` from the architect's ruling of Nov 16, 2026, on decisions 0124 and 0125: no stand task and no carrier task is formed for a force that already has one of that kind in force, whoever made it, and a side with no such task stated beside an end that names the force gets the orders `supply-13` gave; `"supply-13"` from decision 0127: ammunition read with fuel in the stand doctrine and in the choice of a free escort, a force short of ammunition at its risk neither sorties nor escorts, and a side whose forces are none short of ammunition gets the orders `supply-12` gave; `"supply-12"` from decision 0126: the escort and the covering force of a run under keep supplied, the run held at preserve the force on a threat with no escort free, and the air doctrine taking a keep-supplied intent whose forces limit names task forces; a side whose keep-supplied intents name no task force gets the orders `supply-11` gave; `"supply-11"` from decisions 0124 and 0125: a task force as an addressee, the forming of stand and carrier tasks in the deny and hold doctrines, the stand doctrine and the carrier doctrine, and the task force as a level in a reason; a side whose intents name no task force gets the orders `supply-10` gave; `"supply-10"` from decision 0123: the deny doctrine, the strike doctrine and the search doctrine, the cover's arc over the waters to deny, a raid's leaving out of a bomber a strike-ships task of higher rank may use, and the air group as a level in the reason of a sizing order; `"supply-9"` from decision 0120: the neutralise doctrine and the raid doctrine, the cover's give of a raid's bomber, and the level in every reason; `"supply-8"` from step 2 of stage 2, decisions 0115 and 0117: the air doctrine after the supply doctrine, which serves keep-supplied intents alone, for hold and cover intents, and a side with none gets what `supply-7` gave; `"supply-7"` from the soak ashore, decision 0093 item 10: the ships of an ended plan are freed first in the morning, before any intent is served; `"supply-6"` from decision 0112: a ship is free on the morning after the day it has finished fuelling at the source, by the hours of the fuelling (Its days), and its fuel is read as it stood at the end of the day before; `"supply-5"`: each reason is handed in with its intent's statement in words; `"supply-4"` from step 4 of stage 1: the notices, cannot meet, waits for a leave and a run ended, and the reason a day gives that begins "No new run:"; `"supply-3"` from step 3 of stage 1: holds, runs set aside, the supply aboard a free ship, the fallback of a cancelled plan and where a run lands in the projection; `"supply-2"` from step 2, priority between intents and displaced demand; `"supply-1"` in step 1; `"none"` before a doctrine existed). A change to what a staff decides from a view changes it. A saved game records it. It is checked when a game kept by `play --game` is resumed for more play, which is refused under another doctrine, naming both the version the game was begun under and the one the program has, and not when a game is replayed: a replay is what was saved. A `play --game` file begun before the key existed is read as `"none"`. [0107 item 11]

Where the staff comes from. Every loop that resolves days gets its staffs from the one place, `sim_staff::staffs()`, and passes them in: `run`, `batch`, `export`, `hash`, `events`, `time`, `replay --check-staff` and a game held open for play. `sim-agent`'s session is handed them by its caller. [0107 item 7; docs/engineering/layout.md]

A side sees: none of this. A saved game belongs to the umpire (chapter 1, What a saved game holds).

## A scenario's commands

A scenario may state a side's commands by day, as it states orders, so that a test can begin with an intent in force. A `[[command]]` table has `day`, `side`, `kind` and the keys of its kind, and no other key. Only the commander's kinds may be written; the staff's are the staff's to give, `form_intent` among them: a scenario states a commander's end, as a root, and the staff forms what serves it. A scenario with no `[[command]]` plays as it did. [0107 item 16]

| `kind` | Keys |
| --- | --- |
| `set_intent` | `intent`, `base`, and `intent_kind`: `"keep_supplied"` (the default where the key is absent, so a scenario written before the kinds reads as it did), which also takes `floor_days` and `interval_days` (optional); `"cover"` (cover `base` from the air) or `"hold"` (hold `base`), which take neither; `"neutralise"` (neutralise the enemy base `target`), which takes `intent` and `target` and no `base`; `"raid"` (raid `target` from `base`), which takes both; `"deny"` (deny the waters within `distance` whole hexes of `hex`, given as `[q, r]`), which takes `intent`, `hex` and `distance` and no `base`; `"strike_ships"` (strike ships from `base`), which takes `base`; `"search_waters"` takes `base` too, and is read, but the engine refuses it as "Not a root" when the command is taken in; `"stand_at"` (stand the task force `task_force` at `hex`, given as `[q, r]`) or `"operate_carriers"` (operate the carrier force `task_force` from `hex`), each of which takes `intent`, `task_force` and `hex` and no `base` (decisions 0124 and 0125); `"take_place"` (take the hex of land `hex`, given as `[q, r]`, with an optional `by_day`, a whole number from 1), which takes `intent`, `hex` and `by_day` and no `base` (decision 0142); `"control_island"` (control the island `island`, its number in the scenario's `[[island]]` tables, with an optional `by_day`), which takes `intent`, `island` and `by_day` and no `base`, `target`, `task_force` or `hex` (decision 0151); `"patrol"` (patrol the waters within `distance` whole hexes of `hex`, given as `[q, r]`, with submarines), which takes `intent`, `hex` and `distance` and no `base` (decision 0218) |
| `withdraw_intent` | `intent` |
| `set_limit` | `limit`, and the keys of that limit, below |
| `set_leave` | `leave` (`"prepare"` or `"launch"`) and `given` |
| `approve`, `cancel_plan` | `plan` |
| `hold`, `release` | Exactly one of `ship`, `task_force`, `air_group`, `land_unit` and `submarine`; with `air_group`, `setting` (`"search_arc"`) holds that setting alone and no `setting` holds the whole group; no other target takes a `setting` |
| `set_interrupt` | Either `interrupt`, `base` and `below_days` (a numbered interrupt set), or `standing` (`"cannot_meet"`, `"waits_for_leave"`, `"ship_lost"`, `"cannot_bring_enough"`, `"lift_turned_back"` or `"asks_leave_to_pursue"`) and `on` (`true` or `false`) |
| `acknowledge` | Either `interrupt`, or `standing` with exactly one of `plan`, `ship` and `task_force` (a standing interrupt for a task force, `asks_leave_to_pursue`) |
| `set_pursuit` | `intent` and `leave` (`"ask"`, `"go"` or `"stay"`; left out clears the setting so that it is read up the tree again) (decision 0214) |
| `answer_pursuit` | `task_force`, `contact` (the commander's number of the contact) and `allow` (a bool) (decision 0214) |

The limits: `"forces"` takes `intent` and, each optional (a list not given is empty), `ships`, `task_forces`, `air_groups`, `land_units` and `submarines`, and `source` (a supply intent's forces limit must name one, another kind's need not); `"area"` takes `intent` and `hexes`, as `[[q, r], ...]`; `"priority"` takes `intent` and `rank`; `"protected"` takes `intent` and `ships`; `"reserve"` takes `base`, `supply_t` and `fuel_t`; `"risk"` takes `intent` and `risk`, `"preserve"`, `"balanced"` or `"press"`; `"not_before"` takes `intent` and `from_day`; `"wait_for"` takes `intent` and one of `task_force` and `land_unit` (both is refused); `"after"` takes `intent` and `other_intent` (decision 0159, 9). `"targets"` takes `intent` and, each optional, `kinds` (a list of ship kinds by name, each once, most wanted first; left out clears the limit) and `waters_first` (`true` or `false`, `false` where left out; decision 0190). Each of the last three before it with its other key left out clears the limit. A quantity is a whole number or a decimal string, never a float.

The loader refuses, when the scenario is read: a day that is 0 or after the scenario's last day; a key the kind does not take, or that is no key of any command; a missing key; a TOML float; a kind that is the staff's (`end_run` among them); an unknown limit, leave, risk or setting (a `set_pursuit` leave that is none of ask, go and stay among them), a `set_pursuit` with no `intent`, an `answer_pursuit` with no `task_force`, `contact` or `allow`; a hold or release that names none or more than one target, or a `setting` with a ship, a task force, a land unit or a submarine; a `patrol` without `hex` or `distance`, or with a `base`; an `intent_kind` that is none of the ten, a `deny` without `hex` or `distance`, a `deny` with a `base`, a `stand_at` or `operate_carriers` without `task_force` or `hex` or with a `base`, a `distance` on a kind but deny, a `hex` on a kind but deny, stand_at and operate_carriers, a `task_force` on another kind, or a `cover` or `hold` with a key only a supply intent takes; a `set_interrupt` or `acknowledge` that mixes the keys of its two forms; a standing kind that is none of the six; a `set_interrupt` with `standing` and no `on`; an `acknowledge` with `standing` and not exactly one of `plan`, `ship` and `task_force`. Whether a base, ship, task force, intent, plan or interrupt exists, whether a number of days is 0 and whether an interrupt has fired are not the loader's to say: the engine checks it when the command is taken in, and refuses it as above. A scenario's commands are kept by day in the order written, kept when a seed is changed, and handed in before the day is resolved, as the commander's, so the engine takes them in at its first step. Two commands of one day for one side are taken in in the order written, and the Allied side's before the Japanese side's. [0107 item 16]

A scenario's `[[island]]` tables name the islands a `control_island` may name (Controlling an island, above): a `number`, a `name` and `places`, each `{ base = N }` or `{ hex = [q, r] }`. The loader refuses what Controlling an island says it refuses. Whether the island a `control_island` names exists is the engine's to say when the command is taken in, and it is refused as "No such island". [0151 item 1]

A scenario's `[[order]]` may give the issuer `staff` and an `intent`. Such an order is not checked against a forces limit or against the holds, and the override skips it (above, The forces limit on a staff's orders; Holds and the override). [0107, As built]

## The day's order

Chapter 1 owns the sequence. Where this chapter's rules run, in the numbering of decision 0107's table (which chapter 1 now numbers the same: the staff is asked at its step 4, and its commands, notices, interrupts and the orders intake are its step 5):

| Step | What happens | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | The commander's commands are taken in, `hold` and `release` among them | This chapter, Commands |
| 2 | The override: the commander's orders to units in an intent's forces limit (an air group an intent may use, and a boat a patrol may use or a limit names, among them) are taken in and hold them. In the code it runs after step 3 | This chapter, Holds and the override |
| 3 | Forces back from the yard are home. Ships in port are not refuelled here: they take fuel in the loading phase of each pulse, each base down to its reserve | Chapter 4, Repair; chapter 2, Refuelling in port |
| 4 | Each side's staff, if it has one, is asked on its view as it now stands. Then the days of supply of the base of each interrupt the commander set are read on that same view | This chapter, The staff; Interrupts |
| 5 | The staff's commands are taken in (the runs set aside first; the air doctrine's after the supply doctrine's, with `form_intent` among them), then its decisions to give no order are recorded, then its notices; then each side's interrupts are tested, on those readings and those notices; then all orders not already taken in, the commander's and the staff's | This chapter, The staff; Notices; Interrupts; chapter 1, Orders |
| 6 | The rest of the day, as it was | Chapters 2 to 7 |

**The staff's morning, in its order** (what each of these does, in the sections named; one pass over the side's view as the staff step leaves it, the sub-intents formed in the pass read by the doctrines after them in the same pass):

1. The supply doctrine, for the keep-supplied intents.
2. The air doctrine's leaves, for each hold or cover root and, since decision 0120, each neutralise or raid root, and, since decision 0123, each deny or strike-ships root, and, since decisions 0124 and 0125, each stand-at or operate-carriers root, and, since decision 0151, each control-an-island root. **The island doctrine runs right after these leaves and before every other air doctrine:** it forms the island's takes, holds, denial, carrier tasks and neutralisings (Controlling an island, above), so that what it forms is served by the doctrines after it the same morning. It gives no order. **The carrier pass** runs right after it (decision 0202): the free carrier forces handed out to the commander's denies and neutralises, with their tasks formed, kept or withdrawn (Carriers serving an order for an end, below).
3. The hold doctrine: the stand and carrier tasks of the forces named in its forces limit, then cover under each held base (A task force as an addressee, above).
4. The neutralise doctrine: raid tasks under each neutralise intent (Neutralising a base, below). It forms sub-intents and gives no order.
5. The deny doctrine: strike-ships and search-waters tasks under each deny intent, then the stand and carrier tasks of the forces named in its forces limit (Denying an area and A task force as an addressee, above). It forms sub-intents and gives no order.
6. The cover doctrine, over its groups, leaving a raid's or a strike-ships task's bomber out; it reads which fighters it keeps and at what share, and takes the waters of a released deny intent its searchers reach as threats beside its own (Covering a base from the air, A raid's bomber; Searching waters, above).
7. The raid doctrine, over its groups, which reads what the cover wants of the fighters it has, and leaves out a bomber a strike-ships task of higher rank may use (Raiding a base, below).
8. The strike doctrine, over its groups (Striking ships from a base, above).
9. The search doctrine, over its groups (Searching waters, above).
10. The stand doctrine, over the stand tasks: each force's orders and a stand's reasons (Standing at a hex by night, above).
11. The carrier doctrine, over the carrier tasks: each force's orders and its groups' (Operating a carrier force, above), with the raid of a carrier task under a neutralise (Carriers serving an order for an end, below), except those standing under a "take a place" or under the denial of an island, which step 14 serves.
12. "Take a place" forms its landing, the tasks that ready units are served, and "take a place" launches it (Readying a landing, and building up a base; Taking a place, above).
13. The bombard doctrine: bombard tasks formed under each released neutralise and take-a-place end, then each bombard task's orders (Bombarding a base, above).
14. The carrier tasks under "take a place": formed under each end whose plan is released, or, to look, under each end whose plan is preparing (Operating a carrier force, item 25), then each served by the carrier doctrine with the strike on the place, or, on the first morning the end is met, sent home (Operating a carrier force, Under take a place, above). The carrier tasks under the denial of an island are served here too, with the takes' carrier tasks, after the landing's morning, and are never sent home (Operating a carrier force, Under "control an island"). [0148; 0151]
15. The patrol doctrine, last: for each patrol intent in force whose plan is released, in priority order, the stations of the boats it may use and their orders (Patrolling waters with submarines, above). It forms nothing, reads no other doctrine's work and is read by none, so no other doctrine's hand-in changes for a game with no patrol. A boat held by the commander that morning is passed over, since the hold was made at step 2. [0218, rule 54]

Orders are handed in in that order and are taken in after the commander's, as above. [0107, The order of a day; rulings 23, 30 and 31; 0120; 0123; 0124; 0125; 0218]

## Not built

- **A hold of the commander on a base,** and holds on a setting other than a search arc: they wait for a stage whose doctrine acts on a base or changes a setting. (A hold intent is an intent, and is built.) [0107, rulings 17 and 30]
- **Interrupts of other kinds, and the withdrawal of a numbered one.** The one set kind is a base's days of supply below a number. A numbered interrupt cannot be withdrawn, only set again with another base and number. [0107, ruling 33]
- **A notice on every day the staff weighs no new run.** A cannot-meet notice is handed in only when the staff weighs a new run for the plan: none is handed in for an intent with no forces limit, a base that is not the side's, a pool with no ship afloat or one whose every ship afloat is protected for another intent, a plan that has ended, or a day a run of the plan is still loading or waits to sail. Those days give a reason, or a plan marked failed, and no notice. A preview carries no notice. A run-ended notice is handed in only for a run set aside for a hold: a run of a cancelled plan, which the staff reads as finished, has none. [0107, ruling 35]
- **A second source base** for an intent. [0107 item 19]
- **The recall of a run, an intent for fuel and a phase of a plan**: stage 2. Escort of a run is built (Escorting and covering a run); how many escorts a run is given, an escort sized to the threat, timing a run to the dark and air cover of a run are not. [0107; 0126, What it leaves out]
- **Bombers and fighters sent as escort on purpose** (the strike intent), and a hex of sea as the subject of an intent other than a station. The cover doctrine orders air groups at a base and no ship; the ships that stand at a base's hex or in the waters to deny are the stand task's (Standing at a hex by night, below). A raid is built (Raiding a base), and so are the strike of ships that come within reach and the size of a strike decided at the group's level (decision 0123). Escort as a task of its own is not. [0115, What it leaves for later steps; 0120; 0124]
- **Part of a search group flown,** and searching where nothing is known but something is feared (the area limit's to say). The arc points at what is known. [0115]
- **Sending a share of a search group,** weighed and left out: a group that searches on the days its fatigue allows was enough for stage 2's proof. A neutralise intent whose target is no longer an enemy base on the side's map gives a reason and forms nothing, and the staff does not withdraw it. [0120]
- **A land formation as the addressee of an intent,** a commander stating a sub-intent under a parent he names, a commander taking a staff-made intent over, the staff withdrawing a sub-intent because what it needs has gone (the cannot-meet notice serves), a ranking of siblings stated and tested beyond the one kind of sub-intent built, and friction between the levels (a delay in passing an order down, a subordinate's narrower view). [0116 item 19; 0117, What it leaves out]
- **Where a carrier force should be, and the enemy's expected places.** The staff keeps a carrier force where it was put and stands a surface force at the centre of the waters or at the base's hex; it does not choose a station from the enemy's reach, close the range on a contact the bombers cannot reach, fuel at sea or keep scarce ships out of the enemy's air reach, which wait for a belief about that reach. A surface force is not kept clear of the enemy's air by day: it steams for home at the morning and is at sea for as long as the passage takes. A damaged ship is not sent to a base that can patch it or to the yard by the staff. A station more than a night's steaming at full speed from the force's home port is not met: the staff hands in the notice that it cannot, and moving a force's home forward is the commander's. [0124, What it leaves out; 0125, What it leaves out]
- **A commander's temper anywhere but in closing.** A rating moves nothing but the closing of a carrier force on a contact of its own: not whether its aircraft strike, not an area it keeps to, not the speed, fuel, search or break-off. Changing a commander in the game, relief and refusal are not built. [0137, Why only this; 0132, What it leaves out]
- **Forces brought together for an operation:** units and ships moved to one base, forces joined, an operation that sails from two bases, a source chosen for its supply, airfield or port beyond the check that the place can be held, and forces kept back for an operation not yet stated. The staff assembles what already lies together (Taking a place, With no forces named). [0149, What it leaves for later]
- **What controlling an island leaves for later.** An island with a place inland, which waits on the march from a beach (stage 4); the order the places are taken in, chosen by the staff (they are taken in the island's order, each as its forces come free); garrisons sized and moved between places, and defences built under a hold; leaving formed by the staff when a place cannot be held (decision 0150, moved to after the proof); and where a carrier force stands under an island, chosen against the enemy and not only by the bombers' reach of the first place, and carriers of one island answering for another. [0151, What it leaves for later]
- **What the leave to pursue leaves for later.** It is of carrier forces alone, and not of one covering a landing or serving an island. It does not ask about a contact of a kind the order's risk does not close on: a commander who wants every kind closed on says press. A commander's rating does not move ask, go or stay: a man who goes without leave is the question of refusal that decision 0119 left to the designer. The staff does not weigh one order against another, form an order of its own or change or cancel one of the commander's. It does not ask the staff again inside a day (decision 0155, item 4, stands). An unanswered asking never lapses into a yes. It does not mend the closing rule's not reading the area limit (`docs/rules/differences.md`). [0214, What it does not do]
- **A carrier force weighing its rounds.** A carrier force goes back for its fuel and weighs nothing of ammunition, under an island as anywhere; only the stand doctrine, the bombard doctrine and the choice of a free escort read rounds (Ammunition as policy). [0151 item 8a; 0127]

## Not in this chapter

- How a command's refusal and an order's refusal differ for a player: the agent's tools, not rules.
- How orders are numbered, sorted and refused, and who can issue them: chapter 1, Orders.
- Refuelling, rearming, loading to a weight, reserves and forming, joining and standing down forces: chapter 2.
- Belief, contacts and claims, which a staff reads through the view: chapter 3.
- The men's supply, landings and the land units a base feeds: chapter 7. Aircraft upkeep, repair and sorties: chapter 5.
- The saved game's other contents and the state hash: chapter 1, The state hash and saved games.
